# ARM firmware (arm1.u) functions and data, one entry per symbol

The named functions and data of `usr/gfx/ucode/KONA/arm1.u` (IRIX 6.5.30m), one table row per debug-table symbol, grouped by what they do. [arm1_firmware.md](arm1_firmware.md) covers the structure (start-up, the input loop, the engine ports, the host-interface registers and the token handlers by group) and [arm1.md](arm1.md) the token map.

Each row was written from the code at its address in [arm1.s](arm1.s): its callers, the registers and state it touches and its effect. Labels as elsewhere: **established** (read from the code or the image), **inferred** (with the reasoning in the row). A code row cites `+0x` offsets from the read-only image base 0x0c000000, so `+0x2336c` is ARM address 0x0c02336c; data rows give the ARM address (initialised data from 0x04040260, zero-initialised data from 0x04067edc). "The state block" is the RAM the firmware addresses through r8 (base 0), which holds the GL state shadow; "the host-interface block" is the register window at 0x00200200 it addresses through r9. "Port n" is offset n in that window: 0x8c0 takes an engine handler address, 0x400 a descriptor, 0x474-0x47c operands, and reads at 0, 0x3c and 0x40 return payload words. Engine handler names are the `geif.u` slots of [ge.md](ge.md). Names that occur twice belong to two objects and have one row each.

## Start-up, exceptions and the run-time library

Reset, start-up, fault reporting, printing and the ARM C library helpers. A short lowercase name is written in backticks so it is read as code.

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `ucode` | 0x0c000000 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x0). The reset entry, the first instruction executed: it zeroes the state-block register, writes zero to 128 words spaced 0x20 bytes apart in the window at 0x00a00000, stores 0x28 at RAM address 0 and jumps through the word at 0x28, which holds the address of the start routine at +0x2c. Two other symbols with this name label runtime-library routines at +0x400e4 and +0x4015c, so a call listed as going to this name at +0x11784 really goes to +0x4015c. **Inferred:** the loop clears the instruction-cache tags, because that window is the tag area in the host-interface address map and 0x20 is the line stride. |
| `start` | 0x0c00002c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2c). The firmware entry after the reset code: it sets the saved status to user mode, stores a return address at 0xffc and returns from exception through it, so the firmware proper runs in user mode. It then sets the state-block register to 0, the host-interface register to 0x00200200 and the stack to 0xffc, calls `init`, and loops forever: load the next handler address from the host-interface input port and jump to it. The code at +0x74-0x120 has no label of its own: it is the data-abort stub, which saves all registers and the live stack, sets the aborted flag at 0x04045e98 and rejoins this loop. |
| `vector_start` | 0x0c0002e8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2e8). The exception vector block of 16 words: eight pc-load instructions followed by the eight handler addresses they load. Reset, undefined instruction, prefetch abort, the reserved vector, interrupt and fast interrupt go to stubs that save the registers and print an error; the software-interrupt vector goes to the stack-restoring code at +0x128 and the data-abort vector to the stub at +0x74. `init_arm` copies the block to RAM address 0. |
| `vector_end` | 0x0c000328 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x328). The end of the vector block, at the same address as the first word of the static token list; the copy loop in `init_arm` stops here after 0x40 bytes, and no instruction loads this name. |
| `find_ucode` | 0x0c001ab0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1ab0). Searches the table of files the host loaded into ARM memory, up to ten records of 16 bytes starting 0x40 bytes past the free-memory base, for the first record at or after the starting index (first argument) whose tag word equals the second argument; it returns that index, or -1 when none matches. Its callers look up the engine microcode, the geometry-engine slot table and the distributor program by tag. |
| `init_arm` | 0x0c001aec | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1aec). Prepares the ARM's own memory: fills the stack area from just below its frame down to 0xd00 with the 0xdeadbeef pattern, copies the 16-word vector block to RAM address 0, points six pointer variables at the context save areas, stores a 0xdeaddead guard at 0xcfc, runs the line-mode table builder, and sets a work-around flag from a board-revision field in the configuration block. |
| `init_ge_board` | 0x0c003d0c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x3d0c). Start-up step for the geometry board: it runs in order the engine-presence, scan-chain, ARM-memory, host-interface, distributor and engine set-up routines and the back-end chip set-up, then tail-calls the routine that zeroes three board registers. `init` calls it once. |
| `init_simulator_hacks` | 0x0c003eb8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x3eb8). Late start-up step that is only partly simulator-specific. It always sets read-back defaults (state-block words 0xc98-0xca0), writes screen-size values built from state-block bytes 0x40-0x45 to raster and image-processor registers unless the simulator's skip word at RAM 0x103c is set, and sets state-block bytes 0x86c-0x873 to fixed values. In the simulator it first fills bytes 0x40-0x45 with a 1280 by 1024 screen size and a third value of 1, and ends by sending the pipe-setup handler once per engine; when bit 0x100 of a host-supplied flag word is set it clears a 1280 by 1024 rectangle in 32 passes plus one 0xaaaaaaaa pass. |
| `init` | 0x0c004108 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4108). The start-up sequence: it reads the simulator bit from register 0x200000 into the data block, prints the banner and release strings, records the end of zero-initialised data as the free-memory base, runs scan-chain discovery, the geometry-board set-up, the display-state builder (not in the simulator), the raster-board and display-board set-up, stores three 16-bit values from state-block offsets 0x40-0x45 into the configuration block, then runs memory set-up, the simulator tweaks and a final pipe check. |
| `arm_initpipe` | 0x0c0042bc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x42bc). One instruction, a branch to `init`. No token-map entry and no code reaches it. **Inferred:** a restart entry for a debugger, because it would rerun the whole start-up sequence from outside. |
| `sleep` | 0x0c00437c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x437c). Busy-waits for the number of ticks in the first argument, watching the free-running counter at 0x2000b0 and handling the counter wrap, then returns. The display-generator code uses it between register writes that need settling time. |
| `simfast_bgn` | 0x0c019c1c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x19c1c). Reads the simulator flag at the start of the data area and, only when it is set, writes 1 to RAM address 0x1000, then returns; on real hardware it does nothing. **Inferred:** the word is a marker a simulator watches to run fast through the bracketed code, because the matching end routine writes -1 to the same word and both wrap the print and context-init routines. |
| `simfast_end` | 0x0c019c3c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x19c3c). Reads the simulator flag and, only when it is set, writes -1 to RAM address 0x1000, then returns; on real hardware it does nothing. **Inferred:** it closes the fast-simulation marker the begin routine opens, because it stores the opposite value in the same word. |
| `clear_icache_stats` | 0x0c019c58 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x19c58). When the simulator flag is set, writes 0 to RAM address 0x1040 and returns; otherwise returns at once. The context-init handler calls it after the one-time context set-up. **Inferred:** a word a simulator watches to reset its instruction-cache counters, because the paired reporting routine writes the neighbouring word. |
| `report_icache_stats` | 0x0c019c78 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x19c78). When the simulator flag is set, writes 0 to RAM address 0x1044 and returns; otherwise returns at once. Its single caller sits in the end-of-simulation handler. **Inferred:** a word a simulator watches to print its instruction-cache counters, because it pairs with the clearing routine that writes the neighbouring word. |
| `putchar` | 0x0c019c98 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x19c98). Sends the low byte of the first argument as one console character. In the simulator it stores the byte at RAM address 0x1004; on hardware it tail-calls `send_host_intr`, which waits for the host-interrupt pending bit to clear, puts the character in the host mailbox and raises the interrupt, so the host receives one character per interrupt. |
| `printn` | 0x0c019cb8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x19cb8). Prints an unsigned number for the formatted-print routine: divides the value (first argument) by the base (second) with `__rt_udiv` until it reaches zero, turning each remainder into a character from the digit table and holding them in a buffer, pads on the left to the width (fourth argument) with zeros when the third argument is nonzero or spaces otherwise, then sends the buffer most significant digit first through the character output routine. |
| `_printf` | 0x0c019d64 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x19d64). The firmware's formatted print to the host console. It spills the register arguments to the stack, brackets the work with the simulator markers (on hardware it also starts the message with a zero byte), scans the format string and sends plain characters one at a time, handling a leading zero flag, a field width and the conversions d, x, o, b, c and s in either case (numbers go through the number printer; d prints a minus sign for negative values, and there is no unsigned or floating conversion). It ends the message with a zero byte and returns 0. |
| `null_printf` | 0x0c01e09c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e09c). Returns at once without doing anything; the serial-number page reader calls it where it would print its CRC and read diagnostics, so those messages are discarded. |
| `InitFFTGlobal` | 0x0c037548 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37548). Start-up routine called once from global initialisation: copies the twiddle-factor table (512 words) and eight bit-reversal order tables of sizes 2 to 256 from the firmware image into engine memory at nine fixed addresses (0x73a5, 0x75a5-0x76a3), through the engine-memory writer. Used by the engines' fast Fourier transform code. |
| `__memmove` | 0x0c0400e4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x400e4). Copies words from the source to the destination address in sixteen-byte blocks with load-multiple and store-multiple, finishing the tail; its only caller is `arm_movelist_ldma`, which branches to it at the end to move a display list within ARM memory. |
| `wzero` | 0x0c04011c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4011c). Zeroes a range given as a start address and a word count, first one to three single words then four words per store-multiple; `context_init` calls it twice to clear a new context's state before its defaults are loaded. |
| `__rt_divtest` | 0x0c04015c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4015c). Returns when its argument is nonzero and otherwise spins forever, the library's divide-by-zero stop; the bytes after it are the shared shift-and-subtract loop the divide routines branch into. Called once, from `DoDGRqstHPhase`. |
| `__rt_sdiv` | 0x0c040194 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x40194). Signed division for a processor with no divide instruction: divisor in the first argument, dividend in the second, quotient returned first and remainder second, spinning on a zero divisor. Called from 106 sites, among them `PixelRestore` and the pixel planners. |
| `__rt_udiv` | 0x0c0401d8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x401d8). Unsigned division with the same argument order and results as the signed routine, sharing its shift-and-subtract loop and spinning on a zero divisor; called from four sites, among them `init_ge` and `printn()`. |
| `__rt_udiv10` | 0x0c0401f4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x401f4). Divides an unsigned value by ten with shifts and adds instead of a loop, returning quotient and remainder; used once, by the signed divide-by-ten routine that follows it. |
| `__rt_sdiv10` | 0x0c040228 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x40228). Signed divide by ten: negates a negative argument, calls the unsigned version and restores the signs of quotient and remainder. No branch or pool word in the image reaches it, so it is linked but unused. |
| `fault_cookie` | 0x040516ec | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Zero in the image; `fault()` writes 0xdead into it before printing the exception, so a debugger reading memory can tell that the firmware stopped in its fault handler. |
| `REV_REL` | 0x04067ed4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A pointer to the string "sherwood-root", the build tree name that `init()` prints in its start-up banner. |
| `REV_VER` | 0x04067ed8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A pointer to the string "0607201829" that `init()` prints beside the tree name. **Inferred:** a build time stamp (date and time). |

## Host interface, token map and input loop

Building the token map, the input loop, refused-token paths, payload helpers and the vertex-array token chains. The token map and its entry format are in [arm1.md](arm1.md); the loop is in [arm1_firmware.md](arm1_firmware.md#the-input-loop).

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `hipctl` | 0x0c000328 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x328). The static list of 512 token-map entries followed by an all-ones terminator at +0xff0: a word with bit 0 set is a whole entry, an even word takes the next word shifted left by 4. `init_hip` walks it to build the hardware token map, and the matrix-mode code copies fifteen of its entries back into that map at run time. |
| `hipwc` | 0x0c000ff4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xff4). Table of 512 words, one per token, giving how many payload words `arm_bgnend` must read and discard when that token arrives inside a begin/end pair; 89 entries are zero (including the two double-precision matrix tokens, which have a special case) and the largest count is 36. |
| `arm_badcmd` | 0x0c0017f4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x17f4). Handler for tokens the firmware does not implement: it prints the current and previous token numbers read from host-interface registers 0x200078 and 0x20007c, then reads 100 payload words printing each, then reads the payload port forever without returning. 54 of the 306 ARM-run tokens point at it. |
| `arm_bgnend` | 0x0c0018e4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x18e4). Handler the host interface calls for a token refused between a begin and an end: it records the GL invalid-operation error, reads and discards the token's payload words using a per-token count, and finishes with the last-word port read. It then writes register 0x200068: 1 for a token of kind 0 or kind 1, 0 for the end token. A token with no count that is not one of the two double-precision matrix tokens prints an error and spins reading the port. |
| `arm_divert` | 0x0c001a34 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a34). Handler stored at host-interface register 0x200054 for the diverted-token path: it prints the current and previous token numbers and a message that the divert is not handled, then reads the payload port forever, printing each word. It never returns. **Inferred:** the divert path is never used, because the firmware only ever writes 0 or 1 to the control register that enables it. |
| `init_hip` | 0x0c001c60 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1c60). Builds the token map: with the ARM-access bit set in register 0x200050, it expands the static list into 512 words stored both in the RAM copy and at the hardware map at 0x00400000, fills unlisted tokens with the bad-command handler, installs the begin/end and divert handlers at registers 0x20006c and 0x200054, then copies 512 words of the geometry-engine slot file into the slot table and packs the first 128 as halfword pairs at state-block offset 0x48. |
| `init_hip_tbus` | 0x0c003760 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x3760). Writes zero to three board registers, 0x441, then 0x453, then 0x463, through the engine register-write path, the last as a tail call. It is the final step of `init_ge_board`. |
| `arm_pipe_sync` | 0x0c0042c0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x42c0). Handler for token 0x1ea: reads one payload word and passes it to `check_pipe_sync` as that routine's verbosity argument. If the result is not 0 it branches back to the compare without calling again, so the ARM spins forever on the first failed engine handshake. |
| `arm_gerollcall` | 0x0c0042e0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x42e0). Handler for token 0x1e5: reads one payload word, prints that it is issuing a roll call to every engine, sends each engine that word as an operand with the number-printing handler, runs `check_pipe_sync` and finishes with the last-word payload read. **Inferred:** a diagnostic in which each engine reports the word, from the handler's name. |
| `SendDGRply` | 0x0c0064f8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x64f8). Sends a reply to the host through the read-back path: checks the reply type is below 0x20 (else prints an error and stops), opens the read-back stream, writes the word count plus one, the type, then each payload word to raster-bus register 0x476, and finally the sum of the type and the words as a checksum, then closes the stream. Called by every get-style display request. |
| `eeprom_state` | 0x0c019f7c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x19f7c). Issues one command byte (first argument) to the flash part behind the EEPROM window at 0x800000 using the three-cycle unlock handshake: writes 0xaa at window offset 0x15554, 0x55 at 0xaaa8, then the command at 0x15554, with a short two-iteration spin between writes. Callers pass 0xa0 for a program, 0x90 to enter ID mode and 0xf0 to leave it. |
| `eeprom_write` | 0x0c019fdc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x19fdc). Programs flash through the EEPROM window at 0x800000: takes a device offset (first argument) and byte count (second) and writes the bytes of the 128 KB scratch buffer in pages of at most 0x100, each preceded by the program command from `eeprom_state`. After each page it re-reads the first window word until two consecutive reads agree (up to 25000 tries) and abandons the rest of the write if they never do. Bytes go to window offset four times the device offset plus four per byte written. |
| `eeprom_read` | 0x0c01a07c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a07c). Copies from the flash window at 0x800000 into the 128 KB scratch buffer: takes a byte offset (first argument) and a byte count (second) and moves count divided by four 32-bit words, starting at that offset in the window; a zero count returns at once. Its addressing differs from the write routine, which spaces bytes four apart in the window. |
| `eeprom_id` | 0x0c01a0a8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a0a8). Reads the flash part's identification word: sends the ID-mode command through `eeprom_state`, sleeps one million time units, reads the first word of the window at 0x800000, sends the reset command to leave ID mode, sleeps again and returns the word read. |
| `arm_eeprom_op` | 0x0c01a0ec | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a0ec). Handler for host token 0x1ec (EEPROM operation). It reads the first payload word and dispatches on it. Operation one loads a flash image from the host; two reads a block back to the host a word at a time through engine-bus register 0x476, between a start and a finish of the read-data transfer; three records transfer parameters; four runs a mailbox check; five returns the flash ID word; six zeroes the start of the scratch buffer and writes four bytes of it to the flash at 0x20000, which wipes the stored display-combination header. Operations one, two, four and five return their result the same way. |
| `eeprom_load` | 0x0c01a230 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a230). Receives a flash image from the host and programs it: reads an operation code and, for code 0, a flash offset, then a word count; for code 1 it first writes the magic word 0xfacefeed and the count as a header. It rejects counts of 0x7ffc words or more, reads count-1 payload words from the host-interface block into the scratch buffer plus a final word from the last-word port, then calls `eeprom_write` to the chosen target (the supplied offset, 0x20000 for code 1, or 0x7ff00 for code 2) and clears the buffer. Returns 1 on success and 0 on a bad code or count. |
| `eeprom_setup` | 0x0c01a31c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a31c). Handler body for EEPROM operation 3: copies two payload words and the final payload word into the state block at offsets 0xc98-0xca0 and zeroes offset 0xca4, recording the parameters of a pending transfer; nothing in this function itself touches the flash. |
| `eeprom_check` | 0x0c01a340 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a340). Handler body for EEPROM operation four, which despite its name never touches the flash: it clears the geometry-engine mailbox-valid flag, sends the free-running timer value to the engines through engine-bus registers 0x430 and 0x434, then polls until the engines set the valid flag (about 268 million tries) and compares the mailbox data word with the value sent. It reports 1 for a match, otherwise 0, to the host through engine-bus register 0x476 as the read-data transfer. |
| `arm_f3_war` | 0x0c022380 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22380). Token 0x1dc: stores the last payload word as the fourth word of the kernel-communication block that the kernel-communication writer sends. The input-DMA routine tests that same word to choose how it waits between pieces, so it is a wait-mode flag; the name suggests a workaround for a chip erratum, but the code only keeps this one word. |
| `arm_kernel_private` | 0x0c022394 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22394). Token 0x1fe: returns at once without reading a payload word or touching any register; the kernel-private command is ignored by the ARM. |
| `arm_dmapool_setup` | 0x0c022398 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22398). Token 0x1ed: after the privilege check it stores three words in the state block: the first payload word at offset 0xcdc, the same word with its low 12 bits cleared at 0xce0, and the last payload word at 0xce4. **Inferred:** they describe a pool of DMA pages, from the command name; the code only stores them. |
| `start_kcomm` | 0x0c022800 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22800). Programs the read-back registers to send a kernel-communication record to the host (the first argument is written last to register 0x476). When the flag word at the start of initialised data is nonzero it uses the state-block read-back address; when zero it uses the three-word block of the kernel-communication record, first writing the marker 0xdad00dad to register 0x476 and then repeating the same register set-up before the final write. |
| `finish_kcomm` | 0x0c022980 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22980). Ends a kernel-communication transfer: it clears register 0x477 and, when the flag word at the start of initialised data is nonzero, restores the read-back registers from the state block and starts and stops the transfer through 0x476 and 0x477; when the flag is zero it writes zero to register 0x400 instead. Both paths finish by clearing 0x463, setting 0x464 to 0x400 and clearing 0x440. |
| `arm_ge_intr` | 0x0c022cfc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22cfc). Token 0x1dd: after the privilege check it sends the first payload word to the host as a kernel-communication record, the way the engines report an event, by starting the transfer and then tail-calling the finish routine. |
| `send_host_intr` | 0x0c022d50 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22d50). Raises a host interrupt carrying one word (the first argument): it waits until the interrupt-request bit at 0x200094 clears, writes the word to the host mailbox at 0x200090 and sets the request bit. |
| `arm_do_idma` | 0x0c022df4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22df4). Token 0x1eb: runs an input DMA from host memory into the pipe. After the privilege check it programs the input-DMA registers (0x200010-0x20001c and control 0x200020) with page number, offset and byte count, then feeds the data through the pass-through counter with the input source bit (0x8000000), in pieces of at most 0x3fffff8 bytes, polling the input-DMA status at 0x200024 between pieces. A repeat count with a stride splits the transfer into several rows. At the end it sets the last-word flag, sends the host an interrupt word if the last payload word is nonzero, writes zero to register 0x3ff1 when the context mode is 3, clears the mode word and calls a pending completion routine. |
| `arm_data` | 0x0c026590 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x26590). Token handler 0x000: a single branch to the bad-command routine, which prints the token numbers, reads and prints 100 payload words and then spins; the firmware implements no real handler for this token. It is a placeholder for the data slot at the bottom of one half of the host FIFO window. |
| `arm_wg_flush_0` | 0x0c0265fc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x265fc). Token handler 0x0ff: a single branch to the bad-command routine, which prints the token numbers, reads and prints 100 payload words and then spins; the firmware implements no real handler for this token. It is a placeholder for the write-gather flush token at the top of the first half of the host FIFO window; the code does not show whether the host interface ever delivers it. |
| `arm_data_1` | 0x0c026600 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x26600). Token handler 0x100: a single branch to the bad-command routine, which prints the token numbers, reads and prints 100 payload words and then spins; the firmware implements no real handler for this token. It is a placeholder for the data slot at the bottom of one half of the host FIFO window. |
| `arm_gethistogramparameterfv` | 0x0c026608 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x26608). Stub for the float form of the histogram parameter query: one branch to the bad-command routine at 0x0c0017f4, which prints the token numbers, reads 100 payload words and spins. No entry of the token map points at this address, so nothing reaches it; the integer form of the query is implemented elsewhere. |
| `arm_getminmaxparameterfv` | 0x0c02660c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2660c). Stub for the float form of the minmax parameter query: one branch to the bad-command routine at 0x0c0017f4, which prints the token numbers, reads 100 payload words and spins. No entry of the token map points at this address, so nothing reaches it; the integer form of the query is implemented elsewhere. |
| `arm_pixeltexgen` | 0x0c026610 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x26610). Token handler for HP_PIXELTEXGEN (token 0x11a): one branch to the bad-command routine at 0x0c0017f4, which prints the token numbers, reads 100 payload words and spins. The firmware implements nothing for this entry point and the host driver is not expected to send it. |
| `arm_texsubimage1d` | 0x0c026614 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x26614). Token handler for HP_TEXSUBIMAGE1D (token 0x11c): one branch to the bad-command routine at 0x0c0017f4, which prints the token numbers, reads 100 payload words and spins. The firmware implements nothing for this entry point and the host driver is not expected to send it. |
| `arm_texsubimage2d` | 0x0c026618 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x26618). Token handler for HP_TEXSUBIMAGE2D (token 0x11d): one branch to the bad-command routine at 0x0c0017f4, which prints the token numbers, reads 100 payload words and spins. The firmware implements nothing for this entry point and the host driver is not expected to send it. |
| `arm_texsubimage3d` | 0x0c02661c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2661c). Token handler for HP_TEXSUBIMAGE3D (token 0x11e): one branch to the bad-command routine at 0x0c0017f4, which prints the token numbers, reads 100 payload words and spins. The firmware implements nothing for this entry point and the host driver is not expected to send it. |

The firmware implements nothing for this entry point and the host driver is not expected to send it. |

The firmware implements nothing for this entry point and the host driver is not expected to send it. |

The firmware implements nothing for this entry point and the host driver is not expected to send it. |

The firmware implements nothing for this entry point and the host driver is not expected to send it. |

The firmware implements nothing for this entry point and the host driver is not expected to send it. |

The firmware implements nothing for this entry point and the host driver is not expected to send it. |

The firmware implements nothing for this entry point and the host driver is not expected to send it. |

The firmware implements nothing for this entry point and the host driver is not expected to send it. |

Token handler for HP_WG_FLUSH_1 (token 0x1ff): one branch to the bad-command routine at 0x0c0017f4, which prints the token numbers, reads 100 payload words and spins. The code only shows the branch; the sibling flush token 0xff has the same single branch. **Inferred:** the host interface drops these top-of-window stores before token dispatch, because the X module stores to this token after every cursor load and a pipe that dispatched it would hang. This code cannot confirm the drop. |

| `vertex_table` | 0x0405102c | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 60 words indexed by vertex-array type and size giving the vertex token for each array format, zero where none exists; `varray_build_command` reads it to build the vertex-array token-map chain. |
| `normal_table` | 0x0405111c | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 60 words giving the normal token for each array format (for example 0x76), zero where none exists; read by `varray_build_command`. |
| `color_table` | 0x0405120c | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 60 words giving the colour token for each array format (such as 0x61), zero where none exists; read by `varray_build_command`. |
| `index_table` | 0x040512fc | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 60 words giving the colour-index token for each array format, zero where none exists; read by `varray_build_command`. |
| `tex_coord_table` | 0x040513ec | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 60 words giving the texture-coordinate token for each array format, zero where none exists; read by `varray_build_command`. |
| `wordCountTable` | 0x040514dc | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 60 payload word counts per array format that `varray_build_command` puts into bits 6-8 of each vertex-array token-map entry. |
| `lastWrdTable` | 0x040515cc | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 60 values (0-3) per array format that `varray_build_command` puts into bits 27-28 of each vertex-array token-map entry. |
| `byteCmpTable` | 0x040516bc | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Twelve values (0-3) per component type that `varray_build_command` puts into bits 29-30 of a vertex-array token-map entry, the component-type field. |
| `icu_map` | 0x04068e54 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). The firmware's 512-word copy of the token map that `init_hip` builds; the vertex-array builder and `arm_pack` read entries from it to build vertex-array and packed entries. |

## Engine and distributor set-up

Loading the distributor program and tables, the geometry-engine microcode and the bef chip, and the per-engine set-up commands ([arm1_firmware.md](arm1_firmware.md#how-a-handler-reaches-the-engines), [ged.md](ged.md)).

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `init_ged_ucode` | 0x0c001e20 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e20). Loads the distributor: it finds the program file by tag 0x4591 and writes its 1024 words through descriptor 0x48200020, replacing every word that names an engine slot with that slot's handler address, then writes a 16-word table through descriptor 0x42200020 and a 512-word table through 0x42600020, after resetting each write pointer. |
| `init_ged_regs` | 0x0c0020ec | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x20ec). Writes power-up values into the distributor through descriptor-and-data store pairs: zeros and a few fixed constants, ten zero words, sixteen 0xfefefefe words, a 128-word table whose first 32 entries are zero and the rest 0xabcdabcd, the transfer mask 0x1000ef0f, and last the handler address of the pixel-colour entry into descriptor 0x58c00020. `init_ged` calls it once the handler table is written. |
| `init_ged` | 0x0c0024e8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24e8). Sets up the distributor at start-up: writes a flag byte of the state block through descriptor 0x44000020 (1 when bit 0x20 of a host-supplied flag word is clear and at most four engines are present, else 0), zero through 0x44200020 and the engine occupancy mask five times through 0x44400020; writes engine handler addresses into 43 of the 64 handler-table indexes (indexes 0x13-0x15 are not among them); calls `init_ged_regs`; then alternates small numbers between two neighbouring descriptors starting at 0x40000020 and fills a table through 0x50600020, and tail-calls the program loader. It prints a warning that the pre-production distributor is no longer supported when a revision nibble in the configuration block is zero. |
| `check_pipe_sync` | 0x0c002f7c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2f7c). Synchronises the ARM with the engines: it writes 1 to the mailbox-valid register at 0x20009c, reads the real-time counter at 0x2000b0, sends that value to board registers 0x430 then 0x434 through the engine register-write path, spins a bounded time until the valid register reads non-zero, then compares the mailbox word at 0x2000a0 with the value sent. It returns one of four codes, 0 for valid-and-correct up to 3 for invalid-and-incorrect, printing the outcome (the all-good case only when the first argument is zero); it returns 0 at once in the simulator or when a bookkeeping word in the data block is set. |
| `init_ge_config` | 0x0c003164 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x3164). Works out which geometry engines are present: it clears an 8-entry list in the configuration block, then forms a mask from the engine count (or, in the simulator, from fields at RAM 0x1030-0x1034), ANDs it with the occupancy mask the host loader left in memory, and stores the count, the mask and the list of engine numbers. A mask that comes out empty prints a message and keeps the unmasked value. |
| `ungray` | 0x0c0032d4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x32d4). Returns entry n of the 16-word table at 0x04040284; the table is the inverse of the 4-bit Gray code, so the geometry-board position code is decoded to a plain number. Only `init_ge` calls this routine; a second routine of the same name at +0x1a3e4 serves the scan-chain code. |
| `init_ge` | 0x0c0032e4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x32e4). Brings up the geometry engines: it sets the grant bit in register 0x200004 when any of engines 4-7 is present, writes the engine masks through descriptors 0x44000020-0x44400020, loads every engine-microcode file found by tag 0x4593 word by word through the 0x46600020 data descriptor after setting the 0x46a00020 address descriptor, writes board-dependent constants chosen from the decoded board position, runs `mgeb_GlobalInitialize` on all engines, sends each engine its own numbering command and checks the pipe once per engine. It ends by initialising the global fog, anti-aliased-line, multisample-point and pixel tables, the last as a tail call. |
| `init_bef` | 0x0c00375c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x375c). One instruction, a branch to the routine that sets up the shadow of the back-end chip's registers; it is called once from `init_ge_board`. |
| `compute_gedxfermask` | 0x0c012a98 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12a98). Forces on, in the two distributor transfer-mask words in the state block at offsets 0xc90 and 0xc94, the bits the diagnostic engine load needs: bits 0-3 plus 8-11 plus 13-15 of the first word, and bits 8-11 plus 13-23 plus 28 of the second, so that every vertex attribute is transferred. Called only by the diagnostic-load handler, which jumps to it at its end. |
| `set_ged_xfer_mask` | 0x0c012ba8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12ba8). Sends the distributor its attribute transfer mask: it writes descriptor 0x55c00020 to the descriptor port at offset 0x400 and the first argument (the mask) to the handler-address port at 0x8c0, then returns. About forty routines call it after they recompute the mask in the state block. |
| `XferMask` | 0x0c013098 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x13098). Rebuilds the distributor's vertex-attribute transfer mask and stores it at state-block offset 0xc90, then jumps to the routine that sends it. The base is 0xf, or 0xff when bit 0 of the flag byte at 0xcf0 is set. It adds 0xf00 (0x1e00 when the colour-index width at 0x1ce is non-zero) unless lighting is on without colour material and the lighting pointers are set. It adds 0x1000e000 when the render-mode word at 0x862 is 0x1c01 or a flag byte at 0x760 or 0x771-0x772 is set. It adds 0xf0000 in render mode 0x1c01 or when the word at 0x890 is non-zero. About twenty enable, disable and pop handlers call it. |
| `XferMaskRpos` | 0x0c01315c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1315c). Sets the transfer-mask word at state-block offset 0xc94 used for raster-position commands: 0x10ffe000 when bit 0 of the flag byte at 0xcf0 is clear and 0x1fffe000 when it is set, plus 0xf00 when the colour-index width at 0x1ce is zero and 0x1e00 otherwise. It only stores the word and sends nothing. Called once, by the buffer-configuration handler. |
| `tbus_regwrite` | 0x0c015298 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15298). Writes one raster-board register through the engines: the first argument (register number) goes to port 0x474, the second (value) to port 0x840, and the register-write engine handler address (low half of the word at state-block offset 0x11c) to port 0x8c0. Roughly 300 call sites use it. |
| `tbus_regwritenoincr` | 0x0c0152b4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x152b4). Like the register writer, but for a register that must not auto-increment: it writes the first argument (register number) to port 0x470 instead of 0x474, the second to port 0x840, and a different engine handler address to port 0x8c0. Used by pixel read-back and by diagnostic checks. |
| `init_geBefAddrs` | 0x0c01fc38 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1fc38). Broadcasts the list of BEF register addresses to all geometry engines: points the broadcast select (port 0x438) at the state block, sends the header word 0x1233 and the 89 addresses of the BEF address table through the data port 0x840, then triggers handler slot 131 through port 0x8c0. |
| `init_befshadow` | 0x0c01fc80 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1fc80). Loads initial BEF shadow values into the single selected engine: points the single-engine select (port 0x430) at the state block, streams the 89 words of the BEF initial-value table through the data port 0x840, then triggers handler slot 187 through port 0x8c0. |
| `arm_pipe_setup` | 0x0c023298 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23298). Token 0x1f1: after the privilege check stores the first two payload words and the last-payload word in a three-word block, writes descriptor 0x44200020 with 1 (distributor step register on), then for each engine (the count is in the board configuration block) sends the three words with a flag (0 when the initialised-data flag word is zero, else 1) to port 0x474 and data port 0x840, and the engine handler in slot 374. It finally writes descriptor 0x44200020 with 0 again. |
| `SetupGE_ColorStream` | 0x0c02cce0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2cce0). Engine stage of paths 0 (colour stream) and, through a shared slot, other colour paths. If the transfer-active word at pixel state +0xc is nonzero it builds the module chain with the colour path loader; otherwise, if bit 0x80 of the operation word is set, it writes five words from pixel state +0x35c to engine control memory address 0x161. It ends by tail-calling the per-rectangle engine set-up. |
| `SetupGE_ColorLine` | 0x0c02cd7c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2cd7c). Engine stage of path 2 (colour, line at a time, convolution). When the convolution bits are set it fixes the kernel direction and loads kernel 4 into the engines, adds a dispatch module, then sends five single-engine writes of 1 through descriptor 0x44000020 (distributor register 0x222) followed by five more of the engine-count-dependent word. If transfer is active it builds two module chains with the colour path loader (second pass, with the first pass's output sizes), advancing the distributor pointer between; otherwise it reloads the volatile convolution state. Ends with the per-rectangle set-up. |
| `SetupGE_Convolution` | 0x0c02cee8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2cee8). Engine stage for the convolution-filter load and read paths. Takes bit 0x100 of the operation word (pixel state +8) to choose between the convolution load-path builder and the convolution read-path builder, then tail-calls the per-rectangle engine set-up. |
| `SetupGE_PolygonStipple` | 0x0c02cf1c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2cf1c). Engine stage of the polygon-stipple load. Builds the stipple module chain with the stipple path loader, writes the high half of the word at state block +0xc4 (an engine handler address) to port 0x4f8, and returns without a per-rectangle set-up. |
| `SetupGE_LuminanceStream` | 0x0c02cf48 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2cf48). Engine stage of path 1 (luminance stream). Builds the module chain with the luminance path loader (argument 0, the operation word from pixel state +8) and tail-calls the per-rectangle engine set-up. |
| `SetupGE_FFT` | 0x0c02cf70 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2cf70). Engine stage of path 9 (FFT). Builds on the stack a list of engine handler addresses: an optional first handler when the per-context flag +0x560 is set, then the one for the transform width (any power of two from two to 256) with a control-memory constant, otherwise printing an unsupported-width message; then a handler chosen by +0x55c and two stream handlers chosen by packet counts. It sends the per-engine set-up (handler slot 0x1a0 with the half width) to each engine, writes the list to control memory address 0x129 and sets the output format word. |
| `SetupGE_LuminanceLine` | 0x0c02d2e0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2d2e0). Engine stage of path 3 (luminance, line at a time). The same sequence as the colour-line stage with the luminance path loader: kernel direction and kernel 1 load when convolution is set, a dispatch module, five single-engine writes through descriptor 0x44000020 then five of the full mask, two chain builds (the second with a pass-two adjustment of the distributor pointer) or a reload of the volatile convolution state, then the per-rectangle set-up. |
| `SetupGE_StencilStream` | 0x0c02d444 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2d444). Engine stage of path 8 (stencil). When bit 0x40 of the operation word is set and the per-context mode word at +0x650 equals 3, it sends the words 1 and 0x0167140f through descriptor 0x584fc800 and then a closing write. It then builds the module chain with the stencil path loader and tail-calls the per-rectangle set-up. |
| `SetupGE_DepthStream` | 0x0c02d4b0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2d4b0). Engine stage of path 7 (depth). Builds the module chain with the depth path loader. If the input format record's first word is not 6 it then tail-calls the per-rectangle set-up; if it is 6 (packed depth) it runs the per-rectangle set-up twice, first with the input format pointer set to the format-table entry at +0x2280 and then with the entry at +0x22c0. |
| `SetupGE_IndexStream` | 0x0c02d504 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2d504). Engine stage of path 6 (colour index). Builds the module chain with the index path loader, then writes the compare-format table entry for the input format to port 0x478, two zero words to port 0x840 and the low half of the word at state block +0xbc (an engine handler address) to port 0x8c0. It does not call the per-rectangle set-up. |
| `SetupGE_Bitmap` | 0x0c02d558 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2d558). Engine stage of the bitmap draw. Builds the module chain with the bitmap path loader (operation word from pixel state +8) and tail-calls the per-rectangle engine set-up. Called by `do_bitmap`. |
| `SetupGE_ShortCircuit` | 0x0c02d57c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2d57c). Engine stage of paths 4 and 5 (colour and luminance short circuit). If the transfer-active word is nonzero it builds the chain with the short-circuit path loader; otherwise, when bit 0x80 of the operation word is set, it writes five words from pixel state +0x35c to engine control memory address 0x161. It ends with the per-rectangle engine set-up. |
| `SetupGE_ColorTable` | 0x0c02d5d4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2d5d4). Engine stage of the colour-table load. Builds the module chain with the colour-table path loader, passing the first argument through, then writes the compare-format table entry for the input format to port 0x478 and the engine handler address stored at +0x200 of the handler table to port 0x8c0. Called by `arm_colortable`; it does not call the per-rectangle set-up. |
| `befsh_addrs` | 0x04067c0c | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 89 register addresses of the bef chip (starting at 0x441) that `init_geBefAddrs` broadcasts to every engine as one command; `arm_slop` also reads it. |
| `befsh_init` | 0x04067d70 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 89 initial values for the bef chip shadow (mostly zero, some 0x3fff8000), matching the address list entry by entry; `init_befshadow` sends them to one engine as one command. |
| `gepc` | 0x04068654 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 512 words, the engine handler address table copied from `geif.u` by `init_hip`; every engine send loads its handler address from here or from the packed copy in the state block. |

## Boundary scan, boards and serial numbers

The scan chain, board and chip identification, the board-configuration block and the serial-number (NIC) readers.

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `ungray` | 0x0c01a3e4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a3e4). Returns entry n of a sixteen-word table at 0x04045ad8, where n is the first argument. The table is the four-bit reflected Gray code in order of the binary index, so it decodes a Gray-coded position to binary; its only caller extracts a four-bit field of the geometry board ID and passes it in. |
| `jt_select_chain` | 0x0c01a3f4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a3f4). Selects one of the board scan chains: looks up the chain's select code in the chain table, shifts it left by five and writes it to the scan controller's control register at 0x2000c8, records the chain number in the current-chain word, and returns 0. |
| `jt_soft_reset` | 0x0c01a420 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a420). Resets the scan controller's test-access port on the current chain: writes the chain select code with bit 0 set to the control register, writes 0x1f to the test-mode-select register and 0 to the data register, then polls the status register at 0x2000cc until bit 0 clears, up to 1000 times. On timeout it prints "JTAG stall timeout, exceeded 1000 iterations!" and returns 1; otherwise returns 0. |
| `jt_send_inst_or_data` | 0x0c01a4c8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a4c8). Shifts a bit string through the current scan chain. Arguments are the bit count, an input buffer, an output buffer and a selector (1 for the instruction register, 0 for the data register). It feeds 32 bits at a time to the controller's data register at 0x2000c0, with the test-mode-select register set for the register chosen and a different value for the last chunk, waits each time for the status bit at 0x2000cc to clear (1000-poll limit, then it prints a stall message and returns 1) and stores what comes back into the output buffer. A null input shifts zeros. Returns 0 on success. |
| `insert_bits` | 0x0c01a6e4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a6e4). Copies a bit field between two bit buffers one bit at a time: the source buffer is the second argument starting at bit 0, the destination is the first argument starting at the bit given in the third argument, and the fourth argument is the number of bits; each destination bit is cleared and then set from the source. The scan routines use it to stage register values into the shift buffer. |
| `extract_bits` | 0x0c01a738 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a738). The mirror of the insert routine: copies a number of bits (fourth argument) out of a source buffer (second argument) starting at the bit in the third argument into the destination (first argument) starting at bit 0, one bit at a time. The scan register reader uses it to pull one register's bits out of the captured vector. |
| `jt_reg_read` | 0x0c01a78c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a78c). Returns the current value of one scan-chain register. It finds the entry for the register number and chip code in the 425-entry register table, adds the selected chip's bit offset in the shift vector to the entry's start bit, and extracts the bits up to the entry's end bit from the shift buffer into a word that is returned. In the simulator it instead stores its four arguments in RAM at 0x1008, sets operation code 2 at 0x1028 and returns the word the simulator leaves at 0x102c. |
| `jt_reg_write` | 0x0c01a874 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a874). Stages a value for one scan-chain register: finds the entry for the register number and chip code in the register table, stores the value (stack argument) in the entry's shadow word, and inserts it into the shift buffer at the register's bit position; nothing is shifted out until the flush routine runs. Returns 0, or in the simulator hands its arguments to RAM at 0x1008 with operation code 3 at 0x1028. |
| `jt_reg_init` | 0x0c01a954 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a954). Replays shadowed register values: for every entry in the scan register table whose chip code equals the first argument and whose third field equals the second argument, it calls the register writer with the value held in the entry's shadow word. Does nothing in the simulator. Callers pass chip 0x13 with the value 0x60 before changing a single field of that chip. |
| `jt_select_chip` | 0x0c01a9d4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1a9d4). Aims the scan chain at one chip: given a chain, chip code, instance and instruction index, it walks the chain's chips from last to first and builds the instruction vector, inserting the indexed instruction for the matching chip and a bypass instruction (0xff) for every other chip, records each chip's bit offset in the data vector, then shifts the instruction vector into the chain. Returns 0, and does nothing in the simulator. |
| `jt_flush` | 0x0c01ab00 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1ab00). Shifts the staged register values out: unless simulating, it sends the total data-register length of the current chain from the shift buffer, using the shift buffer as both input and output, through the bit-shifting routine and returns what that returns. |
| `board_id` | 0x0c01ab38 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1ab38). Reads the 32-bit identification word of the board on one scan chain (first argument): fills the shift buffer with 0xa8a8a8a8, selects the chain, shifts 0x140 instruction bits, then shifts 0x20 data bits into a local word and returns it. An all-ones result means no answer; the callers test for that. |
| `ge_board_id` | 0x0c01ab98 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1ab98). Reads the geometry-board identification word by calling the board-ID reader for scan chain 5. |
| `rm_board_id` | 0x0c01aba0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1aba0). Reads the identification word of raster board n (first argument) by calling the board-ID reader for scan chain n+1. |
| `chip_id` | 0x0c01aba8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1aba8). Reads one chip's identity register over the scan chain: arguments are a chain base, a board number (added to the base), a chip code, an instance and a register number. It aims the chain at the chip with instruction index 5, shifts the chain's data register into the shift buffer, and returns the chosen register's bits from it. |
| `jt_vector_to_int` | 0x0c01ac18 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1ac18). Packs the first four words of a vector (second argument), each holding a byte, into one 32-bit word with the first as the low byte, stores it through the pointer in the first argument and returns it. Nothing in the image calls it. |
| `init_kona_jtag_chain` | 0x0c01ac3c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1ac3c). Detects the board set and picks the scan-chain layout. It reads the geometry board ID (a missing answer prints a message and assumes the base board set), reads each raster board's ID and classifies the boards by their type field, prints an error when Reality and InfiniteReality raster boards or InfiniteReality4 and older boards are mixed, and stores a configuration code in the first word of the board-information block. It then copies one of three seven-chain layout tables into the scan-chain table and leaves the display-generator chain descriptor in a holding word for the display-generator probe to install. |
| `init_jtag` | 0x0c01aea4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1aea4). Scan-chain bring-up: resets the test-access port on chain 5, then chain 1, runs the layout detection, then resets chains 0-6 in turn. Unless simulating, it then fills the board-information block, copies the geometry-engine and raster-board counts to the working copies and forces either count to 1 if it is 0, printing "init_jtag: whacking numrms to %d" or "init_jtag: whacking numges to %d". |
| `init_dg_jtag` | 0x0c01afcc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1afcc). Loads the simulator flag from the start of the data area and returns it; it touches no scan chain, so the display-generator scan step is a stub in this image. |
| `config_imp8_jtag` | 0x0c01afd8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1afd8). Programs one image-processor chip (type 0x34) over the scan chain for a raster board and chip number (first and second arguments, the raster board's code coming from the code table): selects the chip with instruction index 2, stages 58 register writes (fixed values plus that board code in one register), and flushes. |
| `config_imp9_jtag` | 0x0c01b824 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1b824). The shorter variant used for configuration code 2: selects the image-processor chip (type 0x34) as the other variant does, writes only 8 registers (the board code from the code table plus fixed values), and ends in a flush. |
| `config_imp8d_jtag` | 0x0c01b974 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1b974). The variant for the 0x39-type image-processor chip used when the configuration code is 6: selects chip 0x39 with instruction index 2, writes 24 of its configuration registers (the board code from the code table plus fixed values) and ends in a flush. |
| `init_rm_jtag` | 0x0c01bcfc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1bcfc). Configures the raster boards over the scan chains. If the texture-memory option bit is clear it prints that a configuration without texture memory is unsupported. It picks the per-image-processor routine from the configuration code (the shortest variant for code 2, the 0x39-chip variant for code 6, the longest otherwise) and stores it for reuse, then for each raster board selects four support chips (codes 0x30-0x33, or 0x35-0x38 for code 6), writes their registers and flushes, and finally calls the chosen routine for every image-processor chip on the board (10 or 20 per board). |
| `reset_imps` | 0x0c01cba8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1cba8). Pulses and reconfigures the image-processor chips. Unless simulating, it asks the engines to flush the raster boards (handler slot 399 through port 0x4f4), clears the mailbox flag, writes 1 to engine-bus register 0x434 and waits for the engine acknowledge (else it prints "reset_imps() NO acknowledge via HA_GE_MBOX_VALID, continuing..."), then for each board resets its chain and shifts instruction 7 and then 0xf into every image-processor chip with a short sleep between, sleeps a second, re-runs the stored configuration routine for every board and chip, and finally sends handler slot 188 through port 0x4f0 so the engines reload from the BEF. |
| `init_ge_jtag` | 0x0c01cd70 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1cd70). Configures the BEF chip (type 0x13) on the geometry chain. Unless simulating, it reads the board ID, converts its engine-position field with the Gray-code table, selects the chip, writes 20 of its registers (fixed values, a value of 0x400 or 0x180 depending on that position, board option bits from the information block and the stored word at offset 0x64) and flushes, then reselects the chip with instruction index 15. |
| `bef_tbus_redirect` | 0x0c01d0c8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1d0c8). Called once from the slop handler: selects the BEF chip with instruction 2, restores its registers from the shadow, writes its register 0x229 with the argument, flushes, reads back register 0x230, restores the shadow again, writes the read value back, flushes, and stores the argument in the redirect word at 0x04045be4. |
| `bef_set_ge` | 0x0c01d178 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1d178). Prints "bef_set_ge() setting current_ge to %d" and writes the argument into the BEF chip's register 0x230 over the scan chain (chip selected with instruction 2, shadow values restored first), then flushes. **Inferred:** register 0x230 holds the current engine number, from the message text and because the chip set-up routine writes a stored engine value there. No code in the image calls it. |
| `bef_set_numges` | 0x0c01d200 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1d200). Sets the BEF chip's input width: if the argument has any of bits 4-8 set it selects sixteen-bit input (printing "setting up bef for 16 bit input") and writes 1, otherwise thirty-two-bit input and writes 0, to the chip's register 0x22c over the scan chain, then flushes. No code in the image calls it. |
| `dg_board_id` | 0x0c01d2d0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1d2d0). Probes the display-generator board on scan chain 0 and returns its 32-bit identification, or -1 if it still does not answer. It fills the shift buffer with ones, shifts 0xc8 instruction bits, 0x16 data bits, then 0x82 instruction bits led by the word 0xffffa8a8, and 0x20 data bits for the answer; if bit 0 of the answer is clear it installs the held display-generator chain descriptor as chain 0 and returns the word, and if it is set it repeats once with a 0x40-bit instruction, returning the second answer unless that one is also set. |
| `extract_nic_ser_nums` | 0x0c01d464 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1d464). Reads the serial-number chips (one-wire devices) on one board. Arguments are a name string, a scan-chain number and an output record. It sets up the bit-bang for the chain, scans for devices with `nic_next`, retries up to ten times reading the first and second memory page of each through `nic_read_redirected_page`, and stores each device's identifier bytes and eight page words in the record, flagging the chips found; it prints "Not NIC queriable from %s." or "Exhausted all %s NIC probe attempts" on failure. |
| `init_config` | 0x0c01d8a0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1d8a0). Fills the board-information block by reading board and chip identification over the scan chains: the geometry board ID gives the engine count (through a four-byte table) and option bits, then for each raster board it reads the board ID and the identity registers of its support chips and image-processor chips, checks the raster-board count against the chips seen (printing "RM board mismatch detected"), reads the display-generator ID ("No RM board detected" and "DG not present" are the failure messages) and finally runs the serial-number reader for each board and module it found. |
| `nic_wait_done` | 0x0c01e0a0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e0a0). Waits for a bit-bang step to finish: repeatedly calls the status-read callback (first word of the context) with the context's handle word until bit 1 of the result is set. |
| `nic_presence` | 0x0c01e0c8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e0c8). Sends a one-wire reset pulse and returns the level sampled afterwards (zero means a device answered): calls the write callback with a 500-unit low time and 65-unit sample delay, waits, reads the sampled bit, then makes a second call of 395 units to finish the time slot. |
| `nic_write` | 0x0c01e128 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e128). Sends one bit on the one-wire line through the context's write callback: a low pulse of 80 units for a zero bit or 5 units for a one bit, then a wait of 5 units, then a further wait of 1 unit for a zero or 60 units for a one, each step waiting for completion. |
| `nic_read` | 0x0c01e190 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e190). Reads one bit from the one-wire line: calls the write callback for a 5-unit low pulse and a sample after 5 units, waits for completion, keeps the sampled level, makes a second step of 1 unit, waits again and returns the sampled bit. |
| `nic_write_byte` | 0x0c01e1ec | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e1ec). Sends a byte (second argument) on the one-wire line, least significant bit first, by calling the bit-send routine eight times. |
| `nic_read_byte` | 0x0c01e228 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e228). Reads a byte from the one-wire line, least significant bit first, by calling the bit-read routine eight times and shifting each bit in from the top. |
| `nic_next_scan` | 0x0c01e268 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e268). One step of the one-wire ROM search: returns 1 if the search was already finished, 2 if no device answers the reset pulse, otherwise sends the search command 0xf0 and for each of 64 positions reads a bit and its complement and chooses a branch, recording the identifier bits and the last point where a choice remained in the context. It returns 2 if both reads give one (no device on the line), otherwise 0, setting the finished flag when no choice point is left. |
| `nic_setup` | 0x0c01e3b0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e3b0). Initialises a one-wire context: stores the status-read callback, write callback and handle (second, third and fourth arguments) in its first three words, clears the search position and finished flag, and tail-calls the presence routine, so its return value is the presence result. |
| `nic_next` | 0x0c01e3c4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e3c4). Finds the next one-wire device: runs the search step, packs the 64 found bits into 8 bytes, copies six of them (reversed) to the first output pointer and one to the second if not null, then checks all 8 bytes with the CRC-8 table. Returns 0 when the CRC checks, 3 when it fails, or the search step's own code. |
| `nic_rom_skip` | 0x0c01e4c4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e4c4). Despite its name this does not skip the ROM step: after a presence check (returning 2 if no device answers) it sends the match-ROM command 0x55 and then the 64 identifier bits held in the context from offset 0x1c, so the next command goes to that one device. Returns 0. |
| `nic_crc16` | 0x0c01e518 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e518). Folds one byte (first argument) into a running CRC (second argument) with the one-wire CRC-16 step: the odd-parity table decides whether to xor the constants 0xc001, then the byte shifted left six and seven places is xored in. Returns the new value and also leaves a trace word in the table area. |
| `nic_calc_crc` | 0x0c01e574 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e574). Computes the same CRC-16 over a buffer (first argument) of the given length (second) and stores the complemented low byte through the third argument and the complemented high byte through the fourth. |
| `nic_read_redirected_page` | 0x0c01e5e4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e5e4). Reads a memory page from the one-wire device the match-ROM routine last addressed, following page redirection. Starting at the page number in the second argument, it sends the read command 0xa5 with the page address, reads three bytes while checking the address CRC, and if the redirect byte is 0xff reads the 32 data bytes and two CRC bytes into the buffer in the third argument, checking the CRC; otherwise it moves to the redirect page. Returns 0 on success, 2 if no device answers or the page number reaches 64, 3 on a CRC failure (its diagnostics go to the do-nothing print routine). |
| `knic_setup` | 0x0c01e938 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1e938). Prepares a scan chain for bit-banging the serial-number line: records the chain number (first argument), then branches on the chain and, for most chains, on the configuration code, driving the scan controller's control, test-mode and data registers directly to load the instruction that connects the line to the controller. Returns 0, or 1 for chain 2, chain 0 or chains above 8; an unrecognised chip case prints a message such as "TM7 instr: unexpected case = %d". |
| `knic_pulldown` | 0x0c01f120 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1f120). Drives the serial-number line low: for the current chain, writes the scan controller's control, test-mode and data registers (chosen by chain and configuration code) so the chip's pin outputs zero, then waits for the controller's status bit to clear and returns. |
| `knic_release` | 0x0c01f2ac | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1f2ac). Lets the serial-number line float high again: the counterpart of the pull-down routine, it writes the scan controller's registers (chosen by chain and configuration code) so the chip's pin stops driving, waits for the status bit to clear and returns. |
| `knic_sample` | 0x0c01f5d0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1f5d0). Reads the serial-number line level: shifts a capture through the scan controller (per chain and configuration code), takes the sampled bit from the top of the data register, stores it in the last-sampled-value word and returns it. |
| `knic_stall` | 0x0c01fae0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1fae0). Waits for the given number of time units: returns at once if the count is at or below the per-chain no-sleep threshold, otherwise sleeps for the excess times the chain's clock factor. |
| `knic_write` | 0x0c01fb04 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1fb04). The one-wire step callback. The second argument packs a low time in the top half and a wait in the bottom half; if the low time is nonzero it pulls the line down, stalls for that time and releases it. It then stalls for the wait and samples the line, leaving the sampled bit as the last-sampled value. |
| `knic_read` | 0x0c01fb40 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1fb40). The one-wire status callback: returns the last-sampled-value word with bit 1 (step done) forced on. |
| `sysconf` | 0x04067edc | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). The board-configuration block at the start of zero-initialised data, filled at start-up (engine count at +0x54, occupancy mask at +0x5c, engine numbering from +0x64); `arm_getconfig` returns its 478 words to the host. |
| `v0` | 0x0408ce34 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A 1 KB bit-vector buffer for the boundary-scan chain, shifted in and out by `jt_reg_read`, `jt_reg_write` and the board and chip identity readers. |

## Display generator

The display-generator requests (`arm_video_cmd`, [dg_dispatch.tsv](dg_dispatch.tsv)) and their helpers. [docs/dg.md](../docs/dg.md) explains the display side; the entries here say what each routine in `arm1.u` does.

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `init_voc` | 0x0c0037a0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37a0). Returns at once: one move of the return address into the program counter. Nothing calls it. **Inferred:** a placeholder for video-output-chip set-up that the display-generator code does instead, because the other two empty routines beside it are never called either. |
| `init_dac` | 0x0c0037a4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37a4). Returns at once and nothing calls it. **Inferred:** a placeholder for video-DAC set-up, which the display code does instead through its own request handlers. |
| `init_fm` | 0x0c0037a8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37a8). Returns at once and nothing calls it. **Inferred:** a placeholder for set-up of the display board's frame-merge chip, which later display requests program instead. |
| `init_dg_board` | 0x0c003d3c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x3d3c). Start-up step for the display-generator board: it runs the scan-chain set-up, and on an older board prints that external gamma is forced to internal and clears the flag; unless simulated, it then blanks every channel, runs the stored-combination loader, refreshes the configuration record and writes 0 to board register 0x420. |
| `dgLoadCombo` | 0x0c0043c4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x43c4). Runs a stored video combination (first argument a word pointer, second the word count): a first pass checks that each command number is below 0x5d and that its payload words sum to the trailing checksum word, then a second pass runs each command through the dispatch table. When the late-start flag is set it runs only the commands aimed at the option-board channels, otherwise only the others; it returns 1 on success and 0 on a bad checksum or a failed command. |
| `DoDGRqstAutoInit` | 0x0c004674 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4674). Display request 45: reads two header words from the EEPROM at 0x20000 into the 128 KB scratch buffer and, if the first is 0xfacefeed and the word count is below 0x8000, reads the combination and runs it with `dgLoadCombo`. A bad header goes straight to the built-in emergency format of 0x3fe words; a stored combination that fails to load first prints a message and rebuilds the display state from scratch with `dgFetal`. A failed emergency load prints a failure message. |
| `dgAutoInit` | 0x0c0047d4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x47d4). Clears the late-start flag and calls the auto-initialise request with a one-word zero payload, so the stored combination is loaded for every channel that is not an option-board channel. `init_dg_board` calls it at start-up. |
| `dgAutoInitLateChannels` | 0x0c004800 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4800). Sets the late-start flag, calls the auto-initialise request with a zero payload so only the commands for option-board channels run, then clears the flag. The X-server video-init request calls it as a tail call. |
| `dgWaitForVocPllInterface` | 0x0c004834 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4834). Polls bit 0 of video-output-chip register 0x70 of the channel in the first argument, at most 99 times; when it never sets, it prints a timeout message naming the source file video_clocks.c and line 62. It returns to its caller either way. |
| `dgLoadVocClockParams` | 0x0c004894 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4894). Loads one clock-parameter set into a video-output chip: it turns the eight payload words into seven packed words (dividers reduced by 1, control bits merged) and writes each to the chip with an index-then-value pair, the slot number to register 0x71 and the word to register 0x73, with short stack-counter delays. Set 0 uses slots 0-6 and set 1 slots 8-14; the last word differs for three option-board channel ids. |
| `dgProgramClock` | 0x0c004a5c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4a5c). Waits for the clock interface of the chip named by the first argument, then writes its clock-control register 0x70 with bit 4 taken from the second argument, bit 3 set when that argument is zero and bit 1 taken from the third argument, followed by a short delay. |
| `dgSelectVocClockSrc` | 0x0c004af8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4af8). Read-modify-writes video-output-chip register 0x81: bit 0 becomes the second argument's truth value (inverted when the first configuration word is zero and the decoded board code is below 2) and bit 1 the third argument, with a delay after the write. It picks the clock source the channel runs from. |
| `dgStartVocClock` | 0x0c004bbc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4bbc). Starts a channel's pixel clock. With the mode argument zero it programs the clock, waits for the clock interface and sleeps a long interval before selecting the source; with a non-zero mode it selects the source first and then programs the clock. |
| `DoDGRqstEnableVocReadback` | 0x0c004c1c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4c1c). Display request 42: builds a fixed clock-parameter set on the stack, loads it into the chip named by the payload word, restarts that chip's clock with mode 0, then sets bit 1 of its register 0x44. **Inferred:** that bit is the read-back enable, from the request name. |
| `DoDGRqstMasterClockSelect` | 0x0c004ce8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4ce8). Display request 43: sets bit 23 of display-bus register 0x8e to 1 when the payload word is 1 and clears it otherwise, with settling delays, and saves the payload word in the display state for the matching query. |
| `DoDGRqstGetMasterClockSelect` | 0x0c004d68 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4d68). Display request 44: replies to the host with reply type 0xd and the four-byte master-clock-select word that the set request stored in the display state. |
| `DoDGRqstXMapClockSelect` | 0x0c004d94 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4d94). Display request 62: when the payload differs from the stored value and is 0 or 1, writes it to display-bus register 0x1900 on one board type or 0x8000 on the other, with settling delays, and stores it in the display state; any other value prints an error message. |
| `DoDGRqstGetXMapClockSelect` | 0x0c004e94 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4e94). Display request 63: replies to the host with reply type 0x12 and the four-byte colour-map clock-select word that the set request stored in the display state. |
| `dgLoadChannelGenlockParams` | 0x0c004ec0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x4ec0). Writes the 16-word genlock parameter block (second argument) into the video-output chip named by the first argument: register 6 bits 12-9 and bits 2-1 (set except on channel 1), register 0x81 bit 0, register 0x70 bit 1 cleared, registers 0x82-0x84, then the frame-detection fields at 0x30-0x32, and register 0x43 with its low bit cleared (on channel 1 also register 1 is changed and bit 0 of 0x43 set again after a 50-tick pause). Each store is followed by a short delay. |
| `DoDGRqstLoadGenlockParams` | 0x0c005484 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x5484). Display request 25: a tail call that runs the per-channel genlock-parameter writer for every present video-output chip, passing the request payload. |
| `dgIsChannelGenlockable` | 0x0c005494 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x5494). Returns 1 when the channel's register 0x81 holds a non-zero lock multiplier in bits 11-2, else 0, and returns 0 for channel 1 when the internal-genlock flag in the display state is set. |
| `dgEnableChannelGenlockClocks` | 0x0c0054d4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x54d4). When the channel's record has its genlock-capable bit set, the channel is genlockable and the request's enable word is zero, reprograms the channel's clock for its own timing and waits for the clock interface; otherwise it returns doing nothing. |
| `dgEnableChannelGenlock` | 0x0c005538 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x5538). Switches one channel in or out of genlock according to the request's enable word: it selects the clock source, programs the clock, rewrites register 6 bits 12-9 from the stored reset ratio and its enable bits, sets bit 0 of register 0x30 (and bit 4 when genlock is being switched off), and for the three option-board channels also updates registers in the option-board windows at 0x630000-0x63a0c0. Channel 1 additionally has bit 0 of register 0x43 cleared during the change and set again after a 50-tick pause. |
| `dgShutdownRequesters` | 0x0c0059d0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x59d0). For a channel whose record has the requester bit set, clears bits 0-1 of register 1 and bit 0 of register 0x44, stopping the channel's video requester; otherwise returns without writing. It is run for every present chip before a reset or a genlock change. |
| `dgStartupRequesters` | 0x0c005a90 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x5a90). For a channel whose record has the requester bit set, writes 0 to register 2, sets register 1 bits 0-1 to 1, sets bit 0 of register 0x44 and writes 1 to register 2, restarting the video requester; otherwise returns without writing. It undoes `dgShutdownRequesters`. |
| `DoDGRqstEnableGenlock` | 0x0c005be0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x5be0). Display request 26: blanks all channels, stops the requesters on every chip, waits, and when the payload is zero reprograms every channel's clock; then switches every channel's genlock to the payload value, records it in bit 31 of the display-state flag word, waits, restarts the requesters and unblanks. |
| `dgShutdownChannelGenlockNicely` | 0x0c005cb0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x5cb0). Probes one channel for genlock: it prints that it is checking, toggles bits 0-1 of its register 1 and verifies the readback, then tests register 6 bit 0, register 0x81 bit 0 and register 0x30 bit 0; if the channel is genlocked it clears bit 0 of register 0x44, prints that it detected a genlocked channel and switches genlock off for it. |
| `dgShutdownGenlockNicely` | 0x0c005f28 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x5f28). Runs the genlock probe for channel ids 0-7 and then for ids 0x1e-0xfd, one id at a time, shutting down genlock wherever it is found. No call or literal-pool reference to this routine exists in the image, so it is dead code. |
| `dgFlushStatus` | 0x0c005f68 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x5f68). Called once per present video-output chip: unless the chip is channel 1, ORs its status register (register 0) into the word the second argument points to. |
| `dgGetInterruptState` | 0x0c005f8c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x5f8c). Fills a three-word buffer with the display-bus words at 0x90 and 0x300, clears the third, then ORs in the status register of every present video-output chip other than channel 1, so the host sees one combined interrupt-state word. |
| `DoDGRqstGetDGInterruptState` | 0x0c005fb8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x5fb8). Display request 61: collects the interrupt state, sends it to the host as reply type 0x11 with 12 bytes, then writes 0 to display-bus register 0x8f. |
| `DoDGRqstChannelResetRatios` | 0x0c006028 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6028). Display request 64: stores the request's second and third words in the channel record at offsets 0x9c and 0xa0 for the video-output chip named by the first word; the genlock code later puts one of them into register 6. |
| `DoDGRqstResetRMBoards` | 0x0c006064 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6064). Display request 65: prints a start message, blanks all channels, stops every requester, clears bits 0-1 of display-bus register 0x301 and bit 0 of 0x306, waits, resets the image-processor chips on the raster boards, prints that it is restarting the master chip, sets bit 0 of 0x306 again, waits, restarts the requesters, waits and unblanks. Bits 0-1 of register 0x301 are never set again by this routine. |
| `DoDGRqstGenlockSwitching` | 0x0c006250 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6250). Display request 68: copies bit 0 of the payload into bit 30 of the display-state flag word, the genlock-switching flag; no hardware register is written. |
| `DoDGRqstGetGenlockSwitching` | 0x0c006270 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6270). Display request 69: clears bit 0 of display-bus register 0x343, waits 50 ticks and restores it, then replies with reply type 0x14 and bit 30 of the display-state flag word. Despite the name of a query, it pulses channel 1's register 0x43 each time it runs. |
| `DoDGRqstInternalGenlock` | 0x0c006344 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6344). Display request 84: copies bit 0 of the payload into bit 27 of the display-state flag word, the internal-genlock flag that later makes the genlock-capable test refuse channel 1; no hardware register is written. |
| `DoDGRqstGetInternalGenlock` | 0x0c006364 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6364).
