Replies to the host with one word, bit 27 of the firmware's genlock-state word at 0x0406cd1c, which the internal-genlock request stores from its payload; reply type 0x1c, sent through the reply writer. Touches no display hardware. |
| `arm_video_cmd` | 0x0c006398 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6398). Token 0x1a6 handler (the display-generator command port): reads a command number from the host-interface block, rejects numbers of 0x5d or more, then reads as many payload words as the command's entry in the dispatch table gives (on the stack up to 16 words, else into the 128 KB scratch buffer). Adds them to the command number, compares with the checksum word at offset 0x3c and prints a message and drops the command on a mismatch, else calls the entry's handler. Keeps the last two command numbers in a history pair. |
| `DoDGRqstCursorChannel` | 0x0c0065d4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x65d4). Selects which video channel owns the hardware cursor: looks up the per-channel record named by payload word 0 and writes its first word (the channel's index) to XMAP register 0x100, with short delay loops around the access. Dispatch-table request only. |
| `DoDGRqstCursorType` | 0x0c00663c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x663c). Sets the cursor mode in XMAP register 0x103: payload 0 writes 2, payload 1 writes 1, any other value writes 0 (cursor off). Delay loops surround the display-bus write; no state is kept. |
| `DoDGRqstCursorPosition` | 0x0c0066a4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x66a4). Writes the cursor position to XMAP register 0x102: payload word 0 masked to 13 bits is the horizontal part and payload word 1 shifted left by 13 and masked to bits 13-25 is the vertical part, combined into one word, the same layout the host kernel driver writes. |
| `DoDGRqstCursorPriority` | 0x0c006710 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6710). Stores payload word 1 (the cursor priority) at offset 4 of the per-channel record named by payload word 0; it writes no display register, so the value only changes firmware state. |
| `dgVDacControlWrite` | 0x0c006730 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6730). Writes one indexed control register of a video DAC: argument 0 is the channel, argument 1 the register number, argument 2 the value. Sends the register number as a low byte then a high byte to the DAC index port at display-bus address 0xb00 plus 0x100 per channel, then the value to the data port at 0xb02, with delay loops between accesses. |
| `dgVDacControlRead` | 0x0c006814 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6814). Reads one indexed control register of a video DAC: argument 0 is the channel, argument 1 the register number. Sends the number as two bytes to the DAC index port at display-bus address 0xb00 plus 0x100 per channel, waits, and returns the value read from the data port at 0xb02. |
| `dgVDacClutWrite` | 0x0c0068b8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x68b8). Writes one byte of a video DAC's colour map: argument 0 is the channel, argument 1 the map index sent as two bytes to the index port at 0xb00 plus 0x100 per channel, argument 2 the value written to the colour-map data port at 0xb01. No caller in the image uses it. |
| `dgVDacClutRead` | 0x0c00699c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x699c). Reads one byte of a video DAC's colour map: argument 0 is the channel, argument 1 the map index sent as two bytes to the index port at 0xb00 plus 0x100 per channel; returns the byte read from the colour-map data port at 0xb01. No caller in the image uses it. |
| `dgVDacModeWrite` | 0x0c006a40 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6a40). Writes argument 1 to a video DAC's mode port at display-bus address 0xb03 plus 0x100 per channel (argument 0), after a delay loop and followed by another. Its only caller is the video-DAC reset sequence. |
| `dgVDacModeRead` | 0x0c006a90 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6a90). Returns the value read from a video DAC's mode port at display-bus address 0xb03 plus 0x100 per channel (the first argument), with no delay loops. No caller in the image uses it. |
| `dgGDacChannelWrite` | 0x0c006a9c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6a9c). Writes a register of the gain or gamma DAC serving a channel: argument 0 is the channel (0-7, or 0xdc/0xde for the option-board DAC), argument 1 picks register slot 0-2, argument 2 is the value. Channels 0-7 use display-bus block 0x1300 plus 0x100 per channel pair, registers 4/5/0xf for even channels and 2/3/0x17 for odd ones; the option-board channels write 0xe600/0xe601/0xe610 (bit 0 only). |
| `dgSetDacPixelMux` | 0x0c006db4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6db4). Sets the pixel-multiplex ratio of a video DAC: argument 0 is the channel (0-7, or 0xde mapped to 0xdc), argument 1 the pixels per clock. Ratios 1/2/4 map to codes 0/1/3, written to bits 7-6 of video-DAC control register 7 (the other bits are written as zero); any other ratio prints an error and writes nothing. |
| `dgBlankChannel` | 0x0c006e40 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x6e40). Blanks or unblanks one channel's output: argument 0 is the channel id, argument 1 points to the blank flag (zero means use the channel record's stored blank bit). For video DACs it writes 0 (blank) or 0xff to control register 4; for the option-board id 0xc6 it sets a blank field in register 0xc104 and, when blanking, writes 1024 zero entries at 0xf000; for id 0x5e it sends the option board a command. |
| `DoDGRqstChannelBlanking` | 0x0c007088 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x7088). Records the blank flag (payload word 1, one bit) in bit 31 of the channel record's flag word for the channel in payload word 0, and applies it through the blanking routine only when no temporary all-channel blank is in force. |
| `DoDGRqstGetChannelBlanking` | 0x0c0070d0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x70d0). Replies (type 9) with one word: the channel's stored blank bit, from bit 31 of its record's flag word, or 1 when a temporary all-channel blank is in force. Reads no display hardware. |
| `DoDGRqstTempBlankAllChannels` | 0x0c007124 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x7124). Nests a temporary blank of every present channel: a nonzero payload increments a blank-depth counter and blanks all channels only if it was not already positive; a zero payload decrements it (clamped at zero) and unblanks all when it reaches zero. Walks the present video-output chips with the per-channel blank routine. Used by genlock handling and the board reset. |
| `dgTempBlankAllChannels` | 0x0c007188 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x7188). Wrapper that puts its argument (nonzero to blank, zero to unblank) on the stack as a one-word payload and calls the temporary-blank request. Called once with 1 by the display-board start-up code, so every channel is blank before the stored video combination loads. |
| `DoDGRqstOutputGain` | 0x0c0071a4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x71a4). Sets the output gain of a video DAC channel (payload word 0, ids 0-7 or 0xde; word 1 the gain 0-255). Stores the gain in the channel record and writes 255 minus the gain to the gain DAC through the gain-DAC writer; channel 1, when its record marks encoder drive, instead stores it in the encoder gain word and writes display-bus register 0x1708. |
| `DoDGRqstGetOutputGain` | 0x0c007270 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x7270). Replies (type 0xa) with one word, the gain last set for the channel in payload word 0: the stored value in its channel record, or for channel 1 with the encoder-drive flag set the shared encoder gain word. Reads no display hardware. |
| `dgResolveVDacGain` | 0x0c0072d8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x72d8). Recomputes the gain-select field, bits 4-3, of video-DAC control register 8 from the sync and pedestal switches held in registers 6 and 8: gain code 2 when no switch is set, 1 when exactly one of the two groups is set, 0 when both are. Argument 0 is the channel (0xde maps to 0xdc); ids outside 0-7 are ignored. |
| `DoDGRqstSyncSelects` | 0x0c007394 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x7394). Sets one sync-output option of a video DAC channel: payload word 0 is the channel, word 1 picks one of five options (control register 8 bit 1, register 6 bit 2, register 8 bit 2, the gain-DAC sync bit, or the sync-source bits of display-bus register 0x8400), word 2 the on/off value, word 3 control register 8 bit 0. Option-board ids use registers 0x3400/0xcd00 instead; it ends by recomputing the DAC gain select. |
| `DoDGRqstGetSyncSelects` | 0x0c007808 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x7808). Replies (type 0xb) with six words giving the sync options of the channel in payload word 0: control register 8 bit 1, register 6 bit 2, register 8 bit 2, register 8 bit 0, the record's stored gain-DAC sync bit, and a sync-source code read from register 0x8400 or the option-board register. Unsupported ids return minus one in four of them. |
| `DoDGRqstOutputPedestal` | 0x0c0079ec | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x79ec). Sets the output pedestal (payload word 1, low bit) of the channel in payload word 0: channel 1 additionally goes through the encoder pedestal routine; ids 0-7 set bit 3 of video-DAC control register 6 from the value, then the gain select is recomputed. |
| `DoDGRqstGetOutputPedestal` | 0x0c007a54 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x7a54). Replies (type 0xc) with one word: bit 3 of control register 6 of the video DAC for the channel in payload word 0 (ids 0-7), or minus one for any other id. |
| `DoDGRqstLoadDacCursor` | 0x0c007aa8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x7aa8). Loads a video DAC's hardware cursor (263 payload words: channel, six colour words, 256 image words): selects the DAC's indexed register 0x0303 and writes six colour bytes to the data port, then selects index 0x0400 and streams 1024 image bytes, four from each image word, low byte first. Only ids 0-7 and 0xde are accepted. **Inferred:** index 0x0303 is the cursor colours and 0x0400 the cursor RAM, from the byte counts and the 64 by 64 two-bit size of the image. |
| `DoDGRqstDiagXmapColor` | 0x0c007ef0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x7ef0). Diagnostic request 3 (ten payload words): packs the colour in the payload, in a layout chosen by word 7, into two words written to XMAP registers 0x106 and 0x107 for every XMAP chip whose bit is set in the mask in word 9 (the chip number goes to 0x108 first), switches on the substitute colour in XMAP register 0x105 and forces an XMAP copy-buffer update. |
| `DoDGRqstDisableDiagXmapColor` | 0x0c008120 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x8120). Diagnostic request 4: writes 0 to XMAP register 0x105, turning the substitute colour off, then forces an XMAP copy-buffer update. It reads no payload. |
| `DoDGRqstVCBusTrace` | 0x0c00816c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x816c). Diagnostic request 5: prints the message that the firmware must be compiled with bus tracing enabled and returns, so in this build the request does nothing to the hardware and ignores its four payload words. |
| `DoDGRqstDiagCursorFromImage` | 0x0c0081a0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x81a0). Diagnostic request 6 (4107 payload words): writes payload word 0 to XMAP register 0x118, then converts a 64 by 64 image of two-bit pixels, one pixel per word, taken from the bottom row up, into four packed words per row (two bit planes of 64 pixels) written to XMAP data port 0x119, then writes colour words packed from ten-bit fields near the payload end to port 0x119 and a closing 0-3 value sequence to ports 0x11f and 0x11e. |
| `dgEncoderControlWrite` | 0x0c00852c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x852c). Writes one register of the video encoder chip: argument 0 is the register number, sent to the encoder index port at display-bus address 0x1800, argument 1 the value written to the control data port at 0x1802, with delay loops between the accesses. |
| `dgEncoderControlRead` | 0x0c0085bc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x85bc). Reads one register of the video encoder chip: sends the register number (argument 0) to the encoder index port at display-bus address 0x1800, waits, and returns the value read from the control data port at 0x1802. |
| `dgEncoderClutWrite` | 0x0c008614 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x8614). Writes one byte of the video encoder's colour map: argument 0 is the index sent to the encoder's map index port at display-bus address 0x1801, argument 1 the value written to the map data port at 0x1803. No caller in the image uses it. |
| `dgEncoderClutRead` | 0x0c0086a4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x86a4). Reads one byte of the video encoder's colour map: sends the index (argument 0) to the map index port at display-bus address 0x1801 and returns the value read from the map data port at 0x1803. No caller in the image uses it. |
| `dgEncoderPedestal` | 0x0c0086fc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x86fc). Sets the encoder's pedestal control: reads encoder register 0xf and rewrites bit 4 and the two 2-bit fields at bits 3-2 and 1-0 from the argument and the master-clock-select word saved by the clock request; when that word is zero the fields become 2 and bit 4 clears, else bit 4 takes the argument and the fields become 1 for a zero argument and 0 otherwise. |
| `DoDGRqstEncoderOutputGain` | 0x0c008784 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x8784). Sets the video encoder's output gain: keeps the low 8 bits of payload word 0 in the shared encoder gain word and writes 255 minus that value to display-bus register 0x1708 (with delay loops), the same gain register channel 1 uses when it is encoder-driven. |
| `DoDGRqstGetEncoderOutputGain` | 0x0c0087e8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x87e8). Replies (type 0xe) with one word, the encoder output gain last stored by the encoder gain request; reads no display hardware. |
| `DoDGRqstSCHPhase` | 0x0c008814 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x8814). Sets the encoder's subcarrier-to-sync phase: writes the low and high bytes of payload word 0 to encoder registers 0x24-0x25, then the low and high bytes of that value plus 0x2000 to registers 0x26-0x27. |
| `DoDGRqstGetSCHPhase` | 0x0c008870 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x8870). Replies (type 0xf) with one word, the encoder's phase value rebuilt from the bytes read back from encoder registers 0x24 (low) and 0x25 (high). |
| `DoDGRqstLoadEncoder` | 0x0c0088b4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x88b4). Programs the video encoder from scratch: clears control register 0 twice, sets it again from the master-clock-select word, then writes registers 0xe/0xf/1/2 (bit 2 from payload word 0), clears registers 3-4, loads one of two fixed tables into registers 0x10-0x23 chosen by the master-clock-select word, and clears registers 0x26-0x27 (0x27 gets 0x20 for the second table). **Inferred:** the two tables are the two video standards, since the choice follows the master clock select. |
| `DoDGRqstEncoderTestPattern` | 0x0c008c00 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x8c00). Controls the encoder test pattern: reads encoder register 1, clears bit 5 and sets bit 4, then payload 1 clears bit 4 and payload 2 sets bit 5 (other values leave bit 4 set and bit 5 clear), and writes the result back to register 1. |
| `DoDGRqstEnableEncoder` | 0x0c008c44 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x8c44). Enables or disables the video encoder: writes encoder control register 0 twice with bit 0 and bit 4 both taken from bit 0 of payload word 0 and bit 3 set when the master-clock-select word is zero. |
| `DoDGRqstFetal` | 0x0c008cb0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x8cb0). Builds the display board's default state from scratch (the payload is ignored): clears the blank-depth counter, writes 0xff to display-bus register 0x1711, sets the raster-board count to zero, fills display-bus words 0-0x3f with 0x20 and 0x40-0x5f with 0, zeroes three 4096-word XMAP banks, then runs the XMAP, channel, VOC, video-DAC, gain-DAC and encoder default builders for each VOC the board reports present, the option-board builders by board type, and a board-generation-specific final register write. |
| `dgFetal` | 0x0c009628 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x9628). Thin wrapper that pushes a zero word as a payload and calls the default-state request; the request ignores its payload. Called by the start-up code unless the firmware runs in the simulator, and again by the stored-combination loader when the flash image fails to load, just before it loads the emergency format. |
| `DoDGRqstXServerVideoInit` | 0x0c009648 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x9648). Dispatch-table request 83: when the board option word is 6 it pulses display-bus register 0xc000 to 1 then 0 with sleeps between, then walks the 17 option VOC entries, rebuilding default VOC state for each present option VOC (printing warnings for unexpected or missing ones), and finishes by tail-calling the late-channel auto-initialisation. |
| `dgMakeXmapFetal` | 0x0c0097d4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x97d4). Writes the XMAP defaults: zero to seven of the registers 0x100-0x109, then 2 to 0x10c, a board-dependent pair of values (0xa5 with 6, or 0x64 with 3) to 0x10d-0x10e, 0x339 to 0x10f and zero to 0x110, then forces an XMAP copy-buffer update so the values take effect. |
| `dgMakeChannelFetal` | 0x0c009b98 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x9b98). Initialises a channel record: argument 0 is the channel id, argument 1 its ordinal in the record array. Stores the ordinal, the id and minus one (the vertical-phase word), clears the size, format and field fields, stores 0x500 and 0x400 in two size fields, puts the flag word in a known state, then calls the gamma-map request with the id and zero. Writes no display register directly. |
| `dgMakeVocFetal` | 0x0c009c88 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x9c88). Resets one video-output chip to its defaults: writes zero to VOC registers 0x01/0x07/0x30/0x43/0x44/0x50/0x60/0x70/0x81/0x8d/0x8f, the channel ordinal's low five bits to register 0x03, a 0x10-then-zero pair to 0x06 and 0x10000 to 0x14, then resets the VOC's FIFO and reads back register 0. Argument 0 is the channel id. |
| `dgMakeVDacFetal` | 0x0c00a108 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xa108). Resets a video DAC to defaults: writes mode port values 6 then 7 with delays, control register 5 to 0x88, register 6 to 0xe4, loads a 256-entry linear ten-bit ramp into the colour map (each value written three times), then register 4 to 0xff and registers 7/8/0xd/9/0x204 to zero. Argument 0 is the channel (0-7, or 0xdc). |
| `dgMakeGDacFetal` | 0x0c00a3f0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xa3f0). Sets the gain DAC of a channel to its default of 0x80: calls the gain-DAC writer for the channel (argument 0) with selector 0, then selector 1, each with value 0x80. |
| `dgMakeEncoderFetal` | 0x0c00a420 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xa420). Loads a linear ramp into the video encoder's colour map: sets the map index port at display-bus 0x1801 to 0, then for each of 256 entries writes the entry number three times to the map data port at 0x1803. |
| `dgMakeGvoFetal` | 0x0c00a544 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xa544). Does nothing: the function is a single return, so the digital-video-output option board board type gets no default state set from here. |
| `dgMakeTvoFetal` | 0x0c00a548 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xa548). Selects the TVO option board: sets the two mode bits 12-11 of the saved copy of display-bus register 0x8400 to 1, writes the word to 0x8400 and starts the I2C interface with bus base 0xa000. |
| `dgMakeDplexFetal` | 0x0c00a5b4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xa5b4). Selects the DPLEX option board: sets the two mode bits 12-11 of the saved copy of display-bus register 0x8400 to 2 and writes it, writes 0 to 0x8001, writes 1 to 0xe300 and to 0xe500, then resets the option VOC id 0xde and video DAC id 0xdc to their defaults. A table of sequencer signal names follows it in the image. |
| `dgLoadFieldSequentialAndStereo` | 0x0c00a7ac | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xa7ac). Builds the per-field colour and eye selects of a loaded format: for each field (count in payload word 20) combines the field's colour word and eye code from the format payload into a field word, writes it to the channel's VOC register 0x60 plus the field number, and keeps a copy in the channel record; an eye code above 3 prints an error. |
| `dgEnableRequester` | 0x0c00a930 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xa930). Starts or stops a channel's video requester: argument 0 is the channel and argument 1 the enable bit, written to bit 0 of VOC register 0x44 (read, modify, write with delays), and mirrored into bit 27 of the channel record's flag word. |
| `DoDGRqstLoadFormat` | 0x0c00a9b0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xa9b0). Loads a video format (383 payload words) into a channel: disables the channel, copies the summary words into the channel record, loads both clock parameter sets and starts the VOC clock, writes the state table, edge and line-type registers of the sequencer, sets pixel mux, blanking, FIFO and vertical-phase registers, loads partner VOCs of option boards by recursion, sets the DAC pixel mux for ids 0-7, then the field selects and the XMAP channel format. Does not enable the channel. |
| `dgSyncWithMasterVoc` | 0x0c00b9a0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xb9a0). Waits for a frame boundary on the master VOC: reads its status at display-bus 0x300 until bit 7 goes high, then until it goes low, each wait bounded at one million ticks of the free-running counter at 0x2000b0. Returns the ticks elapsed since entry. |
| `DoDGRqstEnableChannel` | 0x0c00ba20 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xba20). Turns a channel on (payload: channel, turn-on flag, set-up flag). A set-up flag pauses the channel, sets VOC register 0x01 bits 1-0 to 1, computes a FIFO threshold from the record's width into register 0x8a (also on option-board copies), starts the requester and resumes it. A turn-on flag clears VOC 0x01 bits 1-0 on the write VOC, syncs to the master VOC, sets bit 0 of register 0x06 and marks the record enabled. |
| `DoDGRqstGetChannelEnables` | 0x0c00bf50 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xbf50). Replies (type 0x13) with two words from the channel record's flag word: the requester-enabled bit 27 and the channel-enabled bit 28 for the channel in payload word 0. Reads no display hardware. |
| `DoDGRqstDisableChannel` | 0x0c00bfb4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xbfb4). Turns a channel off: clears bits 1-0 of VOC register 0x01 and bit 0 of register 0x06 on the channel's write VOC, clears the enabled bit 28 in the record flag word (and in the partner option-board channel's record), and stops the requester. |
| `DoDGRqstGetChannelFormatInfo` | 0x0c00c10c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xc10c). Replies (type 0x17) with five words about the channel's loaded format, all from its record and none from the hardware: the saved pixel-format word at offset 0xac, flag bits 30 and 26, a constant minus one, and the saved vertical-phase word at offset 0x164. |
| `dgWriteDgoTMC22071a` | 0x0c00c1a4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xc1a4). Shifts a 47-bit configuration image out to the digital-video-output board's encoder chip: bits 46 down to 1 of the image (argument 0, an array of words) go one at a time to display-bus register 0xc400, each followed by a sleep of 50 ticks, then bit 0 of word 0 goes to 0xc401 as a final strobe. **Inferred:** a bit-serial load, from the one-bit writes and sleeps. |
| `DoDGRqstDataFormat` | 0x0c00c280 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xc280). Sets the data format of an output channel (payload: channel, format code, flag): stores the format and the flag in the channel record, then by channel id programs the option board. Ids 0x5e-0x60 write the same 0x8400 mode value as the TVO default builder and send a serial-bus byte for device 0xee chosen from the format; the digital video output ids load registers 0xc006/0xc104, the range 0xc200-0xc216 and the encoder bit image; the remaining option ids set mode bit 12 of 0x8400, and 0xde also writes three more registers. |
| `DoDGRqstGetDataFormat` | 0x0c00ca44 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xca44). Replies (type 0x1e) with two words about the channel in payload word 0: the data-format code kept at offset 0xb0 of its record, and 1 when bit 20 of the record's flag word is clear, else 0. Reads no display hardware. |
| `dgConfigureHDGVO` | 0x0c00caa4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xcaa4). Programs the HDGVO option board for one video format over the two-wire serial bus at display-bus address 0xa000. It sends nine short transactions to slave address 0xee, each a start, three or four fixed bytes, the format words (through the format-info sender) or a value that depends on whether the record's total-lines field is one of the two standard-definition line counts, and a stop. Three fixed values compared with the field at record offset 0x4c select further bytes. Takes the format record as its only argument and is called once from the format loader. |
| `dgSendHDGVOFormatInfo` | 0x0c00cea4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xcea4). Sends a format's timing words to the HDGVO option board, each as two serial-bus bytes (high then low) at display-bus address 0xa000: the words at record offsets 0x54-0x60, then the word at offset 0x4bc (plus one when the total-lines field is the 525-line standard). It then branches on that total-lines value for four video standards and appends a fixed tail of register numbers and bytes, including the word at offset 0x4c0. Called twice by the HDGVO configurator with the format record as its argument; writes only to the bus. |
| `EdgeDescArray` | 0x0c00d140 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xd140). Table of 14 pairs, each a pointer to a signal-name string in the code area (horizontal sync, vertical sync, composite sync, blanking, tri-level sync, field start, field, frame start, video active, line start, stereo out and three register-load or enable strobes) and a count of edge slots, from 2 to 8. Only the format loader reads it, walking the pairs to write each signal's edge positions to the video-output chip's edge-slot registers. |
| `dgGetBoardConfig` | 0x0c00d200 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xd200). Fills a five-word board-configuration record for its caller: word 0 is 1, word 1 is the word at offset 4 of the display-generator state, word 2 is 0, word 3 is a bit mask naming the option board, and word 4 is a word from the board-configuration block at the start of zero-initialised data. The mask comes from two numbers in that block: one picks between the two board families, and the other selects a bit pattern through a small jump table. A code outside the table makes it print a warning. |
| `DoDGRqstGetBoardConfig` | 0x0c00d328 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xd328). Display request 18: builds the five-word board-configuration record on the stack through the board-configuration reader, then sends it to the host as a reply of type 6 and 0x14 bytes through the reply writer. Reads no hardware itself and has no arguments of its own. |
| `DoDGRqstGetChannelConfig` | 0x0c00d354 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xd354). Display request 19: replies (type 7, a 0x4c-byte reply) with a description of one channel named by payload word 0. Reply word 0 is a flag mask: bit 0 when the channel has a real record, one bit chosen by the channel's id group (encoder, option-board families), and, only for a real record, two more bits taken from bit 0 of VOC registers 0x06 and 0x44. Only then do reply words 1-16 copy 16 words from channel-record offset 8; the next word is record offset 0x48 and the last is offset 4. |
| `dgUpdateConfigInfo` | 0x0c00d51c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xd51c). Runs the board-configuration reader and condenses it into two words of the zero-initialised data block: the number of set bits in the low byte of the record's word 1 (offset 0x44), and a small code 0-5 for the option-board kind taken from the first set bit of word 3 (offset 0x4c). It then zeroes the word at offset 0x50. Called once by the start-up routine for the display board; touches no hardware. |
| `DoDGRqstQueryMonitorName` | 0x0c00d5cc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xd5cc). Display request 80: reads the identity of the monitor on one output and sends a 0x44-byte reply of type 0x19. Only when the board-type word at the start of zero-initialised data is 1 does it read anything: for a channel number 0-7 it updates the monitor-select shadow word in the display-generator state (a one-hot bit in bits 7-0, the number in bits 10-8), writes it to bus address 0x8400, initialises the serial controller at bus address 0x8800 and calls the EDID reader; channel 0x5e uses the controller at 0xa000. After the read it zeroes the first two reply words, which overwrites the first EDID byte the reader stored, so words 2-4 carry EDID bytes 9-11 and the rest of the reply is unset stack contents. |
| `DoDGRqstMonitorCommand` | 0x0c00d6f8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xd6f8). Display request 81: a single return instruction. The dispatch table gives it 34 payload words, but the handler reads none of them, writes nothing and sends no reply, so the request has no effect on the firmware or the hardware. |
| `i2c_init` | 0x0c00d6fc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xd6fc). Initialises one two-wire serial-bus controller whose data word sits at display-bus address bus and control word at bus + 1 (bus is the argument). It writes control 0x80, data 0x50 (own address), control 0xa0, then 0x1c on the bus at 0xa000 or 0x14 on any other as the clock setting, control 0x80 again, with a 22000-tick sleep after each write. It reads the data word back and returns -1 unless it reads 0x50; otherwise it rewrites 0x50 and control 0xc0 and returns 0. |
| `read_edid` | 0x0c00da20 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xda20). Reads a monitor's 128-byte EDID block over the serial controller named by the first argument and stores four identification bytes for the caller. It runs six probe cycles (control 0xc1, slave byte 0x9a, control 0xc5, poll the status bit, stop 0xc3), re-initialising the controller and retrying on an error flag, then addresses the slave with 0x9b and reads 128 bytes one at a time, sending 0x40 before the last. After byte 12 it checks the first eight bytes against the 8-byte EDID header held in a constant just before the next function. On a match it copies bytes 8-11 (manufacturer and product code) to words 1-4 of the second argument and zeroes word 5. |
| `i2c_start` | 0x0c00de40 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xde40). Opens a transfer on the serial-bus controller named by the first argument: writes control 0xc1, then the second argument's low byte (the slave address) to the data word, then control 0xc5 (start), each followed by a short delay loop and a sleep of 22000 ticks. Returns nothing. Called by the HDGVO configurator, the data-format request and the VBOB command and reply requests. |
| `i2c_stop` | 0x0c00df58 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xdf58). Ends a transfer on the serial-bus controller named by the argument by writing control 0xc3 (stop) to its control word, then a short delay loop and a sleep of 22000 ticks. Returns nothing, and is the last step of every message to the HDGVO and VBOB boards. |
| `i2c_sendbyte` | 0x0c00dfb4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xdfb4). Writes one byte (the second argument) to the data word of the serial-bus controller named by the first argument, waits through a short delay, then polls the control word's status for up to 30000 tries until bit 7 clears. Returns 1 when bit 3 of the status is then clear (the slave acknowledged), and 0 on a missing acknowledge or a time-out. |
| `i2c_recvbyte` | 0x0c00e054 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xe054). Reads one byte from the serial-bus controller named by the first argument into the buffer of the second argument. It polls the status word for up to 30000 tries until bit 7 clears and returns -1 on time-out. With the third argument non-zero it first writes 0x40 to the control word and reads the data word once before the final read, so the last byte of a transfer is not acknowledged. Returns 0 on success. |
| `dgLoadVocIwsc` | 0x0c00e104 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xe104). Writes a channel's input-window registers in the video-output chip that dgGetWriteVoc picks: VOC 0x03 gets the screen width in groupxels minus 1 (bits 14-5) beside the low five bits of the screen record's first word, VOC 0x04 the window origin (x / 4 in bits 21-12, y in bits 11-0, both zero when the channel is its own screen), and VOC 0x05 the size ((width + 3) / 4 and height). A width under 4 groupxels prints a warning. Called by the video-requester and resize routines. |
| `dgConfigureVideoRequester` | 0x0c00e324 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xe324). Programs one channel's video requester, the VOC unit that fetches the frame-buffer window in tiles of 160 by 8 pixels. After writing the input window it splits the window into start, middle and end line requests when it spans more than 8 tiles (4 on one board type), writes them to VOC registers 0x47-0x49, the pan to 0x4a, per-field tile addresses to 0x50-0x67, and the control word, the tile row width and a few mode bits to 0x44 and 0x45 and register 0x06. A field-layout code it does not recognise prints a warning and is treated as progressive. Takes a 5-word window request. |
| `dgResize` | 0x0c00efc8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xefc8). Applies a resize request (eight words: channel, source x, y, width, height, two flags, default-rectangle flag) to one channel. It copies the rectangle into the channel record at offsets 0x50-0x5f and, when the last word is 0, also 0x60-0x6f, clips output widths and heights under 2 with a printed message, computes the horizontal and vertical scaler steps in 12.20 fixed point, and writes them to VOC registers 0x90-0x9d. It writes VOC register 0x02 as 0 then 1 around the update and calls the video-requester set-up for the new window. |
| `dgResizeVampire` | 0x0c00fd1c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xfd1c). Enumeration callback that repeats a resize request on every channel that copies the requested channel's output. It skips the channel itself and the option-board partner ids, and acts only when the candidate's source channel equals the request's channel. It copies the eight request words, substitutes its own channel, scales the rectangle by the ratio of the source channel's current rectangle size to its default size when the last request word is non-zero, copies the window id for channels 0-7, and calls the resize routine. |
| `DoDGRqstResize` | 0x0c00fe84 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xfe84). Display request 20: calls dgResize with the 8-word payload, then, when the channel's count of channels copying it (record offset 0xbc) is above zero, walks all present video-output chips so each dependent channel gets the same resize through the vampire callback. Writes hardware only through those callees. |
| `DoDGRqstGetResize` | 0x0c00fecc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0xfecc). Display request 21: replies (type 8, 0x48 bytes, 18 words) with the channel's current resize state read from its record: the words at offsets 0x50-0x6f, then the placement words stored by the placement request, then two flag bits from the control word at offset 0x184. Reads only firmware state. |
| `DoDGRqstPlacementRes` | 0x0c01003c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1003c). Display request 67: stores payload words 1-8 into the channel's record, four words at offsets 0xc0-0xcc and four at 0x70-0x7c, the eight placement parameters. Touches no hardware and sends no reply; the resize-state reply reads the same words back. |
| `DoDGRqstBindChannelToXWid` | 0x0c010104 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10104). Display request 52: stores payload word 1, the X window id, in the channel's slot of the per-channel window-id table in the display-generator state. Only when that id is 0 does it build a resize request from the channel's saved rectangle (record offsets 0x60-0x6f, two control bits, last word 0) and run it through the resize handler; a non-zero id returns at once without resizing. |
| `dgUpdateFMDidSwapTable` | 0x0c0101b4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x101b4). Called with a window id and a display id: for each of the ten channel slots whose bound X window id equals the first argument it writes the second argument to the swap-group table at display-bus address 0x81 plus the slot number, with a short delay after each write. Called by the display-mode token handler. |
| `dgEncoderOutputLetterbox` | 0x0c010230 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10230). Centres the encoder channel's output in its active picture area by writing the front-end timing registers at display-bus addresses 0x8b and 0x8e. It takes the channel's output width and height from its record, subtracts them from a fixed standard-definition raster (two sets of constants chosen by a state flag), halves the margins, and writes width-derived start and end fields into 0x8b and height-derived fields into 0x8e, setting a 2 in bits 22-20 of the second. |
| `dgDGOOutputLetterbox` | 0x0c0103ac | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x103ac). Centres the DGO channel's output (channel 0xc6 record) on a 720-wide raster by writing display-bus registers 0xc100-0xc103 with the computed horizontal start and end and vertical start and end, then sets bit 7 of 0xc104. The base offsets depend on bit 20 of the record's control word, and the width and height come from the record's output size. |
| `DoDGRqstVampire` | 0x0c010570 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10570). Display request 79: records that one channel copies another's output. It stores the source channel at record offset 0x4c, adjusting the old and new sources' dependent counts at offset 0xbc, the output width and height at 0x80-0x84, the field layout at 0xd0 and two filter sizes at 0xd4-0xd8. For channel 1 it then runs the encoder letterbox when bit 26 of the control word is set, and for the DGO board's channels the DGO letterbox. |
| `DoDGRqstVampireAndFieldDominance` | 0x0c0106b8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x106b8). Display request 57: for channel 0x1e it copies bit 31 of payload word 4 into bit 9 of the channel record's flag word at offset 0x88, then clears that bit in the payload and jumps to the vampire handler to process the rest unchanged. For every other channel it only clears the bit and jumps. Writes no hardware directly. |
| `DoDGRqstGetVampire` | 0x0c010708 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10708). Display request 58: replies (type 0x10, 0x18 bytes) with the channel's copy state from its record: the source channel, the output width and height, the field layout and the two filter words. Reads only firmware state; no hardware access. |
| `DoDGRqstChannelFieldDominance` | 0x0c0107ac | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x107ac). Display request 70: sets bit 9 of VOC register 0x8f of the channel to bit 0 of payload word 1 (field dominance), by read-modify-write, and keeps the new register value in the channel record's flag word at offset 0x88. For channels 0x1e and 0x1f on a board whose revision test passes it clears the bit instead. |
| `DoDGRqstGetChannelFieldDominance` | 0x0c010864 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10864). Display request 71: replies with one word, bit 9 of the channel record's flag word at offset 0x88 (type 0x15, 4 bytes), the field-dominance setting last stored for that channel. Reads only firmware state, no hardware. |
| `DoDGRqstResizeSyncType` | 0x0c0108a8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x108a8). Display request 78: sets bit 24 of the channel record's control word at offset 0x184 to 1 when payload word 1 equals 1 and to 0 otherwise. A flag in the firmware's own state; it writes no hardware and sends no reply. |
| `DoDGRqstBufferParams` | 0x0c0108e4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x108e4). Display request 86: stores bit 0 of payload word 1 in bit 21 of the channel record's control word (offset 0x184) and, except for channel 0x5e, payload word 2 in the record at offset 0x180. Record only: no hardware write and no reply. |
| `DoDGRqstGetBufferParams` | 0x0c010934 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10934). Display request 87: replies (type 0x1d, 8 bytes) with bit 21 of the channel record's control word and the word at record offset 0x180, the two values the buffer-parameter request stored. Reads only firmware state. |
| `isvoc1b` | 0x0c010990 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10990). Returns whether a board-identity record at 0x04068608 passes a revision test, caching the answer in a word at 0x040416e8 (initially -1). The answer is 1 only if flag bits 0-2 of the record's first word are clear, the bytes at offsets 0xa-0xb are non-zero, byte 0xc is 1, and the three-digit ASCII number found at offset 0x2c (leading spaces skipped) exceeds 4. Called when loading formats, resizing, and setting field dominance on the first option-board channel pair. **Inferred:** the name suggests a revision-1b video-output board; the code only shows the number comparison. |
| `DoDGRqstInitTextport` | 0x0c010a50 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10a50). Display request 39: writes 0 to display-bus address 0x118 (the XMAP's indirect-memory address), then 0x80050 to address 0x11c, the textport's initial XMAP configuration word, each followed by a short delay loop. Takes no payload and sends no reply. |
| `DoDGRqstLoadTextportGlyph` | 0x0c010ae8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10ae8). Display request 40: loads the textport cursor glyph into the XMAP. It zeroes the cursor registers at bus addresses 0x100-0x101 and the index register 0x118, sets the cursor register 0x103 to the value 2, then streams 260 words from the firmware's glyph buffer to the data port at 0x119. Reads no payload, so the glyph is whatever the buffer holds: 256 zero image words and four colour words. |
| `DoDGRqstLoadTextportColorIndex` | 0x0c010c64 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10c64). Display request 41: loads one textport colour. It packs payload words 1-3 as three 10-bit components into one word (word 1 in bits 29-20, word 2 in 19-10, word 3 in 9-0), writes the colour index plus 0x1000 from payload word 0 to display-bus address 0x118, then the packed word to the data port at 0x119. |
| `dgForceXmapCopyBuffer` | 0x0c010d24 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10d24). Writes 1 to display-bus address 0x104, the XMAP's copy-buffer strobe, with a short delay loop around it; returns nothing. Called by the XMAP defaults builder and by the two diagnostic-colour requests so the chip latches its newly written configuration. |
| `dgGetNumRMs` | 0x0c010d74 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10d74). Returns the number of raster boards the display board reports: it reads display-bus register 0x90, takes bits 10-8 and maps that code to a count between 0 and 4 boards, or -1 for the three codes that stand for no valid count. Read-only; called by the RM-count setter. |
| `dgSetNumRMs` | 0x0c010dd0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10dd0). Sets the raster-board count from its argument (one, two or four boards; zero keeps the current count, found through the count reader). A request with no board found prints a message and uses one board, and any other count prints an error and uses one. It writes a small code for the count to display-bus register 0x10b, and a board-stuffing code, shifted into bits 7-5, to register 0x90. |
| `dgIsVocPresent` | 0x0c010f6c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x10f6c). Tests whether a video-output chip exists at the given channel id (valid ids are 0-7 and 0x1e-0xfd, else returns 0). It writes the id plus 0x3e8 to the chip's register 0x231, writes 0 to register 0x232, reads 0x231 back and returns 1 when the value minus 0x3e8 equals the id, and 0 otherwise, so a chip that is not there fails the read-back. |
| `dgEnumPresentVocs` | 0x0c011048 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11048). Walks channel ids 0 to 0xfd and, for every id whose record pointer is not the shared placeholder for an absent channel, calls the function given as the first argument with the id and the second argument. Used to apply one operation to every present video-output chip: genlock, blanking, resize, vertical phase. Reads no hardware itself. |
| `dgResetVocFifo` | 0x0c01109c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1109c). Resets one video-output chip's request FIFO by writing 0x2301 to its register 0x8a, with a short delay loop around the write. The argument is the channel id; returns nothing. Called from the per-chip defaults builder. |
| `DoDGRqstDebugMask` | 0x0c0110f4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x110f4). Display request 7: stores payload word 0 in the first word of the display-generator state, the firmware's debug-print mask, and prints a line giving the new value. No hardware access and no reply. |
| `DoDGRqstSetupFrameBuffer` | 0x0c011128 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11128). Display request 8: derives the frame-buffer layout from payload words 0 (width), 1 (height) and 2 (pixel-size code). It stores the width in 160-pixel tiles at offset 0x36b4 of the display-generator state, reads the raster-board count from display-bus register 0x10b, and picks the pixel-size code stored at offset 0x36b8 from the size, count and request. When too few boards exist it prints a warning and sets bit 28 of the status word at 0x0406cd1c. It leaves width, height and size code in the state block at offsets 0x40-0x45. |
| `DoDGRqstInputSync` | 0x0c011490 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11490). Display request 9: sets bits 21-20 of display-bus register 0x8b to the low two bits of payload word 0, selecting the input sync source, by read-modify-write with short delays. Returns nothing. |
| `DoDGRqstGetInputSync` | 0x0c0114f4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x114f4). Display request 10: replies (type 2, 4 bytes) with bits 21-20 of display-bus register 0x8b, the input-sync source last written. Reads the register on the display board. |
| `DoDGRqstBoardResetRatio` | 0x0c011528 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11528). Display request 72: sets bit 22 of display-bus register 0x8b to bit 0 of payload word 0, by read-modify-write with a short delay loop. Returns nothing and sends no reply. |
| `DoDGRqstGetBoardResetRatio` | 0x0c01158c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1158c). Display request 73: replies (type 0x16, 4 bytes) with bit 22 of display-bus register 0x8b, the reset-ratio bit last written. Reads the register on the display board. |
| `DoDGRqstHPhase` | 0x0c0115c0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x115c0). Display request 11: sets a channel's horizontal phase from payload word 1. It splits the value, scaled by the format's line-length fields, into coarse and fine counts and writes them to VOC registers 0x0c-0x0d, with register 0x06 bits 8-4 set to 9, and records the phase at record offset 0x16c. DGO-board channels additionally get a delay value at bus address 0xcc00, channels 0-7 also update the gain DAC, and the VBOB channel, when its record flag is 1, sends a VBOB command and returns without touching the VOC. |
| `DoDGRqstGetHPhase` | 0x0c011928 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11928). Display request 12: replies (type 3, 4 bytes) with the horizontal phase last stored for the channel, the word at record offset 0x16c. Reads only firmware state. |
| `dgAdjustVPhase` | 0x0c011964 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11964). Enumeration callback that applies the vertical phase to one video-output chip. It adds the channel's offset (record offset 0x164) to the requested value, wraps it into the frame's line count (offset 0x90) and writes it into bits 25-13 of VOC register 0x08, leaving the register alone when that field already holds all ones. DGO-board channels get a computed value at bus address 0xcc00, and the VBOB channel a VBOB command. |
| `DoDGRqstVPhase` | 0x0c011b44 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11b44). Display request 13: decodes payload word 0 as a signed vertical phase (sign bit 20, magnitude bits 19-0) with the channel number plus one in bits 31-22. For -1 it stores the value in the shared slot at offset 0x36cc of the state and applies it to every present chip; for channel 1 it stores it in that slot and applies it there; otherwise it stores it at the channel's record offset 0x168. It applies it through the vertical-phase adjuster. |
| `DoDGRqstGetVPhase` | 0x0c011bc4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11bc4). Display request 14: replies (type 4, 4 bytes) with the vertical phase last stored for the channel: the shared word at offset 0x36cc of the display-generator state for channel 1, otherwise the word at the channel record's offset 0x168. Reads only firmware state. |
| `dgResolveXMapChannelFormat` | 0x0c011c14 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11c14). Rewrites one channel's XMAP set-up by sending two display-bus writes through the engine's bus-write handler: the record's first word to register 0x118, then a format word to 0x11d. In that word bits 3-0 are the channel's gamma-map index (record offset 0x170), bit 4 comes from a board-configuration word, bit 5 is bit 25 of the record's control word, and bit 6 is set when record offset 0xac holds 5. |
| `DoDGRqstDVPMult2Freq` | 0x0c011cc0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11cc0). Display request 90: sets bit 7 of the DVP option board's frequency-multiply register to bit 0 of payload word 0, by read-modify-write with short delay loops. The register is at display-bus address 0x3400 when the board-type word at the start of zero-initialised data is 0, and at 0xcd00 otherwise. Returns nothing and sends no reply. |
| `DoDGRqstVBOBCommand` | 0x0c011d8c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11d8c). Display request 91: sends a command packet to the VBOB option board over the serial bus at display-bus address 0xa000. After a start to slave 0xee it sends payload word 0 plus 2 as a length, a zero byte and payload word 1, then payload word 0 bytes from word 2 on, and a stop. Also called internally by the horizontal and vertical phase code for channel 0x5e. |
| `DoDGRqstGetVBOBReply` | 0x0c011e0c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11e0c). Display request 92: reads a reply packet from the VBOB option board on the serial bus at 0xa000, using slave address 0xef. It reads two status bytes, treats 0 or 0xef as no reply, otherwise takes the first byte as the length (at most 0x28) and reads that many bytes, ending with a negative acknowledge. It sends the length and bytes back as a type 0x1f reply of 0xa4 bytes; a read error gives length 0. |
| `DoDGRqstChannelGammaMap` | 0x0c011f30 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11f30). Display request 15: with payload word 1 equal to -2 it sets bit 25 of the channel record's control word (no gamma map); any other value clears the bit and stores the map index at record offset 0x170. It then tail-calls the XMAP channel-format routine so display-bus register 0x11d reflects the change. |
| `DoDGRqstGetChannelGammaMap` | 0x0c011f98 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11f98). Display request 16: replies (type 5, 4 bytes) with the gamma-map index stored at the channel record's offset 0x170 by the gamma-map request. Reads only firmware state; no hardware access. |
| `DoDGRqstChannelGammaMapState` | 0x0c011fd4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x11fd4). Display request 75: records a gamma-map state. For channel -1 it sets bit 29 of the shared state word at offset 0x36c8 from bit 0 of payload word 1 and copies payload words 2-4 to offset 0x36d4. For channel ids 0-7 and 0x1e-0xfd it sets bit 25 of the record's control word from that bit and copies the three words to record offset 0x174; other ids return. Firmware state only. |
| `DoDGRqstGetChannelGammaMapState` | 0x0c01207c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1207c). Display request 76: replies (type 0x18, 16 bytes) with a flag word followed by the three state words stored by the gamma-map-state request, read from the shared slot for channel -1 or from the channel record at offset 0x174; an invalid channel id sends a zero flag. Firmware state only. |
| `DoDGRqstFastlistChunksize` | 0x0c012138 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12138). Display request 59: writes 0x320 or-ed with the low five bits of payload word 0 to display-bus register 0x10f, the XMAP's fast-list chunk-size register, with a short delay loop around the write. Returns nothing and sends no reply. |
| `dgGetWriteVoc` | 0x0c012190 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12190). Maps a channel id to the video-output chip that is actually written for it: each of five option-board primary ids (0x1e maps to 0x20, for one) goes to a fixed partner id, and every other id returns unchanged. Pure arithmetic, called before register writes to option-board channels; no hardware access. |
| `DoDGRqstTest` | 0x0c0121d4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x121d4). Display request 0: a loop-back test. It reads payload word 0, adds 1 and sends it back as a one-word reply of type 0. Touches no hardware, so the host can check the command path and its checksum. |
| `DoDGRqstVCWrite` | 0x0c012200 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12200). Display request 1: writes payload word 1 to the display-bus word whose index is payload word 0, i.e. the ARM address 0x600000 plus four times the index, with short delay loops before and after. It checks nothing, so the host can reach any display-board register; no reply. |
| `DoDGRqstVCRead` | 0x0c012250 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12250). Handler for display-generator request 2, run by the video-command token 0x1a6. It takes the one payload word as a display-bus address, loads the word at 0x00600000 plus four times that address, and sends it to the host as a four-byte reply with reply code 1. |
| `DoDGRqstLoadXilinx` | 0x0c012280 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12280). Handler for display-generator request 82 (six payload words), which loads programmable logic on an option board. Word 0 must be 0, else it prints an illegal-type message. Word 1 is the byte count: -1 writes 1 to the load-control register at 0x00622400, sleeps for a fixed delay count, and succeeds; any other negative value prints an unrecognised-command message; above 16 prints an illegal-packet-size message. Otherwise it writes each byte of words 2-5 to the data register at 0x00622808 and polls bit 7 for up to 999 tries, printing a timeout message on failure. It replies with code 0x1b and a status word that is 1 only on success. |
| `DoDGRqstLoadPabXilinx` | 0x0c01249c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1249c). Handler for display-generator request 53 (1328 payload words). It loads the programmable logic on the display board serially: it runs the clear routine, then shifts out six header bytes and the frame data least-significant bit first through `databit`, running the per-frame check after each frame, then shifts the remaining bytes and finishes with `chkdone`. Word 0 is the byte count, word 1 the frame count, word 2 the bits per frame, and the data starts at word 3. It prints a failure message if a check fails, and returns without sending a reply. |
| `pgmclr` | 0x0c012644 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12644). Starts the serial load of the display-board programmable logic: after a short delay loop it writes 1 to the register at 0x00609000 (display-bus address 0x2400), reads it back, then spins a counter of 100000 down to zero. It never writes 0 to that register itself. Called only by the display-board loader. |
| `databit` | 0x0c0126c8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x126c8). Clocks one bit into the display-board programmable logic: after a short delay loop it writes the lowest bit of the argument to the register at 0x00609400 (display-bus address 0x2500), then runs a second delay loop. Called once per bit by the display-board loader. |
| `waitclr` | 0x0c01271c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1271c). Stub that returns 0 at once; despite the name it waits for nothing and reads no register. The display-board loader treats the 0 as success. |
| `chkframe` | 0x0c012724 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12724). Stub that returns 0 at once; it reads no register, so the display-board loader's per-frame check always passes. |
| `chkdone` | 0x0c01272c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1272c). Reads the register at 0x00609800 (display-bus address 0x2600) and returns 0 when bit 0 is set (programmable logic loaded) and 1 when it is clear. The display-board loader prints a failure message on 1. |
| `DoDGRqstLoadPabSizeRegs` | 0x0c01274c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1274c). Handler for display-generator request 54 (four payload words). It writes the four words in order to the display-board registers at 0x00608c00-0x00608c0c (display-bus addresses 0x2300-0x2303), with short delay loops around each write. It sends no reply. |
| `DoDGRqstLoadPabCtrlRegs` | 0x0c01286c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1286c). Handler for display-generator request 55 (two payload words, of which only word 0 is used). It writes word 0 to the display-board register at 0x00608c10 (display-bus address 0x2304) between short delay loops. It sends no reply. |
| `pabSetupRegisters` | 0x0c0128c0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x128c0). Writes the display-board registers at 0x00608c00-0x00608c10 from the current video format: zero to the two registers at 0x00608c08-0x00608c0c, one size field minus 1 to 0x00608c00, half of a second size field minus 1 to 0x00608c04, and 0xc to 0x00608c10 when a third field equals 0x1e, else 4. The three fields sit inside the structure that the display-generator block at 0x0406c654 points to through its word at offset 0x30c. Nothing in the image calls it: no branch or literal-pool word refers to it. |
| `arm_video_source` | 0x0c013924 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x13924). Token handler 0x114 for the video source. It reads one payload word and tail-calls the video-format setter with it; the handler does nothing else. |
| `arm_dispmode` | 0x0c013dd4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x13dd4). Token handler 0x1e6 for the display mode. After the privilege check it reads three payload words; when the first is non-zero it calls `dgUpdateFMDidSwapTable` with the first two. It then performs a display-bus register write: the second word goes to port 0x474, the third to port 0x840, and the display-bus write handler address to port 0x8c0. |
| `arm_swap_cntrl` | 0x0c013e50 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x13e50). Token handler 0x1db for swap control. After the privilege check it writes the payload word to display-bus register 0x80 through the display-bus write handler. If bit 0 of the word is set, it also writes 1 to display-bus register 0x1920, or to register 0x8001 when the flag word at 0x04067edc is non-zero. |
| `arm_dplex_swap` | 0x0c013ef4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x13ef4). Token handler 0x166 for the display-multiplexer swap. After the privilege check it writes the payload word to display-bus register 0xe500 through the display-bus write handler (value to port 0x840, register to port 0x474, handler to port 0x8c0). |
| `arm_retrace_seqid` | 0x0c013f5c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x13f5c). Token handler 0x1e7 for the retrace sequence id. After the privilege check it makes six display-bus writes to register 0x7f, with the values 1 to 6 in order, then writes the payload word to raster register 0x420 through the register-write routine. |
| `DGGlobalState` | 0x04069654 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). The display generator's 0x37e0-byte state record (channels, formats, genlock and option-board state), cleared by `DoDGRqstAutoInit` and read and written by nearly every display-request handler. |
| `sdram_scratch` | 0x0406ce34 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A 128 KB scratch buffer used for flash PROM contents: `eeprom_read` and the write routine move data through it, and the display auto-initialisation checks the stored video combination in it. |

## State shadow: initialisation, push and pop

When a context is created, `context_init` calls one initialisation routine per attribute group to put the GL defaults into the state block and to the hardware; `arm_popattrib` calls one pop routine per group to send restored state back. Errors keep the first code in the two bytes at state-block offset 0x860.

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `hint_init` | 0x0c0164bc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x164bc). Context initialisation. Stores 0x1100 (the don't-care hint value) in all five hint words at state block offsets 0x7ec-0x7fc. Called from the context-initialisation sequence just before `prim_init`. |
| `prim_init` | 0x0c016584 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x16584). Context initialisation of primitive state. Sets fill polygon modes (offsets 0x250-0x254), back-face culling and counter-clockwise front face (0x258-0x25c) and sends the latter two to the engines. Sets point size and line width to 1.0, line stipple to all ones with repeat 1, render mode to render, re-derives polygon, line and point drawing, sends defaults for colour, index, normal, texture coordinate and edge flag, and writes eight default raster registers through descriptor 0x58404800. |

## Enable and disable

`arm_enable` and `arm_disable` take the capability number from the token's last payload word, check six ranges and jump through one pointer table per range; a number outside every range, or one whose slot holds `set_invalid_enum`, raises the GL invalid-enum error. Every routine except the multipass and unimplemented pairs first stores 1 (enable) or 0 (disable) in its byte of the enable block at state-block offsets 0x758-0x796, then does its work, so the block is the firmware's copy of the enable state; `enable_pop` walks the block and calls the matching routine for each byte. GL names in the entries are the public enum names for the capability numbers.

| Table (enable, disable) | Address (enable, disable) | Capability range |
|---|---|---|
| `e0`, `d0` | 0x04046fe8, 0x04048258 | 0x0b10-0x0de1 |
| `e0a`, `d0a` | 0x04047b30, 0x04048da0 | 0x2a01-0x2a02 |
| `e1`, `d1` | 0x04047b38, 0x04048da8 | 0x3000-0x3005 |
| `e2`, `d2` | 0x04047b50, 0x04048dc0 | 0x4000-0x4007 |
| `e3`, `d3` | 0x04047b70, 0x04048de0 | 0x8010-0x81c7 |
| `e4`, `d4` | 0x04048254, 0x040494c4 | 0x6000 only |

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `e_alpha_test` | 0x0c012e94 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12e94). Enables alpha test: stores 1 in the flag byte at state-block offset 0x758, sends 1 to port 0x474 and the engine handler address from the high half of the word at 0x100 to port 0x8c0, then returns. Also called when attributes are popped. |
| `d_alpha_test` | 0x0c012eb0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12eb0). Disables alpha test: stores 0 in the flag byte at state-block offset 0x758, sends 0 to port 0x474 and the same engine handler address (high half of the word at 0x100) to port 0x8c0, then returns. Also called when attributes are popped. |
| `e_dither` | 0x0c012ecc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12ecc). Enables dithering by storing 1 in the flag byte at state-block offset 0x75e. It writes no hardware register; the flag is read later when the colour mask and blend state are rebuilt. Also called when attributes are popped. |
| `d_dither` | 0x0c012ed8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12ed8). Disables dithering by storing 0 in the flag byte at state-block offset 0x75e. It writes no hardware register; the flag is read later when the colour mask and blend state are rebuilt. Also called when attributes are popped. |
| `e_depth_test` | 0x0c014858 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x14858). Enables the depth test: stores 1 in the flag byte at state-block offset 0x75d and, only when a depth buffer exists, writes 0x200 with mask 0x200 to raster address word 0x01391c08 through descriptor 0x584fc800. Also used when attributes are popped. |
| `d_depth_test` | 0x0c01488c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1488c). Disables the depth test: stores 0 in the flag byte at state-block offset 0x75d and writes 0 with mask 0x200 to raster address word 0x01391c08 through descriptor 0x584fc800, whether or not a depth buffer exists. |
| `e_stencil_test` | 0x0c014a40 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x14a40). Enables the stencil test: stores 1 in the flag byte at state-block offset 0x76d and, only when a stencil buffer exists, writes 1 with mask 1 to raster address word 0x01381c07 through descriptor 0x584fc800. Also used when attributes are popped. |
| `d_stencil_test` | 0x0c014a70 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x14a70). Disables the stencil test: stores 0 in the flag byte at state-block offset 0x76d and writes 0 with mask 1 to raster address word 0x01381c07 through descriptor 0x584fc800, whether or not a stencil buffer exists. |
| `e_cull_face` | 0x0c015564 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15564). Enable half of the face-culling pair. Sets the cull-face enable byte at state block offset 0x75c to 1 and starts the engine handler `mgeb_Enable_Cull_Face` through handler port 0x4f8. Also replayed when a popped attribute set restores the enable. |
| `d_cull_face` | 0x0c01557c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1557c). Disable half of the face-culling pair. Clears the cull-face enable byte at state block offset 0x75c and starts the engine handler `mgeb_Disable_Cull_Face` through handler port 0x4f8. |
| `e_point_smooth` | 0x0c0155f4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x155f4). Enable half of point smoothing. Sets the point-smooth byte at state block offset 0x766 to 1 and jumps to `ResolvePoint` to re-derive how points are sent. |
| `d_point_smooth` | 0x0c015600 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15600). Disable half of point smoothing. Clears the point-smooth byte at state block offset 0x766 and jumps to `ResolvePoint` to re-derive how points are sent. |
| `e_line_smooth` | 0x0c01560c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1560c). Enable half of line smoothing. Sets the line-smooth byte at state block offset 0x761 to 1, calls `ResolveLine`, then jumps to `ResolvePolygon` because polygons drawn in line mode depend on line smoothing. |
| `d_line_smooth` | 0x0c01562c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1562c). Disable half of line smoothing. Clears the line-smooth byte at state block offset 0x761, re-derives line and polygon drawing, and when polygon stippling is on (byte 0x768) jumps to `downLoadPolygonStipple` to reload the stipple pattern memory. |
| `e_polygon_smooth` | 0x0c01565c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1565c). Enable half of polygon smoothing. Sets the polygon-smooth byte at state block offset 0x767 to 1 and jumps to `ResolvePolygon` to re-derive how polygons are sent. |
| `d_polygon_smooth` | 0x0c015668 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15668). Disable half of polygon smoothing. Clears the polygon-smooth byte at state block offset 0x767 and jumps to `ResolvePolygon` to re-derive how polygons are sent. |
| `e_multisample` | 0x0c015674 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15674). Enable half of multisampling. Sets the multisample byte at state block offset 0x783 to 1. When the multisample-capable byte at offset 0x1d1 is set it re-derives polygon, line and point drawing, writes raster register 0x012b0806 through descriptor 0x58404800, then writes register 0x01681408 with bit 0 from the alpha-to-mask byte and bit 2 from the alpha-to-one byte. |
| `d_multisample` | 0x0c015704 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15704). Disable half of multisampling. Clears the multisample byte at state block offset 0x783. When the multisample-capable byte at offset 0x1d1 is set it re-derives polygon, line and point drawing and writes zero to raster registers 0x012b0806 and 0x01681408 through descriptor 0x58404800. |
| `e_blend` | 0x0c0158f8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x158f8). Enable half of blending. Sets the blend byte at state block offset 0x75a to 1 and bit 0x10 of the mode byte at 0xcf0. Unless the byte at 0x1ce is positive or mode bit 0x20 is set, it writes raster register 0x013c1c0d through descriptor 0x584fc800 and, when the byte at 0x207 is set, register 0x01361c03 too. |
| `d_blend` | 0x0c015984 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15984). Disable half of blending. Clears the blend byte at state block offset 0x75a and bit 0x10 of the mode byte at 0xcf0. Unless the byte at 0x1ce is positive or mode bit 0x20 is set, it writes zero to raster register 0x013c1c0d through descriptor 0x584fc800 and, when the byte at 0x207 is set and mode bit 0x2 is clear, register 0x01361c03 as well. |
| `e_reference_plane` | 0x0c015a28 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15a28). Enable half of the reference-plane extension. Sets the byte at state block offset 0x78d to 1, selects engine 0 through port 0x43c and starts the engine handler `mgebi_ReferencePlane_Enable` (low half of the packed handler word at offset 0x130). |
| `d_reference_plane` | 0x0c015a48 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15a48). Disable half of the reference-plane extension. Clears the byte at state block offset 0x78d, selects engine 0 through port 0x43c and starts the engine handler `mgebi_ReferencePlane_Disable` (high half of the packed handler word at offset 0x134). |
| `e_calligraphic_fragment` | 0x0c015a64 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15a64). Enable half of the calligraphic-fragment extension. Sets the byte at state block offset 0x78e to 1 and writes 0x1000000 to raster register 0x01351c02 through descriptor 0x584fc800. |
| `d_calligraphic_fragment` | 0x0c015a90 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15a90). Disable half of the calligraphic-fragment extension. Clears the byte at state block offset 0x78e and writes zero to raster register 0x01351c02 through descriptor 0x584fc800. |
| `e_color_logic_op` | 0x0c015ab8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15ab8). Enable half of colour logic operations. Sets the byte at state block offset 0x764 to 1. Unless the byte at 0x1ce is positive it sets bit 0x10 of the mode byte at 0xcf0 and, if mode bit 0x20 is clear, writes 1 to raster register 0x013c1c0d and (when the byte at 0x207 is set) 0x2000 to register 0x01361c03 through descriptor 0x584fc800. |
| `d_color_logic_op` | 0x0c015b2c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15b2c). Disable half of colour logic operations. Clears the byte at state block offset 0x764. Unless the byte at 0x1ce is positive it clears bit 0x10 of the mode byte at 0xcf0, rewrites the two raster registers it shares with blending to their off values, then re-applies blending by jumping to `e_blend` when the blend byte at 0x75a is set. |
| `e_logic_op` | 0x0c015bb4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15bb4). Enable half of index logic operations. Sets the byte at state block offset 0x763 to 1 and, unless the byte at 0x1cd is positive, writes 1 to raster register 0x013c1c0d through descriptor 0x584fc800. |
| `d_logic_op` | 0x0c015be4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15be4). Disable half of index logic operations. Clears the byte at state block offset 0x763 and, unless mode bit 0x20 of the byte at 0xcf0 is set, writes zero to raster register 0x013c1c0d through descriptor 0x584fc800. |
| `e_normalize` | 0x0c015c44 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15c44). Enable half of normal renormalisation. Sets the byte at state block offset 0x765 to 1 and starts the engine handler `mgeb_Enable_Normalize` (high half of the packed handler word at offset 0x5c) through handler port 0x4f8. |
| `d_normalize` | 0x0c015c60 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15c60). Disable half of normal renormalisation. Clears the byte at state block offset 0x765 and starts the engine handler `mgeb_Disable_Normalize` (low half of the packed handler word at offset 0x54) through handler port 0x4f8. |
| `e_line_stipple` | 0x0c015de8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15de8). Enable half of line stippling. Sets the byte at state block offset 0x762 to 1, writes 1 to descriptor 0x49000020 and jumps to `ResolveLine` to re-derive how lines are sent. |
| `d_line_stipple` | 0x0c015e04 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15e04). Disable half of line stippling. Clears the byte at state block offset 0x762, writes zero to descriptor 0x49000020 and jumps to `ResolveLine` to re-derive how lines are sent. |
| `e_polygon_stipple` | 0x0c015e20 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15e20). Enable half of polygon stippling. Sets the byte at state block offset 0x768 to 1, re-derives line and polygon drawing and, when line smoothing is off, jumps to `downLoadPolygonStipple` to load the stipple pattern memory. |
| `d_polygon_stipple` | 0x0c015e50 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15e50). Disable half of polygon stippling. Clears the byte at state block offset 0x768, sends the line width and the line hint to the engines with the antialiased-line-table download handler, then re-derives line and polygon drawing. |
| `e_polygonoffsetpoint` | 0x0c016308 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x16308). Enable half of point polygon offset. Sets the byte at state block offset 0x769 to 1, builds a mode word from the fill byte (bit 0), line byte (bit 1) and point (bit 2, forced on), and sends it through port 0x478 to `mgeb_Disable_EnablePolyOffsetExt`. |
| `d_polygonoffsetpoint` | 0x0c016334 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x16334). Disable half of point polygon offset. Clears the byte at state block offset 0x769, builds a mode word from the fill byte (bit 0) and line byte (bit 1) and sends it through port 0x478 to `mgeb_Disable_EnablePolyOffsetExt`. |
| `e_polygonoffsetline` | 0x0c01635c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1635c). Enable half of line polygon offset. Sets the byte at state block offset 0x76a to 1, builds a mode word from the fill byte (bit 0), line (bit 1, forced on) and point byte (bit 2), and sends it through port 0x478 to `mgeb_Disable_EnablePolyOffsetExt`. |
| `d_polygonoffsetline` | 0x0c016388 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x16388). Disable half of line polygon offset. Clears the byte at state block offset 0x76a, builds a mode word from the fill byte (bit 0) and point byte (bit 2) and sends it through port 0x478 to `mgeb_Disable_EnablePolyOffsetExt`. |
| `e_polygonoffset` | 0x0c0163b0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x163b0). Enable half of fill polygon offset. Sets the byte at state block offset 0x76b to 1, builds a mode word from fill (bit 0, forced on), the line byte (bit 1) and the point byte (bit 2), sends it through port 0x478 to `mgeb_Disable_EnablePolyOffsetExt`, then jumps to `ResolvePolygon`. |
| `d_polygonoffset` | 0x0c0163dc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x163dc). Disable half of fill polygon offset. Clears the byte at state block offset 0x76b, builds a mode word from the line byte (bit 1) and the point byte (bit 2), sends it through port 0x478 to `mgeb_Disable_EnablePolyOffsetExt`, then jumps to `ResolvePolygon`. |
| `e_multipass` | 0x0c016448 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x16448). Enable half of the multipass mode. Keeps no byte in the state block; starts the engine handler `mgeb_Enable_Multipass` through handler port 0x4f8. |
| `d_multipass` | 0x0c016458 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x16458). Disable half of the multipass mode. Keeps no byte in the state block; starts the engine handler `mgeb_Disable_Multipass` through handler port 0x4f8. |
| `e_lighting` | 0x0c0174ec | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x174ec). Enable handler for lighting (target 0xb50): sets the lighting flag byte at state-block offset 0x760, sends the enable-lighting engine handler to port 0x4f8 (all engines), then jumps to `XferMask` to recompute the distributor transfer mask. |
| `e_colormaterial` | 0x0c017504 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x17504). Enable handler for colour material (target 0xb57): sets the flag byte at state-block offset 0x75b, sends the enable-colour-material engine handler to port 0x4f8 (all engines), then jumps to `XferMask` to recompute the distributor transfer mask. |
| `e_light0` | 0x0c01751c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1751c). | `arm_enable` | 0x0c023380 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23380). Token 0x24: reads a GL capability code from the last-payload port and jumps through one of six tables of enable routines chosen by range. Codes 0xde0-0xde1 use the first table. Codes 0x2a01-0x2a02 use the second. Codes 0x3000-0x3005 use the third table, 0x4000-0x4007 the fourth, 0x81c4-0x81c7 the fifth, and code 0x6000 alone the last. A code outside every range calls the invalid-enumerant error routine instead. |
| `arm_disable` | 0x0c023448 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23448). Token 0x25: the disable twin of the enable dispatcher. It reads a capability code from the last-payload port and jumps through one of six tables of disable routines chosen by the same ranges: 0xde0-0xde1 first, 0x2a01-0x2a02 second, then 0x3000-0x3005 third, 0x4000-0x4007 fourth, 0x81c4-0x81c7 fifth, and code 0x6000 alone last. Any other code calls the invalid-enumerant error routine. |
| `e_unimp` | 0x0c023510 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23510). Filler entry in the enable tables for capabilities the ARM does not implement: it prints the message "unimplemented enable called" and returns through the print routine; it changes no state. |
| `d_unimp` | 0x0c023538 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23538). Filler entry in the disable tables for capabilities the ARM does not implement: it prints "unimplemented disable called" and returns through the print routine; it changes no state. |
| `enable_pop` | 0x0c023560 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23560). Used by the attribute-pop token: for each saved capability flag, a byte at state-block offsets 0x758-0x796, it calls the matching enable routine when the byte is set and the disable routine otherwise (alpha test, blend, colour material, cull face, depth test, dither, fog, lighting, line smooth and stipple, logic op, clip planes, multisample, convolutions, sample mask, histogram, min-max and others), so the hardware matches the restored flags. |
| `e_texture_1d` | 0x0c024c60 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24c60). Turns on 1D texturing: sets the 1D enable byte at state-block offset 0x76e, writes all ones to engine port 0x47c, sends the texture handler held in the upper half of the state-block word at 0x110 and branches to the transfer-mask routine. |
| `d_texture_1d` | 0x0c024c80 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24c80). Turns off 1D texturing: clears the 1D enable byte at state-block offset 0x76e; only when the other two dimension bytes (0x76f-0x770) are also clear does it write zero to engine port 0x47c, send the texture handler held in the upper half of the state-block word at 0x110 and branch to the transfer-mask routine; otherwise it returns. |
| `e_texture_2d` | 0x0c024cb0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24cb0). Turns on 2D texturing: sets the 2D enable byte at state-block offset 0x76f, writes all ones to engine port 0x47c, sends the texture handler held in the upper half of the state-block word at 0x110 and branches to the transfer-mask routine. |
| `d_texture_2d` | 0x0c024cd0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24cd0). Turns off 2D texturing: clears the 2D enable byte at state-block offset 0x76f; only when the other two dimension bytes (0x76e and 0x770) are also clear does it write zero to engine port 0x47c, send the texture handler held in the upper half of the state-block word at 0x110 and branch to the transfer-mask routine; otherwise it returns. |
| `e_texture_3d` | 0x0c024d00 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24d00). Turns on 3D texturing: sets the 3D enable byte at state-block offset 0x770, writes all ones to engine port 0x47c, sends the texture handler held in the upper half of the state-block word at 0x110 and branches to the transfer-mask routine. |
| `d_texture_3d` | 0x0c024d20 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24d20). Turns off 3D texturing: clears the 3D enable byte at state-block offset 0x770; only when the other two dimension bytes (0x76e-0x76f) are also clear does it write zero to engine port 0x47c, send the texture handler held in the upper half of the state-block word at 0x110 and branch to the transfer-mask routine; otherwise it returns. |
| `e_convolution_1d` | 0x0c036b1c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x36b1c). Enable handler for one-dimensional convolution (token 0x8010 in the enable table). Sets the state-block byte at offset 0x784, and only if pixel transform is enabled (byte 0x796) and the transform-mode word at offset 0x36c is 0x81c5, ORs bit 0x20 into the pixel-path flag word at offset 0xc88. Touches no hardware. |
| `d_convolution_1d` | 0x0c036b4c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x36b4c). Disable handler for one-dimensional convolution. Clears the state-block byte at offset 0x784 and clears bit 0x20 of the pixel-path flag word at offset 0xc88, whether or not pixel transform is on. Touches no hardware. |
| `e_convolution_2d` | 0x0c036b64 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x36b64). Enable handler for two-dimensional convolution. Sets the state-block byte at offset 0x785; if pixel transform is enabled and the transform-mode word at offset 0x36c is 0x81c5, ORs bit 0x40 into the pixel-path flag word at offset 0xc88.
