Copy-pixels form whose source is the video input, called by `arm_copypixels` when the video-source flag at pixel state +0x348 is set. Writes distribution mode 0 through descriptor 0x44000020, sets the operation word to 0x50 and the transfer flags to 0x40000, resolves the video path, runs the raster set-up, split, distributor and engine slots, starts the video bus transfer, runs the finish slot and stores 0x7fffffff at pixel state +0xe8. |
| `SetVideoFormat` | 0x0c02bdfc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2bdfc). Records the video source format in the per-context state block at +0x6d0 (fields +0x0-0x34) from the argument: the first two formats share one set of constants, and the third and fourth each pick another. When the field at +0x18 equals one it first reprograms the distributor for a video board. Called by `arm_video_source` and `arm_slop`. |
| `e_interlace` | 0x0c02bec8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2bec8). Sets the one-byte interlace flag at state block +0x78f to 1 and writes the one-word value 1 to engine external memory address 0xc03 through the engine external memory writer. Called from `enable_pop`. It switches interlaced video-copy behaviour on in the engines. |
| `d_interlace` | 0x0c02bef8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2bef8). Clears the interlace flag byte at state block +0x78f and writes the word 0 to engine external memory address 0xc03, the counterpart of `e_interlace`. Called from `enable_pop`. |
| `EnableVideoCopy` | 0x0c02bf28 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2bf28). Leaf routine, called from `arm_slop`: stores its argument at pixel state +0x348, the flag that makes the copy, copy-texture and read-pixels handlers use their video-source forms. Touches no hardware. |
| `EnableInterlace` | 0x0c02bf34 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2bf34). Called from `arm_slop`: stores the low byte of its argument at state block +0x78f and writes that value as one word to engine external memory address 0xc03, so the engines' interlace flag follows the argument. |
| `videoCopyTexImage` | 0x0c02c0f8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2c0f8). Copy-to-texture form whose source is the video input, reached by a tail call from `arm_copyteximage`. Writes distribution mode 0 through descriptor 0x44000020, sets the operation word to 0x90 and the transfer flags to 0x40000, resolves the video path, sets up the sub-texture load at position 0, runs the engine slots, applies the size workaround on four of the paths, starts the video bus transfer and jumps to the path's finish slot. |
| `videoReadPixels` | 0x0c02c6d4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2c6d4). Read-pixels form whose source is the video input, called by `arm_readpixels` when the video flag is set. Writes distribution mode 0 through descriptor 0x44000020, sets the operation word to 0x30, sends an engine operand when the select flag at state block +0x890 is set, parses the host image, resolves the video path, runs the packer and engine slots, writes raster register 0x011d3fcc through descriptor 0x58404800, sends the pixels to the host and runs the finish slot. |

## Pixel path: X server pixel commands

The X server's pixel read, write and copy commands and the X pixel planners.

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `cvm_spec_8` | 0x0404d970 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Eight converter words (0x02083800 down to 0x42080000, bit 30 marking the last) for 8-bit X pixels, reached through the 8-bit converter group by `X_PixSetupGE`; `CompGedReg` loads its address only as a base for the register-list count at +0x15c. |
| `cvm_spec_16` | 0x0404d990 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Eight converter words for 16-bit X pixels (0x02103000 ... 0x42100000), reached only through the 16-bit converter group that `X_PixSetupGE` picks. |
| `cvm_spec_32` | 0x0404d9b0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Eight converter words for 32-bit X pixels, alternating between 0x02202000 and the last-word form 0x42200000, reached only through the 32-bit converter group that the X pixel set-up picks. |
| `cvm_spec_48` | 0x0404d9d0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Eight converter words for 48-bit X pixels (0x020c3400 ... 0x420c1000), reached only through the 48-bit converter group of `X_PixSetupGE`. |
| `cvm_spec_rgb10_sw` | 0x0404d9f0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Eight converter words for 10-bit-per-component RGB X pixels written to the framebuffer, reached only through their converter group in `X_PixSetupGE`. |
| `cvm_spec_rgb10_sr` | 0x0404da10 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Eight converter words for 10-bit-per-component RGB X pixels read back, the field order of the write set reversed; reached through `X_PixSetupGE`. |
| `cvm_spec_tbm` | 0x0404da30 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Eight converter words (0x02013f00 ... 0x42010f00) for X bitmap transfers, reached only through their converter group in `X_PixSetupGE`. |
| `cvmg_8` | 0x0404da50 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Converter group for 8-bit X pixels: a count of 8 and a pointer to its converter words; `X_PixSetupGE` chooses it by pixel depth. |
| `cvmg_16` | 0x0404da58 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Converter group for 16-bit X pixels: a count of 8 and a pointer to its words; `X_PixSetupGE` uses it with the small tag table for depth 1. |
| `cvmg_32` | 0x0404da60 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Converter group for 32-bit X pixels: count 8 and a pointer to its words; `X_PixSetupGE` uses it with the big tag table for depth 2. |
| `cvmg_48` | 0x0404da68 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Converter group for 48-bit X pixels: count 8 and a pointer to its words, chosen by `X_PixSetupGE`. |
| `cvmg_rgb10_sw` | 0x0404da70 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Converter group for 10-bit RGB X pixel writes: count 8 and a pointer to its words, chosen by `X_PixSetupGE`. |
| `cvmg_rgb10_sr` | 0x0404da78 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Converter group for 10-bit RGB X pixel reads: count 8 and a pointer to its words, chosen by `X_PixSetupGE`. |
| `cvmg_tbm` | 0x0404da80 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Converter group for X bitmap transfers: count 8 and a pointer to its words, chosen by `X_PixSetupGE`. |
| `ptagBig` | 0x0404da88 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Four tag words alternating between 0x30006 and the plain form 0x30000 that `X_PixSetupGE` pairs with the 32-bit converter group for wide X pixels. A second table of the same name for the GL output modules sits at 0x0404c038. |
| `ptagSmall` | 0x0404da98 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Four tag words, 0x30006 in the second and 0x30000 elsewhere, that `X_PixSetupGE` pairs with the 16-bit converter group; a GL-path table of the same name sits at 0x0404c048. |
| `NumGedRegs` | 0x0404dacc | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). The count of pending entries in the distributor register list `GedRegs`: `CompGedReg` clears it, the formatter, unpacker, reorderer and transfer-unit planners append, and the same routine then writes each entry through a computed descriptor. |
| `tfRegs` | 0x0404dae8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Five words of X-path texture-filter register values, zero in the image: `CompTF` stores a mode (up to 2) and a component mask (up to 0x1f) by pixel kind, and `XSetTF` stores 3 in the third word and applies the mask. |
| `NumRMBoards` | 0x0404dafc | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Second copy of the three words counting up from 0 indexed by raster-board count, in the X pixel object; `XSetupGedRead` reads it when it sets up a framebuffer read. |
| `GedRegs` | 0x040952cc | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Thirteen register-number and value pairs collected by the X pixel planners (formatter, unpacker, reorderer, transfer unit); `CompGedReg` writes each as a distributor register through a descriptor built from the number. |

## Pixel path: output-module records and conversion lists

Fixed records that describe an output module: command words, module routine, tag tables and converter groups.

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `AddOutput` | 0x0c02ffd4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2ffd4). Chooses the last module of a colour chain from `RGBAoutputModuleTable`, indexed by destination format, with special records for floating-point and depth destinations; it runs the record's parameter writer, writes its control words to engine control memory 0x1c4 then 0x166, and adds the record's handler (or its variant) with control word 0x161. It first adds the component-reversal module when requested. |
| `AddOutput1Cmpnt` | 0x0c0302dc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x302dc). Chooses the last module of a one-component chain from `OneCmpntoutputModuleTable`, indexed by destination format, and uses the four-component 32-bit float record instead when the transfer flags ask for floating-point output; it runs the record's parameter writer, writes control words to engine control memory 0x1c4 then 0x166, and adds the handler with control word 0x161. |
| `AddLutOutput` | 0x0c0303c8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x303c8). Appends the colour-table output module: it calls `U_uLutOutput` to write eight words to engine control memory 0x161, then adds the handler for `mmo_Lutout` with that address to the module list passed as the second argument. Only the colour-table path builder calls it. |
| `AddConvolutionOutput` | 0x0c030414 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x30414). Appends the convolution output module: it sends `mgeb_EramFill` to zero the kernel's external-memory area, calls the convolution-output parameter writer, writes the value 1.0 into extra external-memory words for formats that need pass-through components, and adds the handler for `mmo_ConvolveOut` with control word 0 to the module list passed as the second argument. |
| `AddPolygonStippleOutput` | 0x0c0305a4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x305a4). Appends the polygon-stipple output module: it calls `U_uPolygonStippleOutput`, then adds the handler for `mmo_PolygonStippleOut` with control word 0 to the module list passed as the second argument. Only the polygon-stipple path builder calls it. |
| `U_uLutOutput` | 0x0c0315c8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x315c8). Writes eight words to engine control memory 0x161 for the colour-table output module: transfer words, two table words from the destination record, a float limit (0x477ffeff, or 0x457fefff for format 7), a width of 16 (or 12 for format 7) and the handler of the four-component, luminance-alpha or intensity output variant. `AddLutOutput` is its only caller. |
| `U_uConvolutionOutput` | 0x0c031800 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x31800). Writes five words to engine control memory 0x161 for the convolution output module: the destination offset times four, the kernel size in words minus that offset, the external-memory address argument and a conversion word. `AddConvolutionOutput` is its only caller. |
| `U_uPolygonStippleOutput` | 0x0c031854 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x31854). Writes two words to engine control memory 0x161 for the polygon-stipple output module: word 0x30 of the transfer record and the constant 0x26b. `AddPolygonStippleOutput` is its only caller. |
| `U_mPixout_8_16` | 0x0c03188c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x3188c). Parameter writer of the eight- and sixteen-bit output records: when bit 0x80 of the state-block word at 0x908 is set it first sends the last engine's state with `U_loadLastGEState`; then it writes five words (offset, pixel width shifted right by five and its remainder, 0x1c4, a format word) to engine control memory 0x161 and copies them to the staging area. Reached through the record table. |
| `U_mPixout_RGB_in_RGBA` | 0x0c031990 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x31990). Parameter writer of the output record that stores three-component colour inside four-component pixels: writes five words (offset, pixel width shifted right by four and its remainder, 0x1c4, and a format word that depends on bit 2 of word 0x20) to engine control memory 0x161. Reached through the record table. |
| `U_mPixout_2_4` | 0x0c031a14 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x31a14). Parameter writer of the two- and four-bit output records: when bit 0x80 of the state-block word at 0x908 is set it first sends the last engine's state with `U_loadLastGEState`; then it writes five words (offset, pixel width shifted right by three and its remainder, 0x1c4, a format word) to engine control memory 0x161 and copies them to the staging area. |
| `U_mPixout_32` | 0x0c031b04 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x31b04). Parameter writer of the 32-bit output records: writes five words (offset, pixel width shifted right by six plus one when a remainder exists, the remainder modulo 64, 0x1c4, a format word) to engine control memory 0x161. It sends no last-engine state. Reached through the record table. |
| `U_mPixoutI_4_8` | 0x0c031b6c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x31b6c). Parameter writer of the four- and eight-bit colour-index output records: writes five words (word 0x30 of the transfer record, pixel width shifted right by four and its remainder, 0x1c4, and 0) to engine control memory 0x161. Reached through the record table. |

## Pixel path: parameter and format tables

Constant tables the pixel path indexes by format, type or kernel shape.

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `x$constdata` | 0x0c03feb0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x3feb0). Linker mark on a constant area of sixteen words inside the code: four 1.0 floats, four zeros, then the same again. `InitHistogramState` copies the first eight onto its stack as a starting scale of one and bias of zero for the histogram and min-max state. |
| `defaultKernel` | 0x0404d20c | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 100 floats of the default convolution kernel (the first four 1.0, the rest 0), copied into engine memory by `__glkonSetDefaultKernel` when a context starts. |
| `ConvolveEramLoc` | 0x0404d39c | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Three engine external-memory word addresses, 0xc06 then 0xcca then 0xd8e, where the convolution kernels sit; read by `LoadKernel` and the default-kernel and kernel-copy routines. |
| `promotedTotalSize` | 0x0404d408 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Twelve words of kernel word counts after promotion to the working size, from 4 up to 0xc4 for square kernels and smaller for separable ones; read by `LoadKernel` and `AddConvolutionOutput`. |
| `saveRestoreCram` | 0x0404d438 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Twelve words giving where in engine control memory a kernel shape saves and restores its working words (word offsets up to 0x92); read by `LoadKernel` and `RestoreKernel`. |
| `cramKernelLoc` | 0x0404d468 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Twelve control-memory addresses (from 0x4e up to 0xe0) where `LoadKernel` places each kernel shape for the engine convolution module. |
| `promotedSize` | 0x0404d498 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Twelve words of promoted kernel widths, the odd sizes from 1 to 7 repeated, that `LoadKernel` and the engine-memory copy routines use to size the convolution working area. |
| `passthruComp` | 0x0404d4c8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Twelve words of component selectors in the range 0-3 that `AddConvolutionOutput` uses to route components past the convolution unchanged. |
| `ExtractShiftMasksL12A4` | 0x0404d4f8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Repeated shift-and-mask quadruples (shift 4 with mask 0xfff, then shift 0 with mask 0xf) that `U_uExtract` uses to split a 16-bit word into 12-bit luminance and 4-bit alpha. |
| `F4thCompTable` | 0x0404d5b8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Per-type fill value for a missing fourth component: the largest value of each signed and unsigned integer type from 0x7f to 0xffffffff, then 1.0 for floats, with 0xdeadbabe in unused slots; read by `SetupFormatter` and `SetupReorderer`. |
| `UnpackerTable` | 0x0404d60c | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 21 per-format unpacker mode numbers, 0 for plain types and small codes up to 7 for packed formats, that `SetupUnpacker` writes to the distributor's unpacker register. |
| `UnpackedTypeTable` | 0x0404d660 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 21 words mapping each host type code to the component type after unpacking (identity for the eight plain types, then 1 for the packed ones); read by `SetupUnpacker`. |
| `UnpackednumByteInCmpTable` | 0x0404d6b4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 21 words of bytes per component after unpacking (one to four) that `SetupUnpacker` uses to set the unpacker's component width. |
| `rByteSel_PASS` | 0x0404d708 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A constant 0x00053053 that no instruction or pool word in the image refers to. **Inferred** from the name and its siblings: the raster-bus byte-select pattern for unswapped data, compiled from a shared definition into two objects and left unused (a second copy sits at 0x0404dad0). |
| `rWordSel_PASS` | 0x0404d70c | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A zero word that no code refers to; **inferred** to be the word-select partner of the pass-through byte select above, unused at run time. |
| `rByteSel_REVERSE_BYTE` | 0x0404d710 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A constant 0x00688688 that no code refers to; **inferred** to be the byte-select pattern for byte-reversed data, compiled in but unused. |
| `rWordSel_REVERSE_BYTE` | 0x0404d714 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A zero word that no code refers to; **inferred** to be the word-select half of the byte-reversal pattern, unused. |
| `rByteSel_REVERSE_SHORT` | 0x0404d718 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A constant 0x00b64b64 that no code refers to; **inferred** to be the byte-select pattern for 16-bit swapped data, unused at run time. |
| `rWordSel_REVERSE_SHORT` | 0x0404d71c | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A zero word that no code refers to; **inferred** to be the word-select half of the 16-bit swap pattern, unused. |
| `rByteSel_REVERSE_WORD` | 0x0404d720 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A constant 0x00b64b64 that no code refers to; **inferred** to be the byte-select pattern for 32-bit reversal, the same value as the 16-bit case, unused. |
| `rWordSel_REVERSE_WORD` | 0x0404d724 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A constant 0x00016349 that no code refers to; **inferred** to be the word-select pattern that swaps the halves for 32-bit reversal, unused. |
| `rByteSel_SWAP_16B_COMP` | 0x0404d728 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A constant 0x004c14c1 that no code refers to; **inferred** to be a byte-select pattern for swapping 16-bit components, unused at run time. |
| `NumRMBoards` | 0x0404d72c | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Three words counting up from 0, indexed by raster-board count; `SetupRbus` and its FFT variant read this copy to encode the board count into the raster-bus set-up. The X-path copy sits at 0x0404dafc. |
| `BgnLineOffsetTable` | 0x0404d738 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 64 words of starting component offsets per format and alignment (0-3 pattern) that `SetupFormatter` uses to place the first component of a line. |
| `fbRbusIs16Bit` | 0x0404d838 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Sixteen words, 2 for the first seven framebuffer formats and 0 after, that `SetupPTUStream` and `SetupPTULine` use to choose a 16-bit raster-bus packing for framebuffer data. |
| `tmRbusIs16Bit` | 0x0404d878 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Ten words, each zero, two or four, giving the raster-bus packing for each texture-memory format, read by `SetupPTUStream` and `SetupPTULine`. |
| `ifmt` | 0x0404d8a0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 26 words translating the pixel path's internal format codes to raster-board format numbers, with -1 for none; read by `SetupPGTG` and `SetupSubtexload`. |
| `numPixelPerTBUSPacket` | 0x0404d908 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 26 words of pixels per raster-bus packet for each format (one to eight); `CompCommonIndexStream` and `CompLineDist`, like the colour and luminance stream planners, divide the work by it. |
| `compFormatTable` | 0x0404daa8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Nine words of components per X pixel format (one, three or four); only `X_PixSetupGE` reads this copy. A GL-path table of the same name sits at 0x0404ce5c. |
| `rByteSel_PASS` | 0x0404dad0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). The second copy of the unswapped byte-select constant 0x00053053, beside the X pixel tables; no code refers to it. |
| `rWordSel_PASS` | 0x0404dad4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Second copy of the zero pass-through word-select constant, in the X pixel object; no code refers to it. |
| `rByteSel_REVERSE_BYTE` | 0x0404dad8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Second copy of the byte-reversal select constant 0x00688688, in the X pixel object; no code refers to it. |
| `rWordSel_REVERSE_BYTE` | 0x0404dadc | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Second copy of the zero word-select constant for byte reversal, in the X pixel object; no code refers to it. |
| `rByteSel_REVERSE_SHORT` | 0x0404dae0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Second copy of the 16-bit swap byte-select constant 0x00b64b64, in the X pixel object; no code refers to it. |
| `rWordSel_REVERSE_SHORT` | 0x0404dae4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Second copy of the zero word-select constant for the 16-bit swap, in the X pixel object; no code refers to it. |
| `promotedSizeTable` | 0x0404db8c | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 24 words of promoted kernel sizes per filter width and type; `__glkonGetPromotedSize` reads it for the convolution-filter handlers. |
| `FFT_Factors` | 0x0404dbec | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 256 complex twiddle factors as float pairs, the first being one and zero and the rest the cosine and negated sine of steps of 2 pi over 256, that `InitFFTGlobal` loads into engine memory for the FFT pixel path. |
| `FFT_Sort_2` | 0x0404e3ec | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Bit-reversed sample order for a 2-point transform as byte offsets, zero then 8, loaded by `InitFFTGlobal`. |
| `FFT_Sort_4` | 0x0404e3f4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Sample order for a 4-point transform as byte offsets, here the natural order 0x0-0x18 in steps of 8, loaded by `InitFFTGlobal`. |
| `FFT_Sort_8` | 0x0404e404 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Bit-reversed sample order for an 8-point transform as byte offsets (the first two are 0 then 0x20), loaded by `InitFFTGlobal`. |
| `FFT_Sort_16` | 0x0404e424 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Sample order for a 16-point transform as byte offsets in a four-by-four interleave (0 to 0x60 in steps of 0x20, then from 8), loaded by `InitFFTGlobal`. |
| `FFT_Sort_32` | 0x0404e464 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Bit-reversed sample order for a 32-point transform as byte offsets, starting 0 then 0x80, loaded by `InitFFTGlobal`. |
| `FFT_Sort_64` | 0x0404e4e4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Sample order for a 64-point transform as byte offsets, 64 words, loaded into engine memory by `InitFFTGlobal`. |
| `FFT_Sort_128` | 0x0404e5e4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Bit-reversed sample order for a 128-point transform as byte offsets, 128 words, loaded by `InitFFTGlobal`. |
| `FFT_Sort_256` | 0x0404e7e4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Sample order for a 256-point transform as byte offsets, 256 words, loaded by `InitFFTGlobal`. |
| `divModLut` | 0x04091874 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). A 0x1020-byte quotient and remainder cache filled by `InitDivModLut` and read by `DivModFunc` to avoid the slow software divide. |
| `GeTypeFormatTable` | 0x04092894 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Zero in the image and filled by `InitGeTypeFormatTable`: per host type and format, what the engines receive; read by the colour, luminance, index, depth and FFT stream planners. |

## Context switch, saved state and memory

Context creation, activation and deactivation, saved engine and distributor state, memory reservation, swap and list DMA.

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `restore` | 0x0c000124 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x124). The label sits on a software-interrupt instruction; the interrupt vector points at the code after it (+0x128-0x17c), which copies the saved stack words back into RAM from the saved stack pointer up to 0x1000, reloads every register and the saved status from the register save area, and returns into the saved program counter. The context-activate handler (`arm_context_activate`) issues it last to resume the interrupted code. |
| `arm_buffer_config` | 0x0c0131a0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x131a0). Token handler 0x1 (buffer configuration). It reads ten payload words and stores them as bytes at state-block offsets 0x1cc-0x1d5, among them (**Inferred** from how the index, depth and stencil handlers use them) the colour-index width at 0x1ce, the depth width at 0x1d2 and the stencil width at 0x1d3. It rounds the depth width up to a multiple of 8, recomputes both transfer masks, derives the frame-buffer layout bytes at 0x1d6-0x1db, and writes that layout to the raster boards through descriptors 0x58404800 and 0x584fc800. It also runs the multisample set-up, a late state initialiser, the bank resolver and the frame-buffer state routine, and sends engine handler words that select colour-index or colour mode. |
| `arm_readbuffer_config` | 0x0c013650 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x13650). Token handler 0x4 (read-buffer configuration). It reads ten payload words into bytes at state-block offsets 0x1e0-0x1e9, rounds the depth width up to a multiple of 8, computes the read-side layout bytes at 0x1ea-0x205 from the widths, then calls `resolve_banks` and the frame-buffer read-state routine. It stores 1 at offset 0xc48 when the first word equals -3, else 0. It writes no raster register itself. |
| `resolve_banks` | 0x0c01392c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1392c). Sends the frame-buffer bank selection to the raster boards. It first writes 0 to register 0x1408, takes the bank entry chosen by the exclusive-or of the bank bytes at state-block offsets 0x1f0-0x1f4, permutes the buffer bits when the byte at 0x1dc is set and the draw buffer is one of the first nine, then through descriptor 0x58404800 writes channel bits to address word 0x01341c00 (only when the bytes at 0x1cc plus 0x1cf are both non-zero), buffer bits and a bank to 0x01351c02, and the read bank to 0x01341c00. It ends with a synchronising write to 0x01681408. Called after every draw-buffer, read-buffer and configuration change. |
| `arm_drawbuffer` | 0x0c013a60 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x13a60). Token handler 0xc4 for the draw buffer. It checks the buffer enum against what the configured buffers can supply, reporting invalid-operation or invalid-enum otherwise. It stores the enum at state-block offset 0x7e4, converts it to a bit set in the byte at 0x1f5, writes a layout byte under mask 0x3f to raster address word 0x013b1c0b through descriptor 0x584fc800, then jumps to the bank resolver. |
| `arm_readbuffer` | 0x0c013bfc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x13bfc). Token handler 0x37 for the read buffer. It checks the enum against 0x400-0x40c and that the needed buffer exists (stereo or aux), reports invalid-operation or invalid-enum otherwise, stores the enum at state-block offset 0x7e8, writes the matching bank index to the byte at 0x1f4, and jumps to the bank resolver. It writes no register itself. |
| `arm_validate_bank` | 0x0c013d30 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x13d30). Token handler 0x1f4. It first tests bit 2 of the host-interface word at offset -0x1a0; if clear, it prints "Command privilege violation" and calls `arm_badcmd`. It then stores one payload word in the byte at state-block offset 0x1dc and the other in the byte at 0x1f0, and jumps to the bank resolver to apply them; those stores still run if the bad-command call returns. |
| `arm_finish` | 0x0c014040 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x14040). Token handler 0xc6 (finish). It reads a flag word and always sends the raster-flush engine handler address to port 0x4f4. If the flag is zero it returns; otherwise it starts a read-back and ends by tail-calling the read-back finisher, so the host is told when the raster boards have drained. |
| `arm_flush_raster` | 0x0c014070 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x14070). Token handler 0x1cc (flush raster). It reads one payload word, sends the raster-flush engine handler address to port 0x4f4, and returns. Unlike the finish handler it starts no read-back, so the host gets no completion. |
| `late_state_init` | 0x0c014174 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x14174). Called by the buffer-configuration handler once the layout is known. It runs the raster-position initialiser, picks a mask from the depth width (0x10 or 0x20) and stencil width, writes it to raster address word 0x013a1c09 through descriptor 0x584fc800, copies one word of a table at 0x04041b7c into engine memory, sets the words at state-block offsets 0x714-0x718 to the all-ones mask for the stencil width, and tail-calls the per-engine context initialiser. |
| `arm_copylist_ldma` | 0x0c014dd8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x14dd8). Token handler 0x16c for a display-list copy through the list-transfer engine. It reads a list address and a byte count (the count is rounded up by 4 when bit 2 is set), writes the address plus the base at state-block offset 0xce8 (with bit 26 set), the count and mode 1 to the transfer ports at -0x1d0 to -0x1b8, calls `waitForDH`, sends one pad word to port 0x840 when the count was rounded, starts the transfer and polls port -0x1c0 bit 2 until it ends, then clears the mode. |
| `arm_idmacopylist_ldma` | 0x0c014e54 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x14e54). Token handler 0x1c8 for a list copy that uses the indirect-transfer pool. It reads an address and a byte count (rounded up by 4 when bit 2 is set), programs the same list-transfer ports as the plain list copy, then calls through the dispatch port at 0x200 with a return address set (**Inferred:** the next token's handler runs before the transfer starts), sends a pad word if the count was rounded, starts the transfer and polls port -0x1c0 bit 2 until it ends. |
| `arm_usercopylist_ldma` | 0x0c014ec4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x14ec4). Token handler 0x167 for a list copy from a user address. It reads a list address, a user address and a count, programs the user-page ports at offsets -0x1f0 to -0x1e4 (page, in-page offset, count and marker 0xbeefface) and the list ports (count rounded up to 8, marker 0xdeadbeef), waits for status bit 4 at -0x1a0, sends the word 0xf001f001 to port 0x840 if bit 2 of the count is set, starts the transfer, polls port -0x1c0 until it ends and returns to its caller. |
| `arm_execlist_ldma` | 0x0c014f90 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x14f90). Token handler 0x16d for executing a display list from memory. It reads an address and a word count, adds the base at state-block offset 0xce8 and clears the top six bits to get the source address, programs that and the byte count (words times four) into the list-transfer ports at -0x1d0 and -0x1cc, sets mode 2, and returns through the token dispatcher so the list runs. |
| `arm_execlist_ldma_n` | 0x0c014fc0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x14fc0). Token handler 0x170 for executing several display-list fragments from memory in one token. It reads a count and that many pairs of (address, word count) into a stack array, then writes each pair, with the base at state-block offset 0xce8 added to the address, to the list-transfer ports at -0x1d0 and -0x1cc in turn, finally setting mode 2 at -0x19c and clearing -0x1c8, and returns to its caller. |
| `arm_movelist_ldma` | 0x0c01503c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1503c). Token handler 0x16f for moving data within the host memory pool. It reads two addresses and a byte count, adds the base at state-block offset 0xce8 to both addresses, and tail-calls the block-copy routine, which copies the bytes. It programs no transfer ports. |
| `arm_execlist_idma` | 0x0c015058 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15058). Token handler 0x16e for executing a display list from the indirect-transfer pool. It reads an offset and a word count, checks the end against the pool size at state-block offset 0xce4 (printing "IDMA out-of-bounds" on overflow), programs the indirect ports at -0x1f0 to -0x1e4 (page, in-page offset, byte count) with mode 1, and returns through the token dispatcher so the list runs. |
| `arm_execlist_idma_n` | 0x0c015164 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15164). Token handler 0x171 for executing several indirect-pool fragments in one token. It reads a count and that many pairs, then for each pair writes the page, in-page offset and byte count to the indirect ports at -0x1f0 to -0x1e8, finally setting mode 1 at -0x19c and clearing -0x1e4. It does no bounds check, unlike the single-fragment handler. |
| `line_pop` | 0x0c016908 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x16908). Called by the attribute-pop handler after the line state is restored. Rebuilds the stipple run list from the restored pattern (0x248-0x249) exactly as the line-stipple handler does, sends the repeat factor and list to all engines, then calls `ResolveLine`. A fully set pattern sends no list. |
| `polygon_pop` | 0x0c016a24 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x16a24). Called by the attribute-pop handler after the polygon state is restored. Resends the front-face and cull-face values with their engine handlers, the cull enable or disable by byte 0x75c, the line-mode flag at descriptor 0x49a00020, and the fill polygon-offset flag, then jumps to `ResolvePolygon`. |
| `material_push` | 0x0c017990 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x17990). Called by the attribute-push handler: sends twelve get-material commands through descriptor 0xdc2a6800, one per face (front 0x404, back 0x405) and parameter (the ambient, diffuse, specular, emission, shininess and colour-index names), so the engines return the current material state. Sets the transfer mask from offset 0xc94 of the state block first and restores it from offset 0xc90 at the end. |
| `light_pop` | 0x0c017d48 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x17d48). Restores the engines' lighting state after an attribute pop (called from `arm_popattrib`): reapplies the colour-material face and mode, enables or disables colour material and lighting from their flag bytes, then for each of eight lights resends its enable state, ambient, diffuse, specular, position, spot direction, exponent, cutoff and three attenuation values from the state block, plus global ambient, two-side, local-viewer and shade model, and ends with `XferMask`. |
| `arm_poplights` | 0x0c018134 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x18134). Token 0x119 handler (pop of the lighting attribute group): selects all engines (port 0x438), sets the pass-through counter at 0x200058 to the low 26 bits of the final payload word with the continue bit 26, so the host's saved lighting words go straight to the engines, waits with `waitForDH` until the counter drains, then sends the engine pop-lights handler to port 0x8c0. |
| `viewport_pop` | 0x0c019b24 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x19b24). Called from `arm_popattrib`: resends the four depth-range words kept at state-block offsets 0x72c-0x738 through descriptor 0x78601000 and port 0x840 (last word through port 0x8c0) followed by the depth-range engine handler on port 0x4f8, then jumps to `resolve_viewport` to resend the viewport. |
| `activate_private` | 0x0c01fb50 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1fb50). Part of switching a context in: if the context record is flagged valid it sets bit 0x80 in a state-block word, runs the restore routines for vertex arrays, transforms, fog, the lighting power table, textures and min/max, sends the stored handler to the engines when a flag byte is set, and re-downloads the antialiased-line table or the polygon stipple when their flags are set. If the record is not valid it prints "activate_private, X ha_icu_state is bogus" for stray low bits in the saved host-interface state. |
| `context_init_ged` | 0x0c01fcb8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1fcb8). Sets the distributor's per-context defaults for a new context: runs the register initialiser, then loads descriptor 0x52000020 with 7, the four descriptors from 0x49000020 to 0x49a00020 with 0 and descriptor 0x52800020 with 0x55 (also stored in the context record at offset 0x1114). Each load writes the descriptor to port 0x400 and the value to port 0x8c0. |
| `context_init` | 0x0c01fd50 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1fd50). Builds a fresh context: clears 0x1a6 words of the state block from offset 0x1cc and the 0x44a-word context table, makes that table the current context, sets its flag word to 1 and its next word to 0, plants the 0xdeaddead guard word in the state block, runs the distributor defaults, sends the context-initialise handler (slot 365) to the engines, then runs the default-setting routines for transforms, colour, depth, hints, primitives, lighting, attributes, pixel state, textures and fog. |
| `arm_context_init` | 0x0c01fe04 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1fe04). Handler for host token 0x1fd (context initialise). It brackets its work with the simulator markers, prints "Command privilege violation." and treats the token as a bad command if the privilege status bit is clear, runs the one-time context build (`context_init`) unless already built and simulating, clears the cache counters, takes three words from the payload and records them in the state block (at offsets 0xcd0-0xcd8 when it is the first call) and sends them to the engines as a context-load command with handler slot 131. |
| `activate_setcurrentge` | 0x0c01ff20 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x1ff20). Selects the geometry engine named in a saved context (the record in the first argument holds its number in bits 3-5 of its word at offset 0xc): prints an error if that engine is not in the occupied-engine mask, then loads descriptor 0x44400020 with a one-hot mask of that engine five times and, after a null-command handler write, with the full occupied-engine mask five times. |
| `activate_ged` | 0x0c020018 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x20018). Restores the distributor's state from a saved array (first argument), stepping by the stride in the second argument: for about eighty descriptors in turn it writes the descriptor to port 0x400 and the saved word to port 0x8c0, with one descriptor family repeated 128 times and another 16 times. The first two saved words go to the descriptors from 0x44000020 to 0x44200020, then one slot is skipped before 0x44a00020; the skipped descriptor is the engine mask at 0x44400020, which the engine-select routine loads instead. |
| `activate_geregs` | 0x0c02081c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2081c). Restores the geometry engines' registers from a saved context: streams the saved register words (count taken from the record) to the data port 0x840 with the broadcast select (port 0x438) first, then triggers handler slot 132 through port 0x8c0 and finishes by writing the values 1 to 9 to port 0x8c0, each after reselecting the broadcast. |
| `activate_gefifo` | 0x0c020888 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x20888). Restores engine FIFO contents from a saved context: picks broadcast (port 0x438) or single-engine (port 0x430) select from the record's flags, streams the saved words to the data port 0x840 with handler slot 189 through port 0x8c0, and when the record's remainder count is nonzero sends the rest through the second select pair (ports 0x420 and 0x428), the last word going to port 0x8c0. |
| `activate_hip` | 0x0c020978 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x20978). Puts the host-interface state back after a context switch: sets bit 0 of the interface control register at 0x200050, stores one saved word into the instruction RAM at 0x400000, restores the saved control, state, loop-address, array-count and word-count registers from the saved block, and writes the first argument to the stream-count register at 0x200058. |
| `arm_context_activate` | 0x0c0209dc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x209dc). Token 0x1fa: resumes a saved context. After the privilege check it loads three words from the context record into engine ports (one to port 0x478, three to the data port 0x840) and sends the engine handler in slot 133, which loads the flag registers. It restores pixel state when the record's mode word is 1 or 2 and X-pixel state when it is 3, then reinstalls the attribute state, the current engine selection, the engine registers, the engine FIFO and the distributor tables, sends the host an interrupt word read from the payload when it exceeds 0x100, and restores the host-interface registers last. If the saved host-interface record marks the ARM as interrupted, it returns into the saved ARM registers. |
| `load_broadcast_geEram` | 0x0c020c04 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x20c04). Streams a block of host-FIFO words (the count is the second argument) into every engine's external memory: it selects all engines through port 0x438, and for each chunk of at most 0x200 words sends the running offset and the next FIFO word, writes the chunk length minus one with both continue bits (0xc000000) to the pass-through counter at 0x200058, waits for the counter to drain, then sends the engine handler in slot 131 that stores the chunk. The loop ends when the count is used up. |
| `load_broadcast_ge` | 0x0c020c68 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x20c68). Sends one block of host-FIFO words to every engine: selects all engines through port 0x438, forwards the next FIFO word to the data port 0x840, writes the count minus one with both continue bits (0xc000000) to the pass-through counter at 0x200058 when the count exceeds one, waits for the counter to reach zero, then sends the engine handler whose address is the third argument. The count is the second argument. |
| `load_singlecast_ge` | 0x0c020c9c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x20c9c). The broadcast loader aimed at one engine: selects the single-engine port 0x430, forwards the next host-FIFO word, sets the pass-through counter at 0x200058 to the count (second argument) minus one with both continue bits when the count exceeds one, waits for it to drain, and sends the engine handler whose address is the third argument. Context load uses it for the engine blocks whose handlers sit in slots 187 and 190. |
| `load_ged` | 0x0c020cd0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x20cd0). A single branch to `activate_ged`, the routine that restores the distributor state from the context record; it is a second name for that routine and does nothing else. |
| `store_data` | 0x0c020cd4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x20cd4). Copies n host-FIFO words (second argument) into ARM memory at the address given as the first argument. For a destination above 0x1000 with more than six words it uses list DMA: any unaligned leading word and trailing word are read by hand from the payload ports, and the aligned middle is moved by programming the list-DMA address, count and last-word registers (0x200030-0x200038) and the pass-through counter, then waiting for the count and the list-DMA done bit. Small copies read the payload port one word at a time. It records in the saved-state block (offset 0xa4) whether a payload word has already been peeked. **Inferred:** that flag tracks a pending peeked word, from the way it is set after the odd-word read and cleared after the loop. |
| `load_arm` | 0x0c020dd4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x20dd4). Loads one saved ARM section from the host FIFO: it checks that the four leading words each match the section tag, stores the section by tag, then checks the four trailing tag words and prints a mismatch message if they differ. Tag 0x200 stores 10 host-interface words and rewrites the token-map entry named in them so the next command runs `arm_context_activate`; tag 0x400 stores 0x2ca words into the state block from offset 0x1cc; tag 0x401 stores 0x44a words into the context record; tag 0x403 stores 0x11 saved registers; tag 0x402 stores the saved stack, with a length check that prints an alert on mismatch. Each store goes through `store_data`. |
| `arm_context_load` | 0x0c02112c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2112c). Token 0x1f9: loads a whole context from the host FIFO. After the privilege check it reads the header (0x52 words) into the load map, compares its first word with the first payload word and on mismatch prints the values and hangs in a loop. It then walks the three-word entries of the map and handles each by tag: per-engine blocks go through the single-engine loader, engine-memory and table blocks through the broadcast loaders, the distributor and engine-FIFO areas through `store_data`, and the 0x200-0x403 ARM sections through `load_arm`. It ends by checking the end marker 0xedededed and writing 0x10000000 to the pass-through counter. |
| `arm_context_destroy` | 0x0c021650 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x21650). Token 0x1f7: invalidates a context's saved images. After the privilege check it fills every save area with the last payload word as a marker: 10 host-interface words, 0x2ca state-block words, 0x44a context-record words, 0x11 saved registers, 0x400 saved-stack words, 0x800 engine-FIFO words and 0x100 distributor words. It then writes the marker to engine port 0x478 and sends the engine handler in slot 129, the engine-side context destroy. |
| `arm_context_fifo_sync` | 0x0c021770 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x21770). Token 0x1fb: only the privilege check. A privileged command returns at once with no hardware access; an unprivileged one prints the privilege-violation message and calls the bad-command path. |
| `deactivate_ged_ge` | 0x0c0217c4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x217c4). Part of the context save: four times it writes a distributor descriptor to port 0x400 (0x40500020 first, then 0x4a100020, 0x58b00020 and finally 0x44b00020), a zero word to port 0x880 and a small operand (6 down to 3) to port 0x470, then sends the engine handler in slot 181, which stores a value into the saved context. **Inferred:** the descriptors request a read-out of distributor state, because the three register descriptors of the same kind in the context-save routine for the distributor equal the load descriptors with bit 20 added. |
| `deactivate_ge` | 0x0c02184c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2184c). Part of the context save: writes 1 to the engine mailbox-valid register at 0x20009c and stores the engine handler in slot 186 (the engine-side context deactivate) to port 0x4f0 as the handler address. |
| `deactivate_ged` | 0x0c021864 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x21864). Part of the context save: writes a long run of distributor descriptors to port 0x400, each followed by a zero word to port 0x880, with three loops of repeated table descriptors (32 repeats, then 128, then 16). The first three are 0x44100020-0x44500020, the distribution-mode, step and occupancy-mask register descriptors with bit 20 added. **Inferred:** bit 20 requests a read-out, since the same registers are loaded elsewhere without it, so this pulls the distributor state into the stream that the engines save. |
| `deactivate_hip` | 0x0c021df8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x21df8). Saves the host-interface state into the record whose pointer is in the saved-state block: the pending word count, the pass-through counter, the begin/end state, the low nine bits of the current and previous command numbers, the control word, the loop address and the array count, all read from the registers at 0x200050-0x20007c. It also reads the token-map entries of those commands with array access enabled and, when an array was in progress, rewinds the entry and sets bit 1 of the first record word. It then resets the registers (counter to 0x10000000, the rest to zero) and flags the record when words are still owed. |
| `deact_section` | 0x0c021f7c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x21f7c). Streams one section of ARM memory to the engines for a context save: the first argument is the section tag, the second the memory address, the third the word count. For each chunk of at most 0x1f8 words it selects one engine (0x430), sends the tag and a flag word (3 on the last chunk, 0 before), and four marker words (the tag in both halves) on the first and last chunk, then the data, by list DMA when the address is above 0x1000 and by word reads otherwise, and finally sends the engine handler in slot 184 that stores the chunk. |
| `deactivate_arm` | 0x0c02209c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2209c). Sends the ARM's own state to the engines as tagged sections: tag 0x200 (10 host-interface words), 0x400 (0x2ca state-block words), 0x401 (0x44a context-record words), then the record's engine word to port 0x470 with 2 on port 0x840 and the engine handler in slot 181. When the saved-ARM flag is set it advances the saved program counter by 4 (0x4), then sends tag 0x403 (0x11 registers) and the saved-stack part under tag 0x402, using the section streaming routine for each. |
| `arm_context_deactivate` | 0x0c022188 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22188). Token 0x1fc: saves the running context. After the privilege check it stamps 0xdeaddead into the state-block word at 0xcfc if not already set, saves the host-interface state, runs the engine, distributor and engine-side distributor saves, stores the handler of slot 183 in port 0x4f0 and of slot 185 in port 0x4f8, writes the words 1-9 to all engines, saves the ARM state, sends the last payload word with the handler of slot 182 (deactivate finish), and writes zero to raster register 0x3ff1. |
| `arm_context_mopup` | 0x0c02226c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2226c). Token 0x1f8: abandons the running context without saving it. After the privilege check it stamps 0xdeaddead into the state-block word at 0xcfc if not set and resets the host-interface registers (pending count, begin/end state and array count cleared, pass-through counter to 0x10000000, control word reloaded from the byte at state-block offset 0xcf8). It stores the handler of slot 176 in port 0x4fc and of slot 185 in port 0x4f8, writes the words 1-9 to all engines, reports the last payload word to the kernel through the kernel-communication writer, and writes zero to raster register 0x3ff1. |
| `waitForDH` | 0x0c022354 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22354). Spins on the interrupt-controller status register (0x200060) until bit 4 is set and bit 5 is also set; while bit 5 is clear it reads and discards one payload word from the host-interface block and records in the saved-state block (offset 0xa0) that it did so. Engine-memory swap-in uses it to wait for the data handler to catch up. |
| `mem_reserve_arm` | 0x0c023b3c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23b3c). Reserves pages of the first ARM memory area for the current context. When the key (first argument) is 0 it stores the area base plus the page number (second argument) times 0x800 at offset 0x1110 of the context record and the page count (third argument) times 0x800 as the size at offset 0x10c8; any other key prints the invalid-key message. |
| `mem_swapout_arm` | 0x0c023bc8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23bc8). Swaps pages of the first ARM area out to the host: sets the list-DMA address (page times 0x800 above the area base), byte count and last-word marker 0xdeadbeef, sets the source mode at 0x200064, then for each page selects the next engine (0x434), writes 0x200 words with both continue bits to the pass-through counter, waits for bit 4 of the interrupt-controller status and sends the engine handler in slot 239 that forwards the words to the output DMA. |
| `mem_swapin_arm` | 0x0c023c54 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23c54). Swaps pages back into the first ARM area from the host by list DMA: sets the list-DMA address with the write-direction bit (0x4000000), the byte count and 0xdeadbeef, raises the list-DMA source bit (0x200048), sets the pass-through counter to the word count with the input source bit, waits for bit 4 of the status, clears the flush register, waits for list-DMA done (bit 2 of 0x200040), and drops the source bit. |
| `mem_copydown_arm` | 0x0c023cd8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23cd8). Copies pages inside the first ARM area, 0x200 words per page: the first two arguments are the source and destination page numbers and the third is the page count. It touches no hardware register. |
| `mem_reserve_sarm` | 0x0c023d28 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23d28). Reserves pages of the second ARM area: when the key (first argument) is 0 it stores the second area's base plus the page number times 0x800 at state-block offset 0xce8 and the page count times 0x800 at 0xcec; any other key prints the invalid-key message. |
| `mem_swapout_sarm` | 0x0c023da0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23da0). The swap-out of the second ARM area to the host: the same list-DMA and per-page engine handler sequence as the first-area version (slot 239 forwards each 0x200-word page to the output DMA), but with the base of the second area. |
| `mem_swapin_sarm` | 0x0c023e24 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23e24). The swap-in of the second ARM area from the host by list DMA, identical in sequence to the first-area version (write-direction bit, 0xdeadbeef marker, pass-through count, waits for status bit 4 and list-DMA done) but with the base of the second area. |
| `mem_copydown_sarm` | 0x0c023ea8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23ea8). Copies pages inside the second ARM area, 0x200 words per page, from the source page (first argument) to the destination page (second argument) for the page count in the third argument; no hardware access. |
| `mem_reserve_ge` | 0x0c023ef8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x23ef8). Reserves engine-memory pages: the key (first argument) 0-10 selects one of eleven engine-memory words (0x2a0-0x2aa), and the routine writes the page address, the engine-memory base plus the page times 0x400 divided by 4, to that word through the engine-memory writer. Key 9 also stores the address at offset 0x1124 of the context record; a larger key prints the invalid-key message. |
| `mem_swapout_ge` | 0x0c02408c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2408c). Reads engine-memory pages back to the host: for each 0x100-word page, repeated the number of times given in the third argument, it writes the page address to port 0x474 and 0x100 to port 0x840 and sends the engine handler in slot 197 that copies engine memory to the output DMA. |
| `mem_swapin_ge` | 0x0c0240f0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x240f0). Loads engine-memory pages from the host: for each 0x100-word page it writes the address to the operand port (0x474, or 0x478 when the repeat count is one), sets the pass-through counter to 0x100 with both continue bits, waits for the data handler, and sends the engine handler in slot 209 (or the handler from the upper half of the state-block word at 0x9c when the repeat count is one). |
| `mem_copydown_ge` | 0x0c024184 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24184). Copies engine-memory pages inside engine memory: for each 0x100-word page it writes the source address to port 0x478, the destination address and 0x100 to port 0x840, and sends the engine handler in slot 142 (engine memory to engine memory), then advances both addresses by 0x100. |
| `mem_reserve_tex` | 0x0c0241e0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x241e0). Reserves texture memory: when the key (first argument) is 0 it writes the page number, scaled to a 0x1000 stride, through descriptor 0x58404800 to the raster registers 0x0146101a and 0x41455001 (each preceded by an all-ones select word); a nonzero key prints the invalid-key message. |
| `mem_swapout_tex` | 0x0c024284 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24284). Reads texture memory back to the host: it writes the scaled page number to raster registers 0x101a and 0x5001, calls the texture read-back routine for the texture id in the second argument, then clears the two raster registers through descriptor 0x58404800. |
| `mem_swapin_tex` | 0x0c0242fc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x242fc). Writes texture memory from the host: it writes the scaled page number to raster registers 0x101a and 0x5001, calls the texture download routine for the texture id in the second argument, then clears the same two raster registers through descriptor 0x58404800. |
| `mem_copydown_tex` | 0x0c024374 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24374). Returns at once; texture memory has no copy-down, so this table entry does nothing. |
| `arm_mem_reserve` | 0x0c024378 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24378). Token 0x1e4: after the privilege check it reads the memory kind and the key, page number and page count from the payload and tail-calls the entry of the reserve table selected by the kind. |
| `arm_mem_swapout_toobig` | 0x0c024430 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24430). Swaps out a block too large for one transfer: it loops, each pass taking a piece up to the limit, reprogramming the pixel read-back registers 0x450-0x454 for that piece, calling the swap-out routine for the memory kind and advancing the address and count until the whole block has gone. |
| `arm_mem_swapout` | 0x0c024504 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24504). Token 0x1e3: reads the memory kind, page and size words and computes the total byte count. After the privilege check it saves two words at offsets 0x50 and 0x54 of the memory-base table, programs the pixel read-back registers 0x450-0x454 and calls the swap-out entry for the kind, or the oversized-block routine when texture memory exceeds 0x10000000 bytes, and finally suspends the transfer. |
| `arm_mem_swapin_toobig` | 0x0c024664 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24664). Swaps in a block too large for one transfer: each pass programs the input-DMA registers (0x200010-0x20001c, mode 0x200020 = 2) for a piece up to the limit, calls the swap-in routine for the memory kind, and advances the address and count until the whole block has been loaded. |
| `arm_mem_swapin` | 0x0c024710 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24710). Token 0x1e2: reads the kind, page and size words and the total byte count. After the privilege check, unless the handle word is all ones, it programs the input-DMA registers directly for a block that fits and hands oversized blocks to the piecewise routine, calls the swap-in entry for the kind, then sends the host an interrupt word taken from the last payload word. |
| `arm_mem_copydown` | 0x0c02481c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2481c). Token 0x1e1: after the privilege check it reads the memory kind, two page numbers and the page count and tail-calls the entry of the copy-down table selected by the kind. |
| `arm_mem_suspend` | 0x0c0248a0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x248a0). Token 0x1df: after the privilege check it sends the last payload word to the host as a kernel-communication record and writes 5 to register 0x476 and 0 to the registers 0x470-0x474. **Inferred:** this ends the read-back stream that a swap-out started, because swap-out calls it last. |
| `arm_mem_resume` | 0x0c024954 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24954). Token 0x1de: after the privilege check it reads five payload words and writes them to the pixel read-back registers 0x450-0x454 (the last-payload word goes to 0x451). **Inferred:** this restores the read-back set-up that a suspend ended, from the names. |
| `mem_init` | 0x0c0249ec | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x249ec). Start-up routine that sets up memory reservation. It sets the first ARM area base to the address 0x04095bd7 rounded down to 0x800, the second area 0x20000 above it, the engine-memory base to 0x20000, and fills four allocator records in the board configuration block starting at offset 0x90 with their free-page counts: 0x40 pages of 0x800 bytes for the first area, the rest of the ARM space up to 0x5000000 (capped at 0xe00000 bytes) for the second, engine memory in 0x400-word pages, and texture memory in 0x40000-byte pages sized from the smallest per-engine texture size. |
| `init_per_ge_context` | 0x0c024bac | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x24bac). Initialises per-context engine memory at the address given as the first argument: writes 0x4f800000 to that word and 0 to the next two through the engine-memory writer, then uses the engine fill handler (slot 140) to set four words at offset 3 to 1.0 (0x3f800000) and four words at offset 7 to 0, and finally stores the handler of slot 143 to port 0x4f8. |
| `PixelRestore` | 0x0c0290dc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x290dc). Called when a context is activated to resume a pixel transfer. Returns at once when bit 0x100 of the word at 0x908 is set. **Inferred:** it re-derives how much of an interrupted transfer was already sent, from the use of the progress counters. It converts a saved float with the float-to-integer routine, counts the engines from the engine mask, and advances the progress counters at context offsets 0x708-0x710. It then rewrites raster register 0x010f087f and re-programs the position through `SetupPGTG` for the draw form or the sub-load positioner for texture loads, or loads the zero-position engine handlers when the transform mode word is 0x1c00-0x1c01. |
| `tex_swapin` | 0x0c02b6b0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2b6b0). Reloads one texture image for the context-memory swap-in path. It takes a size and a level, updates the texture bind id, fills the pixel state with a fixed single-image download (rectangle from the log of the size), points the unpack record at the fixed record at 0x040497e8, clears the transfer flags, runs the common texel-download body, then restores the saved record and flags. Called by `mem_swapin_tex`. |
| `tex_swapout` | 0x0c02c1ac | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2c1ac). Reads one texture image back to the host for the context-memory swap-out path. Takes a size and a level, updates the texture load id, fills the pixel state with a fixed single-image read (rectangle from the log of the size), points the pack record at the fixed record at 0x040497a0, clears the transfer flags, runs the common texture read body, then restores both. Called by `mem_swapout_tex`. |
| `stored_gedstate_mem` | 0x0408d234 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Storage for the saved distributor state; no code names it directly, `init_arm` rounds its address plus 4 down to eight bytes and stores that in the pointer `stored_gedstate`. |
| `stored_gefifo_mem` | 0x0408d640 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 8 KB of storage for saved engine input words; `init_arm` stores its eight-byte-aligned address in the pointer `stored_gefifo`, through which the context code reaches it. |
| `stored_geregs_mem` | 0x0408f64c | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). Storage for saved engine registers; `init_arm` stores its eight-byte-aligned address in the pointer `stored_geregs`. |
| `saved_stack_mem` | 0x0408f718 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). 4 KB of storage for a saved ARM stack; `init_arm` stores its eight-byte-aligned address in the pointer that the context code and the resume routine use. |
| `loadCtxMap` | 0x04090724 | **Established** (`arm1.u` 6.5.30m). An 82-word zero-initialised record shared by the context load handler and `arm_context_activate`; when a context's pixel record says kind 3 (X pixels), the activation passes three of its words (offsets 0x20-0x24 with 0xc) to `XPixelRestore`, so it holds the X pixel set-up a context resumes with. |

## Diagnostic commands

The `DIAG_DISPATCH` sub-commands (token 0x169) and other routines that exist for the IR diagnostics.

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `arm_ged_verif_load` | 0x0c012ae4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12ae4). Token handler 0x1ef. Reads three payload words, loads the diagnostic engine program with `load_ged` (address 0x00200200), then reads the checksum word. It stores 0 to the host-interface port at offset -0x19c and the three words to the ports at -0x1a4, -0x1a8 and -0x198 (first, second, third word in that order), unaltered, and finishes by jumping to the routine that sets every transfer-mask attribute bit. |
| `arm_ged_verif_dump` | 0x0c012b2c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x12b2c). Token handler 0x1f0. Prints "arm_context_deactivate", sets three host-interface ports at offsets -0x1a8 to -0x198 (0x10000000 in the first, zero in the other two), then sends the engines a descriptor at port 0x400, a marker word at port 0x980, a zero at port 0x430, one payload word at port 0x840 and the diagnostic-deactivate handler address at port 0x8c0, and calls `deactivate_ged`. It finally reads the checksum word. |
| `arm_end_of_simulation` | 0x0c0152cc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x152cc). Token handler 0xe5, sent at the end of a simulation run. It reads one payload word, reports instruction-cache statistics, prints "end of ARM simulation", scans upward from RAM offset 0xd04 while words equal 0xdeadbeef and prints the address of the last such word as the first unused stack entry (or an alert when the stack reached the fill area), then writes 1 twice to raster register 0x1c0f. |
| `arm_arm_nop` | 0x0c022d70 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22d70). Token 0x1e8: a payload-path test. It reads a count, then checks that each following payload word equals an incrementing counter starting at 0, printing the received and expected values on any mismatch, and checks the final word at the last-payload port the same way. |
| `arm_diag_dispatch` | 0x0c037958 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37958). Token 0x169 handler: reads a sub-command number from the host-interface block and jumps through a twelve-entry table to the diagnostic routine for it. By the table layout, number 0 is the processor-memory store, 1 the memory copy, 2 and 3 the distributor table loaders, then a register write, pixel and pass-through feeds, raster-bus writes, next-engine select, and number 11 context deactivation; a number above 11 prints an unrecognised-command message and returns. |
| `diag_set_immediat` | 0x0c0379f0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x379f0). Diagnostic sub-command 0: stores the second payload word at the processor address given by the first. For the token-map entry addresses 0x4007f0-0x4007fc it raises the token-map hold bit at 0x200050 around the store, so the hardware does not read an entry mid-update; other addresses get a plain store. |
| `diag_set` | 0x0c037a34 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37a34). Diagnostic sub-command 1: copies one word from the processor address given by the first payload word to the address given by the second, a memory-to-memory move for the host's debug tools. |
| `diag_ged_register` | 0x0c037a48 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37a48). Diagnostic sub-command 4: writes one distributor register. Builds the register descriptor from the first payload word (shifted left 13, bit 0 set, shifted into bits 11 and up, bit 5 set) and sends it to the descriptor port. If the second word is zero the third word follows through the final port; if it is nonzero the descriptor also gets bit 12 and a zero word goes to port 0x880, so the value is dropped. |
| `diag_ged_pixelsetup` | 0x0c037aac | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37aac). Diagnostic sub-command 5: loads seven pixel set-up registers from seven payload words, one descriptor and one value each, with descriptors running from 0x58000020 to 0x58c00020 in steps of 0x200000. Writes the hardware through the descriptor port only; the firmware's own state block is untouched. |
| `diag_ged_pixels` | 0x0c037b40 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37b40). Diagnostic sub-command 6: sends descriptor 0x06000020 to the descriptor port, peeks the word count at offset 0x40 of the host-interface block, prints the total word count and starts the pass-through counter at 0x200058 with the bare count, so the following words go straight to the distributor. |
| `diag_ged_pixels_b2b` | 0x0c037b90 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37b90). Diagnostic sub-command 7: sends descriptor 0x06000020, starts the pass-through counter at 0x200058 with the count (top six bits cleared) and flag 0x8000000, spins until bit 4 of the status register at 0x200060 is set, sends the descriptor again and then writes the bare count. The back-to-back variant of the previous sub-command. |
| `diag_load_ged_table` | 0x0c037bcc | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37bcc). Diagnostic sub-command 2: loads words into a distributor table. Reads two register numbers, a first value and a count; sends the descriptor built from the first number with the first value, then sends the descriptor built from the second number before each of the next count payload words, the last of which comes from the final payload slot. |
| `diag_transfer_ged_table` | 0x0c037c6c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37c6c). Diagnostic sub-command 3: reads two register numbers, a value and a repeat count; sends the first register's descriptor with the value, then repeats the second register's descriptor (with bit 12 set) followed by a zero filler word at port 0x880 that many times. |
| `diag_tbus_1` | 0x0c037ce8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37ce8). Diagnostic sub-command 8: selects one engine through port 0x430, sends three payload words to the operand port 0x840, then sends the engine handler whose address is the upper halfword of the state-block word at offset 0x48. In effect a single raster-bus register write to that engine. |
| `diag_tbus_1_noid` | 0x0c037d14 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37d14). Diagnostic sub-command 9: selects one engine through port 0x430, sends three payload words to port 0x840, then sends the engine handler given by the upper halfword of the state-block word at offset 0x48. Its code is word for word the same as the previous sub-command; only the table slot that reaches it differs. |
| `arm_diag_reserved0` | 0x0c037d40 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37d40). Token 0x16a handler: a bare return. The entry exists in the token map but the firmware does nothing with the payload. |
| `arm_diag_reserved1` | 0x0c037d44 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37d44). Token 0x16b handler: a bare return. The entry exists in the token map but the firmware does nothing with the payload. |
| `diag_ged_nextge` | 0x0c037d48 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37d48). Diagnostic sub-command 10: discards the final payload word, stores to the engine-select port 0x434 and sends the engine handler address held at offset 0x2c0 of the handler table through the final port. **Inferred:** from its name, this advances the distributor to the next geometry engine. |
| `diag_ged_deactivate_context` | 0x0c037d64 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x37d64). Diagnostic sub-command 11: sends descriptor 0x20, writes the marker 0xdeedcafe to port 0x980, selects an engine through port 0x430, sends the final payload word as operand and then the engine handler at offset 0x2fc of the handler table. **Inferred:** from its name, this saves the distributor state as a context switch would. |
| `arm_slop` | 0x0c038a78 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x38a78). Token 0x17f handler: a multi-purpose diagnostic entry. Reads sixteen payload words and dispatches on the first. -0x3ec reads one word back through the word reader; -0x3eb to -0x3e8 open a read-back and return engine state through one engine handler each; 1 checks the gembox sync counter and then, for a nonzero second word, sends 89 raster-register writes through descriptor 0x58404800; 2 writes the marker 0xbabecafe twelve times; 3 writes a raster register; values from 0x0deadded up to 0x0dee0002 forward the payload words to the engines or switch video copy, interlace or video format. |
| `slop_readword` | 0x0c039194 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x39194). Reads engine memory back for the diagnostic handler: takes a position (the first two arguments, packed as two 12-bit fields) and a selector (the third), opens a read-back, writes the count 1 to raster-bus register 0x476, reprograms the pixel set-up registers and raster registers and sends a rectangle word for that position, then finishes the read-back. **Inferred:** it fetches one pixel at that position. |

## Queries and read-back

Handlers that answer GL queries through the read-back path ([arm1_firmware.md](arm1_firmware.md#how-the-arm-answers-the-host)).

| Symbol | Address | What it is and does |
|---|---|---|
| `set_error` | 0x0c015418 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15418). Records the first graphics error: it reads the two-byte code at state-block offsets 0x860-0x861 and, only if it is zero, stores the argument there as a big-endian pair. A code already stored stays until the error query clears it. Called from one place, the texture colour-table validation. |
| `set_invalid_enum` | 0x0c015438 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15438). Records the invalid-enum error code 0x0500 in the two bytes at state-block offsets 0x860-0x861, but only if no error is stored yet; otherwise it returns with the first error untouched. About thirty handlers call it for a bad enum argument. |
| `set_invalid_value` | 0x0c015460 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15460). Records the invalid-value error code 0x0501 in the two bytes at state-block offsets 0x860-0x861, but only if no error is stored yet; otherwise it returns with the first error untouched. Called by the light, viewport, scissor and clear handlers. |
| `set_invalid_operation` | 0x0c015488 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15488). Records the invalid-operation error code 0x0502 in the two bytes at state-block offsets 0x860-0x861, but only if no error is stored yet; otherwise it returns with the first error untouched. Called by the buffer-selection handlers and others. |
| `set_stack_overflow` | 0x0c0154b0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x154b0). Records the stack-overflow error code 0x0503 in the two bytes at state-block offsets 0x860-0x861, but only if no error is stored yet; otherwise it returns with the first error untouched. Called only by the push-attributes handler. |
| `set_stack_underflow` | 0x0c0154d8 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x154d8). Called by the attribute-pop handler when the attribute stack is empty. Records the stack-underflow error value 0x0504 in the sticky error halfword at state block offset 0x860, but only when no error is already pending, then returns. |
| `arm_seterror` | 0x0c015500 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x15500). Token 0x0e4 handler. Reads the first payload word and, if the error halfword at state block offset 0x860 is clear, stores its low sixteen bits there as the pending error; an earlier pending error is kept. Sends nothing to the hardware. |
| `arm_getlightfv` | 0x0c017874 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x17874). Token 0x041 handler (glGetLightfv): starts a read-back, writes the reply word count (3 for spot direction, 1 for the other single-value names, otherwise 4) to raster register 0x476, then sends the light's engine-memory address to port 0x474, the parameter name to port 0x840 and the engine's get-light handler to port 0x8c0, and finishes the read-back so the engines' reply goes to the host. |
| `arm_getmaterialfv` | 0x0c0178e4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x178e4). Token 0x046 handler (glGetMaterialfv): selects descriptor 0x58400000, starts a read-back, writes the reply word count (4 for ambient, diffuse, specular or emission, 1 for shininess, otherwise 3) to raster register 0x476, then sends the face to port 0x474, the parameter name to port 0x840 and the engine's get-material handler to port 0x8c0 under the transfer mask held at offset 0xc94 of the state block, restores the mask from offset 0xc90 and finishes the read-back. |
| `arm_rdata_setup` | 0x0c022448 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22448). Token 0x1f2: stores the read-back destination in the state block: the first payload word at offset 0xc98, the same word with its low 12 bits cleared at 0xc9c, the word from the last-payload port at 0xca0, and zero at 0xca4. The read-back start routine later loads these into the output registers. |
| `arm_getconfig` | 0x0c022474 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22474). Token 0x1e9: answers a configuration query. It starts the read-back with the first payload word, sends the count 0x1de to register 0x476 and then 0x1de words from the board configuration block at the start of zero-initialised data (0x04067edc), one register write per word, and finishes the read-back. |
| `start_rdata` | 0x0c0224d4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x224d4). Programs the raster-board read-back registers before a reply goes to the host: it clears register 0x477, sets 0x440 to 1, sets the length registers from the state-block word at offset 0xca0, sets 0x464 to 0x400, loads the address registers from the words at 0xc98 and 0xc9c, and finally writes 0 to 0x476 to start. Each write goes through the raster-register writer. |
| `finish_rdata` | 0x0c02255c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x2255c). Undoes the read-back set-up: it writes idle values back to the length registers (4, then 0x400), restores the address registers from the state block, writes 1 then 0 to the start and enable registers at 0x476-0x477, and clears the length register 0x463 and the mode register 0x440. |
| `start_pixel_rdata` | 0x0c022614 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22614). Pixel form of the read-back set-up: clears register 0x477, programs the pixel read-back registers 0x450-0x454 from the state-block words at offsets 0xc98-0xca0 (0x454 gets 0x400), writes 0 and then the first argument to 0x476, and clears 0x477 again. All writes use the incrementing raster-register writer. |
| `start_pixel_rdata_noincr` | 0x0c0226b4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x226b4). The same pixel read-back set-up with every raster-register write sent through the non-incrementing writer, so the register address does not advance between the writes. |
| `finish_pixel_rdata` | 0x0c022754 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22754). Ends a pixel read-back: it writes idle values to the pixel read-back registers 0x450-0x454 (the length registers get 4 then 0, 0x454 gets 0x400), restores the address pair from the state block, writes 1 then 0 to the start and enable registers at 0x476-0x477, and clears the first length register at the end. |
| `arm_start_selectfeed` | 0x0c022a50 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22a50). Token 0x1f5: starts select or feedback output. After the privilege check it reads five payload words and a mode word, sets the state-block flag at offset 0xca4 to 1, programs the pixel read-back registers 0x450-0x456 from the words, and saves the five words in the state block at 0xca8-0xcb8 (and again at 0xcbc-0xccc when the mode word is 1) so a restart can replay them. |
| `arm_restart_selectfeed` | 0x0c022b8c | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22b8c). Token 0x1bc: when the select-feedback flag at state-block offset 0xca4 equals 1 it reloads the five words saved by the start token (0xcbc-0xccc) and rewrites the pixel read-back registers 0x450-0x456 from them, after clearing 0x477; otherwise it returns without writing anything. |
| `arm_finish_selectfeed` | 0x0c022c40 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x22c40). Token 0x1f6: ends select or feedback output. After the privilege check it clears the flag at state-block offset 0xca4, sends a kernel-communication record carrying the first payload word, writes the last payload word to register 0x476, clears the registers at 0x473-0x474, ends the transfer, and resets the pixel read-back registers 0x453-0x454 to their idle values. |
| `arm_gettexenvfv` | 0x0c025098 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x25098). Token handler 0x04d, a texture-environment read-back. Reads the parameter name and brackets the work with the read-data start and finish routines. It writes raster-bus register 0x476 with a word count (1 for mode 0x2200, else 4), sends one of three read operands to port 0x474 with that count, and loads the handler that moves engine external memory to the output DMA, so the value goes to the host. |
| `arm_getfloatv` | 0x0c0265b0 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x265b0). Token handler 0x03f: a single branch to the bad-command routine, which prints the token numbers, reads and prints 100 payload words and then spins; the firmware implements no real handler for this token. |
| `arm_getintegerv` | 0x0c0265b4 | **Established** (`arm1.u` +0x265b4). Token handler 0x040: a single branch to the bad-command routine, which prints the token numbers, reads and prints 100 payload words and then spins; the firmware implements no real handler for this token. |
